36

स जयति संकल्पभवो रतिमुखशतपत्रचुम्बनभ्रमरः यस्यानुरक्तललनानयनान्तविलोकितं
वसतिः ॥ १ ॥

अवधीर्य दोषनिचयं गुणलेशे संनिवेश्य मतिमार्याः । कुट्टन्या
मतमेतद्दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं शृणुत ॥ २ ॥

अस्ति खलु निखिलभूतलभूषणभूता विभूतिगुणयुक्ता । युक्ताभियुक्तजनता नगरी
वाराणसी नाम ॥ ३ ॥

अनुभवतामपि यस्यामुपभोगान् कामतः शरीरवताम् । शशधरखण्डविभूषितदेहलयः किल न
दुष्प्रापः ॥ ४॥

चन्द्रविभूषितदेहा भूतिरताः सद्भजङ्गपरिवाराः । वारस्त्रियो ऽपि यस्यां
पशुपतितनुतुल्यतां याताः ॥ ५ ॥

अतितुङ्गसुरनिकेतनशिखरसमुत्क्षिप्तपवनचलिताभिः । मञ्जरितमिव विराजति यत्र नभो
वैजयन्तीभिः ॥ ६ ॥

अविरतसंचरदवला-चररणतलालक्तकद्रवारुणितम् । स्थलकमलवतीं लक्ष्मीं बिभर्ति
वसुधातलं यत्र ॥ ७ ॥

1 This verse is quoted as Damodaragupta's in the Subhasitaratnakoia (329)

The Bawd's Counsel

Victorious over all is Love, The mind-bom god who's like a bee Yearning to
kiss a lotus-bloom -The lotus of his Rati's face-And who inhabits now each
glance Bestowed sidelong by love-lorn girls. (1)

The Poet's prolegomenon

Spurn, gentle readers, all its faults, Take note of merits, where there are
some, And hear this Counsel of the Band, In verses, by Damodara. (2)

The City of

There is, as we know, a city blest With wealth and every good-by name
Benares-ornament of all The earth's extent, its citizens Sophisticates and
yogic saints. (3)

Венатез sherched

Where, so they say, union with Him Who bears as crown the crescent moon Is
easy for embodied souls Still yielding to the senses's pleasures; (4)

Where even courtesans attain A semblance of the mien of Siva: Fond as they
are of baths in ash, (Or should that read of lucre!); Their bodies graced
with crescent moons (Or does that mean bedizened with gold! Or decked with
nail-mark lunular!"); Cobra-encircled (exerted, rather, By trains of
fitting paramours?); (5)

Where the sky shines as though in bloom With banners fluttering in wind
that blows Off from the towering pinnacles Of dwellings of the gods; (6)

The very surface of whose soil, Reddened with lac that drips on it From
soles of passing women's feet, Gleams like hibiscus blossom; (7)

18

वान बुद्धनीमतम्

यत्र च रमणीभूषणरवबधिरितसकलदिङ्गनभोभागे । शिष्याणां नाचार्यैरवद्यमवधार्यते
पठताम् ॥ ८॥

विन्ध्यधराधरभूरिव या राजति मत्तवारणोपेता । बहुलनिशीथवतीव
प्रोज्ज्वलधिष्ण्योपशोभिता या च ॥ ६ ॥

यतिगणगुणसमुपेता या नित्यं छन्दसामिव प्रचितिः । वनपङ्किरिव ससाला
तुरुष्कसेनेव बहलगन्धर्वा ॥ १० ॥

तारागणो ऽकुलीनः प्रियदोषा यत्र कौशिकाः सततम् । गद्ये वृत्तच्यवनं
परगृहरोधस्तथाचेषु ॥ ११ ॥

शूलभृतो व्यालास्था पदवेदिषु यत्र धातुवादित्वम् । सुरतेष्वबलाक्रमणं
दानच्छेदो मदच्युतौ करिणाम् ॥ १२ ॥

The Band's Couns

Where every heavenly quarter's deaf From women's clinking jewellery, And
teachers cannot hear mistakes Their students make in recitation; (8)

Which, with in roof-top terraces, Shines like the slopes of Vindhya's peaks
That teem with elephants in rut Like nights of the waning moon, adorned
With corrascating stars, it's bright With radiant mansion block (9)

Abounding in ascetics' merit It's like a monograph on metrics, Full of
catsurar, feet and suchlike, And with its ramparts (sive: Sal ren) It seems
a veritable forest, With many bards (or thoroughbredi) It's like a Turkish
army. (10)

There only stars are "lowly born", Or rather nor sunk down to earth, There
owls "will always pick at faults", Or should that mean årlight in nights!
"Depravity..."! No, laper of metre Is found in prose alone; And take-overs
of others' "homes" Can only happen playing dice. (11)

A "faith in rogues...!" No love for makes Is only found in Siva there,
"Alchemist fraud" (or should that means The science of verbal restr) is
found Amongst grammarians; the weak Are sat upon... in acts of love; And
"gifts aren't honoured there..."-or, no: The flow of icher sometimes steps
Of elephants in rut. (12)

तीव्रकरत्वं भानोरविवेको यत्र मिवहृदयानाम् । योगिषु दण्डग्रहणं संधिच्छेदः
प्रगृह्येषु ॥ १३ ॥

छन्दःप्रस्तारविधी गुरवो यस्यामनार्जवस्थितयः । वीणायां परिवादो
द्विजनिलयेष्वप्रसन्नत्वम् ॥ १४ ॥

अनुरूपवृत्तघटना सत्कविकृतरूपकेषु लोके च । रमणीवचने यस्यां माधुर्य
काव्यबन्धे च ॥ १५॥

यस्यामुपवनवीथ्यां तमालपत्त्राणि युवतिवदने च । नखरप्रहारकणितं तन्त्रीवाद्ये
च सुरतकलहे च ॥ १६ ॥

नन्दनवनाभिरामा विबुधवती नाकवाहिनीजुष्टा ।

अमरावतीव यान्या विश्वसृजा निर्मिता जगति ॥ १७॥

The Bated's Counsel

All are taxed keenly,..by the sun, There is no judgement, ...in friends'
hearts: Batons are grasped, ...by mendicants; Liaisons break... between the
words Which (scholars of good Sanskrit say) Should never coalesce. (13)

In parsing of prosodic measure, (...But can the next word really mean
Elders are void of rectitude! No tilde mark the heavy beats. The strains of
slander!... (no) the strain Of lutes is heard; restlessness reigns In
brahmins houses..! (No, insteadi) In nests of birds. (Or might that mean:)
"In brahmin homes is abstinence' (1) (14)

There, in the world (and on the stage In plays of skillful dramatists) Good
actions (or good plots) are seen. There Sweetness reigns in poetry And in
the words of women. (15)

Adorning faces of young girls, And by the paths of city parkı Are found
Tamala leaves. The sounds induced when struck by nails Are heard from lutes
in concert halls And battlegrounds of love. (16)

Delightful for her gracious parks, And savoured by the river Ganges, Home
to the wise-she seems to be A second Amaravati Created by the demiurge On
earth (for isn't Indra's city Prized for its forest Nandand, The home of
gods and favoured haunt Of his celestial army?). (17)

42

दामोदरमुमकिन कुट्टनीमतम्

तस्यां खगपतितनुरिव विलासिनां हृदयशोकसंजननी । आकृष्टेश्वरहृदया
प्रालेयनगाधिराजतनयेव ॥ १८ ॥

संसक्तभोगिनेता मन्दरधरणीभृतो यथा मूर्तिः । उपरिगता शूलानामन्धासुरगावलेखेव ॥
१६ ॥

समुवास वाररामा मानसवसतेः शरीरिणी शक्तिः। निःशेषवेशयोषिद्विभूषणं मालती नाम ॥
२० ॥

पेशलवचसां वसतिलींलानामालयः स्थितिः प्रेग्नः । भूमिः परिहासानामावसथो
वक्रकथितानाम् ॥ २१ ॥

सा शुश्राव कदा चिद्धवलालयपृष्ठदेशमधिरूढा । केनापि गीयमानां
प्रसङ्गपतितामिमामार्याम् ॥ २२ ॥

यौवनसौन्दर्यमदं दूरेणापास्य वारवनिताभिः । यत्वेन वेदितव्याः
कामुकहृदयार्जनोपायाः ॥ २३ ॥

श्रुत्वाथ विपुलजघना मनसीदं मालती चकार चिरम् । अतिसाम्प्रतमुपदिष्टं
सुहृदेवानेन साधुना पठता ॥ २४ ॥

The Baud's Counsel

Now here there lived a courtesan -Inspiring pain in hearts of wooers, She
seemed like Tärkaya (Vişnu's bird) Producing grief in serpents' hearts;
Drawing the hearts of wealthy men, She bote the mien of Pärvati, The
daughter of Himalaya, Who turned the heart of Site; (18)

With suitors' eyes all glued to her, Her form was like Mount Mindara
(Which, when the sea of milk was churned, Had tied to it a serpent-rope);
She soared above all other whores Like demon Andhak's skeleton Alafi upon
the Trident's prongs, (19)

-The god of love's incarnate puissance, The cynosure of prostitutes, Her
name was Malati, (20)

A repertoire of repartes, A cache of frolic coquetry, Affection's home and
laughter's storehouse, An entrepôt of honeyed words. (21)

One day, while on her white-washed terrace, She overheard this verse being
sung By somebody to whom by chance It just so happened to occur: (22)

43

"The loveliness of youth breeds pride, Harlots! cast it far aside
Diligently learn the arts With which to inveigle lovers' hearts." (23)

Now Milati, of generous thighs, On hearing this, at length reflected: "This
was quite opportune advice Recited by that honest man As if he'd been a
confidant. (24)

The heroine is introduced:

She overbears ane day,

ANSKRIT

44

दामोदरनुभविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

तगत्वा पृच्छामो विकरालां कलितसकलसंसाराम् । यस्याः कामिजनौघो दिवानिशं
द्वारदेशमध्यास्ते ॥ २५॥

इति मनसि सा निवेश्य द्रुततरमवतीर्य वेश्मनः शिखरात् । विकरालाभवनवरं
परिजनपरिवारिता प्रययौ ॥ २६ ॥

अथ विरलोन्नतदशनां निम्नहं स्थूलचिपिटनासाग्राम् ।
उल्बणचूचुकलक्षितशुष्ककुचस्थानशिथिलकृत्तितनुम् ॥ २७ ॥

गम्भीरारक्तदृशं निर्भूषणलम्बकर्णपालीं च । कतिपयपाण्डुरचिकुरां
प्रकटशिरासंततायतग्रीवाम् ॥ २८ ॥

सितधौतवसनयुगलां विविधौषधिमणिसनाथगलसूत्राम् । तन्वीमङ्गुलिमूले तपनीयमयीं च
वालिकां दधतीम् ॥ २६ ॥

गणिकागणपरिकरितां कामिजनोपायनप्रसक्तदृशम् । आसन्द्यामासीनां विलोकयामास
विकरालाम् ॥ ३० ॥

अवलोक्य सा विधाय चितिमण्डललीनमौलिना प्रणतिम् । परिपृष्टकुशलवार्ता
समनुज्ञातासनं भेजे ॥ ३१ ॥

The Band's Counsel

"Let's go and question Vikarälä, Who's plumbed the ways of all the world:
By day and night there throng her threshold Such crowds of amotosi." (25)

And Malan solicits counsel

This fixed in mind, swift as she can, Down from the house's roof she comes,
And off she sets, servants in train, To Vikarala's palace. (26)

Her jutting teeth were sparse, her jaw Was sunk, her nose-tip broad and
flat -Projecting nipples, withered breasts-And skin hung loose from her
body. (27)

Viharala, the batud, described.

She'd deeply sunken reddened eyes, Distended carlobes unadorned, And
several whitened strands of hair: And spread across her straining neck The
veins protruded visibly. (28)

Two bleached and laundered cloths for dress; A range of magic herbs and
gems Tied to a thread around her neck. And, at the base of her thumb, she
wore A slender golden signet ring. (29)

There she beheld her, Vikarälä! Seated upon a stool, encircled Inside a
group of prostitutes, Her mind's eye fixed on wheedling gifts From each
one's lover client. (30)

She dropped her eyes and then bowed down, Pressing her head upon the floor.
Enquiries for her health were made And taking leave, she took a seat. (31)

45

Now Malatt presents berself

दामोदरशुलविवि कुट्टनीमतम्

अथ विरचितहस्तपुटा सप्रश्रयमासनं समुत्सृज्य । इदमूचे विकरालामवसरमासाद्य
मालती वचनम् ॥ ३२ ॥

विदधासि हरिमकौस्तुभमहरि रविमगजनाथममरेन्द्रम् । अद्रविणं द्रविणपतिं नियतं
मतिगोचरे पतितम् ॥ ३३ ॥

अयमेव बुद्धिविभवं हृतविभवस्ते पटच्चरावरणः । कामुकलोकः कथयति सत्तागारेषु
भुञ्जानः ॥ ३४॥

उपसंहृतान्यकर्मा धनवर्मा नर्मदाङ्घियुगलस्य । सकलसमर्पितसम्पद्यदुपेतः
पादपीठत्वम् ॥ ३५ ॥

यदुपनतो नयदत्तः सागरदत्तस्य मध्यमः पुत्रः । प्रीणयति मदनसेनां विधाय
पितृमन्दिरं रिक्तम् ॥ ३६ ॥

यल्लीलापितचरणौ मञ्जर्या भट्टपुतनरसिंहः । परितोषं व्रजति परं मृदु मृगन्
पाणियुगलेन ॥ ३७॥

यन्निःशेषितविभवो दीक्षितभवदेवपुत्रशुभदेवः । निर्भत्सितो ऽपि नोज्झति
केसरसेनागृहद्वारम् ॥ ३८ ॥

The Bawd's Counsel

Seizing her moment, Mälati Then getting up, with due respect, Clasping her
hands (in supplication) Addressed these words to Vikarälä: (32)

"You'd render Vişnu stripped of jewels, The Sun divested of his steeds, And
Indra, prince of gods, you'd leave Without his lordly elephant; Kubéra
you'd make destitute Without a doubt, if once they'd stumbled Within the
ambit of your guile. (33)

"There are the lovers, dressed in rags, Feeding themselves in almshouse
kitchens: Stripped of their worldly wealth they now Proclaim your panoply
of talents. (34)

"That Dhanavárman, letting go His every other task at hand, Should offer
all his fortune up To Narmada, whose pair of heels He serves as ottoman;
(35)

"That Nayadátta, middle son Of Sägara, demeaned, should now Have emptied
out his forebears' home To feed Madanasena's whims, (16)

"That Narasimha, son of Bhatt, Should reach a pitch of happiness While
rubbing tenderly the feet That Manjari in play extends; (37)

"That Subha, Bhávadéva's heir, The Saiva oblate, drained his wealth And
though upbraided cannot quit Késaraséna's threshold; (38)

दामोदरसुतिरचितं कुड्नीमतम्

हीनान्वयजन्मानो गुरणहीना रोगिणो निराकृतयः । उपसेविता मयापि

रुचिराकृतिमालतीमूचे ॥ ४३ ॥

किंकरीकुरुते ॥ ४४ ॥

मधुरस्मितसुभगवीक्षितविशेषः ॥ ४५ ॥

The Baud's Coun

"That other women of the street Should also gull admiring men And leave
them just with tattered rags -All this is easily achieved With counselling
dispensed by you. (19)

love. (40)

not get what I desire. Not even when I hawk about My body as my
merchandise. (41)

Their dress and conduct, and the ways To cause to fall on them in sheets
The arrows of the Mind-born god." (42)

by play Of eyebrows gently quivering there, Will bring the firmest to your

The Batod' Counsel

This very loveliness of face, When reaching even jast the ears Of swains of
yours, must surely place Inside their hearts, intensely felt, An amatory
ache. (46)

This row of teeth of yours alone That flashes like bright lightning streaks
Inspires in men, acutely felt, The pain of burning passion. (47)

This flow of words of yours, mignonne, That trumps the koel's dulcet call
Rings out like Siddhas' magic spells For drawing all your lovers. (48)

This pair of curving breasts, folatre. Are where the god of Love resides:
These are your instruments of pleasure; To look for other means is
pointless. (49)

In whom don't they engender passion, These slender arms of yours, adomed,
Curvaceous girl, with golden torques And soft as lotus fibres? (50)

And this your middle region here, Although it's very slim indeed, Is no
less fit to carry out The god of love's behests and can Transport a man of
flesh and blood To that extreme and fatal state Of love (if love is
unrequited). (31)

The Baud Couniel

This line of hair beneath your navel, It has the sheen of filaments That
string the bow of mind-bom Love To render young men walking wounded Pierced
by the points of darts of Kama. (sa)

These ample cheeks, gorgeous as slabs Of gold, my callipygean girl, Will
hold all youths in servitude And drain away the power that's gained By
yogins' self-denial. (33)

This lovely pair of thighs of yours Like plantain stems (so smooth and
cmol)-Tell me, my beauty, is there a man Who would not hanker after them To
soothe the heat of passion's flames! (54)

These rounded calves of yours are like The golden boughs that stir upon The
wish-fulfilling tree of youth Who in this world would not crave them To win
its fruit: the fruit of love! (35)

Your lotus-feet have overcome The lily's grace and trampled down The
pomegranate's rosy blooms Who wouldn't have them occupy, As ornaments, his
heart! (56)

This swaying gait of yours casts shame Upon the prince of elephants And
mocks the goose; it cannot but Churn up the hearts of youths. (37)

But if you're curious to learn. Focus your thoughts, slim girl, and listen.
I'll teach you all my wits have taught me. (58)

दामोदरा कुट्टनीमतम्

स्वीकुरु तावत्प्रथमं नृपसेवक भट्टसूनुमतियत्नात् । स्वाधीनामतिविपुलां यदि
सम्पदमीहसे सुतनु ॥ ५६ ॥

प्रत्यासन्नग्रामे स्वयं प्रभुः पितरि नित्यकटकस्थे ।
भट्टसुतश्चिन्तामणिराकृष्टो भवति पुत्रि नियमेन ॥ ६० ॥

श्रणु तस्य चारुहासिनि वेषग्रहणं च चेष्टितं चैव । निपतति च यथा तूर्णं
प्रियसुरभिकुसुमशरासनप्रसरे ॥ ६१ ॥

लम्बश्रवणनिवेशितकरपत्रिकघटितदशनपङ्किश्च ॥ ६२॥

करशाखाश्रितमुद्रिकचामीकरकण्ठसूत्रिकाभरणः।
परिमृष्टगावकुङ्कुमकिंचित्पिञ्जरितवसनसंवीतः ॥ ६३ ॥

प्रविलम्बिकुसुमदामकगलमण्डनजातरूपकृतशोभः ।

The Bawd's Counsel

Before all else, at any cost, You should lay hold of Bhatta's son, -the
Bhatt, I mean, who serves the king-That is, of course, assuming, sweetie,
You truly want to get your hands On really serious fortune. (59)

Near here he rules a town himself -His father's always out in camps. The
son of Bhatt, if caught, my child, Would definitely prove a gem To grant
your every wish. (60)

Ah yes, how prettily you laugh! Listen to how he'll dress and act, And how
you soon can have him fall In range of Kama's flowery bow. (61)

His hair, exuberant, is styled To leave a crest that's standing up Of just
five finger-breadths in length. Distended car-lobes beat inside Combs that
are shaped like palm-tree leaves. (62)

By way of jewellery he wears A golden necklace, signet-rings Upon his
fingers; and his clothes Are lightly tinged with turmeric Rubbed off from
massaged limbs. (63)

He's primped with dangling flowery wreaths, With gold, a choker at his
throat; His shoes are Turkish Khussa-mules Or other suchlike fopperies With
wax inside (to soften them). (64)

It's Bhatta's

son that the

should aim for

36

शमीवर तुट्टनीमतम्

नानावर्णविवेष्टितबहलदशापाशबद्धततकेशम् । मन्सीसपवर्क कर्णे ॥ ६५ ॥
एकस्मिन्दलवीटकमपरस्मिन्सीसपत्रकं

उच्चण्डकनकगभितकुङ्कुमपिञ्जरितवस्तिपरिधानम् । स्थूलतरकाचवर्तकमालां च गले
दधानेन ॥ ६६॥

वृश्चिकरञ्जितकररुहकरमूलनिबद्धशङ्खचक्रेण । प्रथमववस्त्वं भजता
ताम्बूलकरङ्कवाहिनानुगतः ॥ ६७ ॥

श्रेष्ठिवणिग्विटकितवप्रधानरङ्गस्य सुमहतो मध्ये ।
शूलापालस्थापितकतिपयवधोरुपीठिकासीनः ॥ ६८ ॥

उत्सङ्गार्पितखङ्गैरयथातथभाषिभिर्महीद्धत्यम् । विभ्राणैरनुजीविभिरधिष्ठितः
पञ्चषैः पुरुषैः ॥ ६६ ॥

चतुरतरसेवकार्पितपृष्ठपरिक्षिप्तपूर्वदेहांशः ।
अन्तविधृतावेलप्रोच्छूनकपोलकलितकरपर्णः ॥ ७० ॥

The Bawd's Coun

He's followed by a page who holds A small étui for betel-nut-A person in
the flower of youth: His head of hair is coiffed and bound In turbans made
of many strips Of rainbow-coloured plaited cloth, With, in one ear, a
leaden earring. A coiled leaf inside the other, His clothes are shot with
glinting gold And tinctured at the hems with saffron. And round his neck, a
chunky chain Of crystal beads; his finger-nails Are died with scarlet
amaranth, And conch-shell bracelets ring his wrists." (65-67)

Appointed by a brothel-keeper (To care for him), some men will set A broad
divan for him to sit Amidst an ample crowd in which The larger part are
traders, merchants, And rewr rakes and rogues. (65)

Retainers-five or six of them, Swords at their sides-attend on him,
False-spoken men, insufferably They bear aloft their pride. (69)

Part of his upper body he rests Against a bolster held in place By dextrous
servitors. His hand, Soft as young leaves, he rests against His cheeks that
stretch to hold inside His dose of betel pass. (70)

अनपेक्षितप्रसङ्गः पुनः पुनः पठति सोन्नतक्षुकः । गाथाश्लोकप्रायं भावितचेता
यथातथाधीतम् ॥ ৩৭॥

परसुभाषितश्रवणम् ॥ ७२ ॥

प्रणयविश्वासी ॥ ७३ ॥

पत्त्रकर्तरीं सततम् ॥ ७४ ॥

भार्गवतां परशुरामो ऽपि ॥ ७६ ॥

The Baud's Counsel

And every now and then he sings Some verse, without apparent context,
Mainly in (simple) loka metre, The way he learnt it-any old how-Raising his
brows, his heart aglow. (71)

When others turn a pithy verse, Wobbling his head in wonderment, Slapping
those standing round, in fits Of connoisseurial glee, with cries Of "Oh,
how sharp! how excellent!"," He drowns them out. (72)

"That's what my father told the king In indignation privately; The king, he
told my father this." In such a fashion he shows off The friendship and the
confidence His father and the king enjoy. (73)

Whether or not he knows about The artful cutting up of leaves He always
carries round with him His scissors so he may show off, In company, his
skill in crafts. (24)

"The son of Bhatt outshines in skill Närad himself and sundry sages In
Brahma's histrionic arts, In singing and in playing drums And other
instruments. (75)

"As for the likes of Vasu, Nanda, Citra, Danda, and such arrays, In
handling missiles, swords and knives, Before him even Jämadágnya (The Räma
famous for his axe) Is nothing other than a bowman, Or is (as anyway he wa
A scien of the Birgu clan. (76)

मतम्

वात्स्यायनमयमबुधं बाह्य दूरेण दत्तकाचार्यम् । गरणयति मन्मथतन्वे पशुतुल्यं
राजपुर्व च ॥ ७७॥

यः प्रार्थितो ऽपि यत्त्रात्कवचं राधासुतो ददाति स्म ।
अविचिन्तितवसुवर्षस्त्यागगुणं हसति तस्यायम् ॥ ७८ ॥

प्रपलायनैकहृदये यो विक्रममातनोति हरिणे ऽपि। सिहस्य तस्य शौर्यं वपाकरं
भट्टपुत्रस्य ॥ ७६ ॥

आखेटके ऽपि कौतुकमस्त्येव जयश्च चञ्चले लक्ष्ये । भट्टभयेन न खेलति भट्टसुतः
कि त्वतिप्रकटम् ॥ ८० ॥

इति निजसेवकनिगदितरामणिकावचनजनितपरितुष्ट्या । अन्तर्मुदितो बूते मामेष
खलीकरोतीति ॥ ८१ ॥

कतमत्कतमल्लग्नं प्रस्थानं का च नर्तकी भद्रा । षिङ्गटके का नृत्यति
कोहलभरतोदितक्रियया ॥ ८२ ॥

The Band's Counsel

"In matters of erotic love, Vätayäyaná he thinks a fool; And Dáttaka's
beyond the palt; And Räjapátra's like a beast. (7)

"Unthinkingly he rains down wealth And laughs to scorn the charity Of
Karna, who, though begged, gave up His breastplate only with a qualm. (78)

"Since even when its prey's a deer, Whose only thought is running free, A
lion shows its courage off. Such bravery, for Bhatta's son, Is matter for
embarassment. (29)

"Fond of the chase, he hits with ease A moving mark, in public, though, The
son of Bhatta doesn't hunt Because his father frets for him, (Or should
that mean; be hardly moves, In terror of his father)" (80)

Swelling imide with happiness Engendered by these honeyed tales Told by his
page, he remonstrates: "This fellow here abashes me". (81)

"Which type of melodrama's in! Which opera-girl is good these days! In
operetta-musicals," Who dances in the way that's taught By Kóhala and
Bhátata! (81)

The Bated's Counsel

"How do you stand in rhythmic styles! And how about in one-man plays, The
genre of Dhaváluka?" He grills the dancing master thus About performances
and such. (83) Devotedly, with cries of "Bravo!" -Inopportunely
interposed-Along with gifts of betel leaves, He takes his garland from his
neck And gives it to the dancing girl. (84)

"The cadence of her arms! the way She plants her feet! her gracefulness Her
hips gyrations!--she alone Can pull these off-What poise! What technical
perfection! (85)

"With requisite emotions, moods, And in the way she gestures too, In varied
moves across the stage, -In all these things she outperforms Rambhä
herself. Why speak at all Of other mortal dancing girls 1" (86)

Thus, as the overture concludes, He loudly descants on her rôle Straining
his throat-no pause for breath-Rapt, though she's only shown her feet. (87)

64

दामोदरमुसविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

प्रायेण भट्टतनयो भवतीदृशवेषचेष्टितो वत्से । तं मदनवागुरान्तः पातयसि यथा तथा
बूमः ॥ ८८ ॥

चतुरा प्रागल्भ्यवती परचित्तज्ञानकौशलोपेता । योज्या तस्मिन्दूती
वक्रोक्तिविभूषिता प्रयवेन ॥ ८६॥

स उपेत्य तयावसरे ताम्बूलं सुमनसश्च दत्त्वेत्थम् । अभिधातव्यः सुन्दरि
मकरध्वजदीपकैर्वचनैः ॥ ६० ॥

जन्मसहस्रोपचितैः पुण्यचयैरद्य फलितमस्माकम् । यत्त्वं नयनानन्दन नयनावसरं
समेतो ऽसि ॥ ३१॥

चाटुक्रममनुरागं प्रणयरुषी विरहजनितशोकार्तिम् । प्रकटयति वाररमणी नटीव
शिक्षाभियोगेन ॥ ६२ ॥

प्रवयसि यौवनशालिनि हीनकुले सत्कुलप्रसूते च । रोगवति दृढशरीरे समचित्ता
योगिनश्च गणिकाश्च ॥ ६३ ॥

उपचरिताप्यतिमात्रं प्रकटवधूः क्षीणसम्पदः पुंसः ।

पातयति दृशं व्रजतः स्पृहया परिधानमात्रे ऽपि ॥ ६४ ॥

The Bared Counsel

This is, my darling, more or less, The way the son of Bhatta acts. And
dresses. Let me tell you how You'll snare him in the nets of love. (88)

You must make certain to engage A go-between who's clever, bold, A girl who
has the skill to see Inside the thoughts of others, graced With craftiness
of speech. (39)

Choosing her moment, she'll approach And give him betel nut and flowers,
And this, my love, is how she'll speak With words that kindle passion. (ge)

"The merits of a thousand births Heaped up have blossomed and bone fruit
For me this day, o ryes' delight, Now that you've reached my field of view.
(91)

"The way an actress does, a whore Projects affection, anger, pangs Of
separation, blandishments, Or love-honing the skills of art. (ga)

"Both prostitutes and anchorites Show perfect equanimity When looking at
the elderly And blooming youths, the nobly borm And hoi polloi, at
invalids, And at the rudely fit. (93)

"Although excessively indulged And waited on, a courtesan Will cast
desirous eyes upon. The very shirt on a client's back As he departs without
a cent. (94)

66

दामोदरहितीमतम्

इत्थं दृढतरवासितमनसां पुंसां त्वसाम्प्रतं पुरतः ।
वेशविलासवतीनामशरीरशरव्यथाकथनम् ॥ ६५॥

केवलमगणितलाघवदूरपरित्यक्तधीरताभरणा । मुखरयति मां दुराशादग्धसखी तेन कथयामि ॥
६६ ॥

हृदयमधिष्ठितमादौ मालत्याः कुसुमचापबाणेन । चरमं रमणीवल्लभ लोचनविषयं त्वया
भजता ॥ ६७ ॥

चक्षणमुत्कण्टकिताङ्गी चाणमुल्बणदाहवेदनावस्था । चक्षणमुपजाताकम्पा
स्वेदार्द्रवपुः चक्षणं भवति ॥ ६८ ॥

मुहुरविभावितहास्या मुहुरुज्झितधीरभावमत्युच्चैः । रोदिति गायति च पुनः पुनश्च
मौनावलम्बिनी भवति ॥ ६६ ॥

पतति मुहुः पर्यङ्के मुहुरङ्के परिजनस्य मुहुरवनौ । किसलयकल्पिततल्पे
मुहुरम्भसि मुहुरनङ्गसंतप्ता ॥ १०० ॥

महिषीव पङ्कदिग्धा हंसीव मृणालवलयपरिवारा । सुभग मयूरीवासौ भुजङ्गविद्वेषिरणी
जाता ॥ १०१ ॥

and in the Alamkiraiarvarva (p.83).

The Bated's Counsel

"So there's in fact no need at all To speak of pangs of love induced By
courtesans in front of men Who're thoroughly experienced. (95)

"It's just that here this friend of mine Tormented by her vain desires Has
cast aside the fortitude She wears as her adorning grace And disregards
potential slights. -It's she who makes me garrulous, And that is why I
speak. (96)

"At first the heart of Mälari Was penetrated by a shaft From Kama's bow,
and then by you, You Don Juan, as soon as she Had set her eyes on you. (97)

"One moment all her hairs stand up, The next she's scorched with flames of
pain; One moment quivering all over; All of a sudden drenched in sweat. (98)

"She's bursting now with hidden mirth, And now she throws off self-control
And weeps aloud; again she sings, And now again she's silent. (99)

"Just now she falls upon the sofa, Next in the laps of maids in waiting,
Now on the ground, now on a bed Of tender leaves; now in a flash, Ablaze
with Kamadéva's fire, She plunges into water. (100)

"Just like a female buffalo. Who's smeared with mud she smeared with balms,
Just like a goose who's ringed with twirls Of lotus-strands, she's wrapped
her wrists With (cooling) latar-fibre hands, Just like a peahen, loathing
snakes, She's come to loathe her stains. (101)

कदली चन्दनपङ्कः पङ्केरुहनीरहारघनसारम् । शशधरशशधरकान्तं शान्त्यै नो
मदनहुतभुजस्तस्याः ॥ १०२ ॥

अपसारय घनसारं कुरु हारं दूर एव कि कमलैः । अलमलमालि मृणालैरिति वदति दिवानिशं
बाला ॥ १०३ ॥

संकल्पैरुपनीतं त्वामन्तिकमुल्लसन्मनोवृत्तिः । दृढमालिङ्गति
पश्चात्स्वभुजापीडेन याति वैलक्ष्यम् ॥ १०४ ॥

कुसुमामोदी पवनः पिककूजितभृङ्गसार्थरसितानि । इयमियती सामग्री घटिता विधिनैव
तद्विनाशाय ॥ १०५ ॥

अबलां बलिना नीतां दशामिमां मकरकेतुना रक्ष । आपत्पतितोद्धृतये भवति हि
शुभजन्मनां जन्म ॥ १०६ ॥

नो गृह्णन्ति यथार्था अर्थिजनैर्निगदिता गिरः प्रायः । मालत्या गुणलेशं शृणु
धृष्टतया तथापि कथयामि ॥ १०७॥

आस्फालयतो नूनं धनुरतनीः कौसुमं रजः पतितम् । संहृत्य सा सुमध्या विश्वसृजा
निमिता तेन ॥ १०८ ॥

The Bawd's Counsel

"Bananas, lilies, sandal-paste, And water, camphor, strings of pearls, The
moon, and moonstones none will do To quench the fire of love in her. (sea)

"By day and night the girl exclaims: 'Remove the camphor, take the pearls!
What do I need with lotusesf Enough, my friend, enough of strands Of
lotus-fibre intertwined." (103)

"In thought she fetches you to her -Her powers of fantasy intense-And folds
you tightly to her heart: But then, the pressure of her arms Awakens her to
shame. (104)

"A breeze that bears the scents of flowers, Some bees ahumming, koels
cooing -All these deployed by fare together Conspire to cause her downfall.
(105)

"Take care of her, a helpless waif, Who's been reduced to such a state By
mighty Love, the fortunate Are surely born to rescue those Whom trials and
woes befall. (106)

"People will often not believe The words of those who supplicate, But
nonetheless, quite brazenly, I'll try to give a thumbnail sketch Of
Milati's distinctions. Listen: (107)

"Surely the Maker of the world Did fashion her, this slender girl, On
picking up the flowery dust That fell when Karma shook his bow. (108)

यो कुनीमतम्

उपहसति गिरिसुताया लावण्यं येन सततलग्नेन । न द्रवतामुपनीतं
भोगीन्द्रविभूषणस्य देहार्धम् ॥ १०६ ॥

शशधरबिम्बार्धगतां छायामिव सैंहिकेयवदनस्य । अलिपटलनीलकुटिलामलकावलिमलिकसंनिधौ
वहति ॥ ११० ॥

सरसिजमस्थिरशोभं विभ्रमरहितं च मण्डलं शशिनः । केन समेतु समत्वं हृदयप्रिय
मालतीवदनम् ॥ १११ ॥

अलिरुपरि तदीच्चक्षाणयोर्भान्त्वासौगन्ध्यसूचितविशेषः । निपतति करर्णाम्बुरुहे
निर्गुणताप्यवसरे साध्वी ॥ ११२ ॥

बिभ्राणे ऽरुणिमानं सहजं जितबन्धुजीवरुचिमधरे । यदलक्तकविन्यसनं तत्तस्या
मण्डनक्रीडा ॥ ११३ ॥

चित्रमिदं यत्कृशता तस्या वलिपरिगृहीतमध्यस्य । अथवा नो विधिविहिता
महताप्यपनीयते तनुता ॥ ११४॥

आस्तामपरस्ताव-त्तस्याः स्मरवसतिपृथुतरनितम्बः । श्वथयति कपिलमुनेरपि
दृक्पथपतितः समाधानम् ॥ ११५ ॥

The Band's Counsel

"She laughs at Umi's loveliness, Which being forever fused with Him, Can
melt away one snake-twined half Of Siva's body, never both-(Or should that
mean that unlike bers It liquidives half, no maте, Of male clients' anets)
(109)

"She wears her locks across her brow, Curly and black like bees in maur,
Like Rahu's shadow spread across A portion of the full moon's orb. (no)

"The lotus has a transient beauty, The moon's (bright) urh is motionless,
Ah bel ami! Can any thing With Malati's sweet visage vie! (111)

"Some bee that strays above her eyes, On guessing, since they have no
smell, That they're distinct (from lotates), Falls on the lotus on her ear
How fortunate, when opportune, Can even lack of virtues be! (113)

"When she puts lac upon her lip, Already redder naturally Than blossoms of
the handb It is her motions that give grace. (113)

"How strange her waist should be so thin And yet have space for fleshy
folds.... Laid hold of by a strong-armed man! Perhaps it isn't strange at
all For slenderness designed by fate Is not removed by something strong.
(114)

"We needn't touch just now upon Another of her parts: her rump. An ample
seat for Kimadeva, Which, if it anly crossed his view, Would make the
concentration flag Of even Kipili the seer. (115)

तिमतम्

तस्या रम्भावपुषो रम्भोपममूरुयुगलमवलोक्य । मकरध्वजो ऽपि सहसा
निजसायकलक्ष्यतां याति ॥ ११६ ॥

जघनभरालसयाता नो याता सा विलोचनप्रसरम् । तिष्ठति तेन मनोहर शरजन्मा
ब्रह्मचर्येण ॥ ११७ ॥

यदि कथमपि मधुमथनः पश्यति तामसमबारणसर्वस्वम् । तदसारभारभूतं लक्ष्मीवपुरुरसि
विनिहितं मनुते ॥ ११८ ॥

यदि पतति सा कथं चिद्वीक्षणविषयं हरस्य तदवश्यम् । त्रिभुवनमशिवं कुरुते
वामेतरदेहभागमासाद्य ॥ ११६ ॥

सौन्दर्यं तत्तादृशमशेषयोषिद्विलच्तणं सृजतः । यन्निष्पन्नं धातुस्तन्मन्ये
काकतालीयम् ॥ १२०॥

सहजविलासनिवासं तस्या वपुरनभिवीक्षमाणस्य । मन्ये नाकाधिपतेः सहस्रमपि
चक्षुषां विफलम् ॥ १२१ ॥

शिथिलयतु कुसुमचापं क्षिपतु शरान् बाणधौ मनोजन्मा । संसारसारभूता विचरति भुवि
मालती यावत् ॥ १२२॥

वात्स्यायनमदनोदयदत्तकविटवृत्तराजपुत्राद्यैः । उच्छ्रुसितं यत्कि
चित्तत्तस्या हृदयदेशमध्यास्ते ॥ १२३ ॥

The Bated's Counsel

"She has the shapeliness of Rambhi: If even Käma saw her thighs -Like
plantain stems-, he would become, At once, the butt of his own bolts. (116)

"You handsome man, if Skanda's chaste It's since he hasn't glimpsed her yet
Ambling along with lazy gait, Because she's laden with those hips. (117)

"If Visnu were to see this girl, An arsenal of all Love's weapons, He'd
think of Laksmi on his chest As just some useless burden. (118)

"And if by chance she crossed the path Of Hara's gaze, then certainly She'd
make the three worlds Siva-less A place of inauspiciousness By occupying
God's right side." (119)

"That the creator, making her, Produced such beauty, quite unlike What any
other woman has, Is serendipitous, it seems. (tao)

"I feel there isn't any use In having all those thousand eyes If Indra
doesn't see her shape, A seat of natural gracefulness. (121)

"Now Kama may unstring his bow And put his arrows back again Into the
quiver, while Mälati, Quintessence of this worldly life, Still wanders on
the earth. (122)

"Vätsyäyan, Madanódaya, And Dáttaki and Viravetta, And Räjapútra every
thought Sexologists have even breathed Resides already in het heart. (123)

नीमतम्

भरतविशाखिलदत्तिलवृक्षायुर्वेदचित्रसूत्रेषु । पवच्छेदविधाने भ्रमकर्मणि
पुस्तसूदशास्त्रेषु ॥ १२४॥

आतोद्यवादनविधौ नृत्ते गीते च कौशलं तस्याः । अभिधातुं यदि शक्तो बदनसहखेण
भोगिनामीशः ॥ १२५ ॥

परिगलदालोलांशुकमपयन्त्रणमुपरि मालती रभसात् । निपतति नापुण्यवतां
रतिलालसमानसा रहसि ॥ १२६ ॥

रतिरसरभसास्फालनचलवलयनिनादमिश्रितं तस्याः । तत्कालोचितमणितं श्रुतिपथमुपयाति
नाल्पपुण्यस्य ॥ १२७ ॥

इत्थमभिधीयमानः शुभमध्ये यदि भवेदुदासीनः । एवं ततो ऽभिधेयः संदर्शितकोपया
दूत्या ॥ १२८ ॥

कि सौभाग्यमदो ऽयं यौवनलीलाभिरूपतादर्पः । सहजप्रेमोपनतां मालतिकां न बहु
मन्यसे येन ॥ १२६ ॥

न गणयति या कुलीनान् द्रविणवतः शास्त्रवेदिनः प्रणतान् । सा भवदर्थे शुष्यति
कुस्थाननिवेशितं धिगनुरागम् ॥ १३० ॥

The Bawd's Counsel

"The monographs of Bháratá, Vitäkhila and Dáttila, Arboriculture, painting
too, The artful cutting up of leaves, Legerdemain and modelling. And
culinary artistry And playing taneful instruments And dance and song-in all
these realms, If anyone could tell her skills, It's Séșa with his thousand
tongues. (124-125)

"It's not on those without good karma That Malati would cast herself Sub
rosa-flapping veil askew, Her becast-straps loosening from her
chest-Impulsively and hot with ardour. (136)

"Only those with no little merit May get to hear her well-timed moans Mised
with her bangles' clinking sound Colliding in the heat of sex." (187)

If he should still remain aloof Even on hearing such a speech, Just then,
my pretty-waisted girl, Your messenger, affecting pique, Should speak to
him like this: (talk)

"Is this the arrogance of luck! Or is it pride in youthfulness! Exuberancef
or comeliness! That makes you slight our Mälati, Who's drawn spontaneously
by love! (say)

"She's spurning men of noble birth, The plutocrats, the polymaths Who lay
themselves prostrate before her. Saving herself for you she withers. A
wretched thing is love bestowed Upon unworthy objects! (130)

The Band's Counsel

"The lotus pond desires the sun; The crescent moon, the head of Siva,
Covered in ash; and she craves you, Who're like a beast, and that's enough
To make me waste away. (131)

"It's no surprise that Malati, Should be a butt of gossipping For having
cast herself upon The crooked, dry, inflexible, Intractable,
emotioniess.... (Ah, no, instead that rather means:) It's no surprise the
jasmine vine Should be renowned for clinging on To routed, saples, rigid,
barbed, And rough Acacia catechu. (132)

"But anyway, what's wrong indeed If though it lies in easy reach The crow
rejects a juicy strand Of lotus stalk, because ashamed Of not being good
enough" (113)

"You lucky man! Don't feel aggrieved That I've addressed you scornfully:
For scathing words of confidantes Of ladies who are mad with love Is
something youths can boast about. (134)

"Like moonlight with the moon itself; Like Vinamälä, "Woodland Wreath",
With Krsna, foe of demon Kamsa; Like shoots of sugar cane with Love, The
friend of Spring: like drunken play With Balaram, like strings of pearls On
women's breasts-like these may shr, This lovely woman, joined with you
Become yet lovelier still. (135-136)

नीमतम्

कि बहुना यदि यूनामुपरि विधातुं समीहमे चरणाम् । तत्कुरु रमणीरत्वं
प्रेमोज्ज्वलमङ्कतस्तूर्णम् ॥ १३७ ॥

अथ तद्वचनश्रवणप्रविजृम्भितमदन भट्टदायादः । उपचरणीयः सुन्दरि
निजवसतिमुपागतस्त्वयाप्येवम् ॥ १३८ ॥

दूरादभ्युत्थानं प्रणमनमात्मासनप्रदानं च । प्रविधेयमञ्चलेन
प्रस्फोटनमङघ्रियुगलस्य ॥ १३६ ॥

ईषदयत्रप्रकटं कक्षोदरबाहुमूलकुचभागम् । संदर्थ्य झटिति यास्यसि
नायकदृग्गोचरात्तूर्णम् ॥ १४० ॥

अथ पर्यङ्कसनाथं दीपोज्ज्वलकुसुमधूपगन्धाढ्यम् । विततवितानकरम्यं प्रवेशितो
वासकावासम् ॥ १४१ ॥

मात्रा ते गुरुजघने सादरमवतारणादिकं कृत्वा । अभिनन्दनीय एभिर्वचनविशेषैः
प्रयतेन ॥ १४२॥

अद्याशिषः समृद्धाः परितुष्टा इष्टदेवता अद्य ।

कल्यारणालंकारो यदलंकृतवानिदं वेश्म ॥ १४३॥

"What need of saying any more! If you should wish to plant a foot Upon the

दामोदरपुर नीमतम्

अनुरूपपावघटनं कुर्वाणस्याद्य कुसुमबाणस्य । सुचिराद्वत संजातः
शरासनाकर्षणश्रमः सफलः ॥ १४४ ॥

विन्यस्य शिरसि चरणं सुभगा गणिकाजनस्य सकलस्य । सौभाग्यवैजयन्तीं सम्प्रति
वत्सा समुत्क्षिपतु ॥ १४५॥

दुहितर एव श्लाध्या धिग्लोकं पुवजन्मसंतुष्टम् । जामातर आप्यन्ते भवादृशा
यदभिसम्बन्धात् ॥ १४६ ॥

दृढपरिचया गुणज्ञा भवद्विधा नार्थनार्हका यदपि । तदपि हृदयाभिनन्दन
दुहितृस्नेहादहं बच्मि ॥ १४७॥

सहजप्रेमोपहृता न्यस्ता त्वयि मालती तथा कार्यम् । न यथा भवति वराकी
त्वद्विप्रियजन्मनां शुचां वसतिः ॥ १४८ ॥

मृदुधौतधूपिताम्बरमग्राम्यं मण्डनं च विभ्रारणा । परिपीतधूपवतिः स्थास्यसि
रमरणान्तिके सुतनु ॥ १४६ ॥

सस्नेहं सव्रीडं ससाध्वसं सस्पृहं च पश्यन्ती । किं चिदृश्यशरीरा
प्रविरलपरिहासपेशलालापा ॥ १५० ॥

The Bawd's Counsel

"It's after, oh, an age that now The effort of the god of love The effort
drawing back his bow-. In that he joins a fitting couple, Is finally
rewarded. (144)

"Now let this lucky girl-my child!-. Placing a foot upon the head Of every
other courtesan,

Unfurl good fortune's victory banner. (145)

"It's daughters who deserve all praise! Fie on the world that feels delight
About the birth of sons alone! Through having daughters we are blessed With
sons-in-law like you! (146)

"A man like you a steadfast friend, A judge of worth doesn't deserve To be
solicited with pleas And yet, ah heart's delight, I speak From fondness for
my daughter. (147)

"Brought here by honest love for you, Our Mälati has been entrusted Into
your charge: please care for her In such a way she's never sad. A prey to
sorrows caused by you Through any unkind acts." (148)

You'll stand before your lover then, Dressed up in soft, washed, scented
clothes, And tastefully chosen ornaments,

And after you've inhaled the smoke Of aromatic cigarettes, Affectionately,
bashfully, With trepidation and desire, You'll gaze at him, svelte-bodied
girl, Some flesh just slightly visible, Your conversation full of charm By
sparing use of jokes. (149-150)

मातरि निर्यातायां परिजनमुक्ते च वासकस्थाने । अभियुञ्जाने रमणे वामाचरणं

The Bawd's Counsel

And when your mother has withdrawn, The bedroom's emptied of your maids,
Then, when he flings himself on you, Resist your lover for a moment. (151)

Erotic battle's what he wants, He pulls you roughly up against him, Then
you should cringe away somewhat And feign reluctance to engage. (112)

When intercourse begins in carnest Allow the urgency of love To be revealed
by slow degrees; Offer yourself at last sincerely, Without restraint, my
child. (153)

And love between the two begins.

Whichever limb of yours he wants To strike, or bite, or scratch, you must
Just let it go impulsively And tender it to him. (154)

React to bites by warbling "иммм...". And in response to being squeezed
You'll make a range of throaty gurgles: When scratched emit your breath in
hisses,

And moan abundantly when slapped, Releasing short and heaving tighs; Your
body-hairs should stand on end And every limb should drip with sweat To
rouse the passions of your men. (155-156)

Spontaneously, at fitting moments, Using your sweetly pleasing voice, Do
imitate the heart-felt cries Of cuckoos, partridges or geese," Or turtle
doves or horses. (157)

मा मा मामतिपीडय मुञ्च क्षणमदय नो समथासि इति गद्गदास्फुटाचारमभिधातव्यस्त्वया
कामी ॥ ११८

अनुबन्धमानुकूल्यं वामत्वं प्रौढतामसामर्थ्यम् । सुरतेषु दर्शयिष्यसि
कामुकभावं स्फुटं बुद्धा ॥ १५६॥

असमञ्जसमश्लीलं दूरोज्झितधैर्यमविनयप्रसरम् । व्यवहारमाचरिष्यसि वृद्धिमुपेते
रतावेगे ॥ १६०॥

अविवेचितनखरच्चतिरामीलितलोचना निरुत्साहा । नायककार्यसमाप्तौ स्थास्यसि

जनयिष्यसि मोहनच्छेदे ॥ १६२॥

दृढतरमालिङ्गा रभसतः कण्ठे ॥ १६४॥

The Batod's Couniel

Slurring your words and stammering. You'll tell your lover: "No! No don't!
Stop squeezing me so much! Let go A moment! Oh! Ah merciless! I can't go
on...!" (158)

When you have clearly understood Your lover's mood, you'll manifest While
making love, some tenderness, Complaisance or recalcitrance, Or
self-assured audacity, Or feeble helplessness. (139)

Now when your passion wases strong, Begin to use unseemly words. Talk
lewdly-drop all self-control-, And cast your manners overboard. (160)

And when the hero's played his part, Lie back a while with loosened limbs,
Without a thought for all those nail-marks, Your eyes tight closed, your
vigour spent. (161)

When sweet delirium is past You should be able to produce Ar once,
embarassed smiles, a look Of being spent from effort, a sense Your body's
powerless with weakness, While covering up your thighs. (162)

With congress over, sip some water; Then go and wash your hands and feet In
some secluded place apart; Sit for a while to tie your hair; Chew on a
little scented paan; Then mount the couch and show your warmth, Embracing
tight your paramour With vehemence about his neck, And speak to him like
this: (163-164)

86

भट्टसुत नूनमिष्टा तव जाया यदनुरक्तहृदयस्य । जनयति परितुष्टिमलं
नापररामापरिष्वङ्गः ॥ १६५॥

सफलं तस्या जन्म स्पृहणीया सैव सकलललनानाम् । गौरी तयैव महिता सुभगंकरणं
तपस्तयाचरितम् ॥ १६६ ॥

सैवैका गुणवसतिस्तस्या एवान्वयः सदा श्लाघ्यः । यस्याः शुभशतभाजः पाणिग्रहणं
त्वया विहितम् ॥ १६७॥

तिष्ठतु सा पुण्यवती वंशद्वयभूषणं वरारोहा । या नापयाति भवतो लक्ष्मीरिव
नरकवैरिणो हृदयात् ॥ १६८ ॥

पातयसि कुवलयनिभे कौतुकमात्रेण लोचने यासु । ता अपि सत्यं सुन्दर हर्षोल्लसिता
न मान्ति गावेषु ॥ १६६ ॥

तनुरपि नाथप्रायः प्रायो मुखरीकरोति लघुमनसः । स्वार्थनिवेशितचित्ता करोमि ते
ऽभ्यर्थनां तेन ॥ १७० ॥

तीव्रस्मरतारुण्या-च्चापलतः कौतुकेन घृणया वा । मद्भाग्यसम्पदा वा दूत्या वा
कौशलात्स्वभावाद्वा ॥ १७१ ॥

1656 तब जाया जाया ते Ex 167b सदा। GDEECE. om. NNNE 168 तिष्ठतु। 2. तिष्ठति
Ex 1686 या नापयाति । 2. या नो उपयाति G: 169 एलसिता] NN, DE, भूमिता (?) G₁,

2. प्रणयाद्

The Baud's Counsel

"Clearly you really love your wife, For since your heart is quite beguiled,
Embracing other women can't Entirely please you, son of Bhatt. (165)

"Her life is fruitful; she alone Is envied by all womankind; She must have
venerated Gauri; Or carried out ascetic feats To guarantee felicity; (166)

"It's she who's merit's one true home, Hers is a sterling pedigree
Throughout its past, this girl you've wed, Who's hoarded up a store of
blessings. (167)

"But leave aside that worthy dame, That lovely-thighed embellishment, Of
both her lines of ancestry: Who never leaves your heart, like Sri Clinging
to Vişnu's breast. (168)

"Even the girls on whom you cast, With lazy yen, your lily-eyes, They can't
indeed, you handsome man, Contain themselves within their limbs, But bubble
forth with joy. (169)

"Even a little bit of love

Towards one's master instigates A tendency to prattle on In those of feeble
intellect, And so, intent upon my hopes, I make request to you: (170)

"Whether it's youth, when love's intense, Impulse, or curiosity, Compassion
or because my luck Now favours me, or else because My go-between is
talented Or just because your nature's such-

यो ऽयं प्रेमलवांशः प्रदर्शितो ऽस्मासु जीवनोपायः । बाधा नाव विधेया
गणिकाजनवृत्तमन्यथा बुद्धा ॥ १७२॥

येन स्नेहः क्रोधः शाठ्यं दाक्षिण्यमार्जवं ब्रीडा । एतानि सन्ति तास्वपि
जीवद्धर्मोपनीतानि ॥ १७३॥

निर्व्याजसमुत्पन्नप्रबलप्रेमाभिभूतहृदयानाम् । दयितविरहाच्क्षमारणां गणिकानां
तृणसमाः प्रारणाः ॥ १७४ ॥

अवाकर्णय साद्भूतमाख्यानं वर्णयामि यद्वृत्तम् । अद्यापि विभर्ति वटो विशेषणं
यदभिसम्बन्धात् ॥ १७५ ॥

अस्ति महीतलतिलकं सरस्वतीकुलगृहं महानगरम् । नाना पाटलिपुवं
परिभूतपुरन्दरस्थानम् ॥ १७६॥

त्रिभुवनपुरनिष्पादनकौशलमिव पृच्छतो विरिश्वस्य । दर्शयितुं निजशिल्पं
वर्णकमिव विश्वकर्मणा विहितम् ॥ १७७ ॥

अश्रेयोभिरनाश्रित-मभिभूतं नाभिभूतिदोषेण । न स्वीकृतमुपसर्गेः
कलिकालमलैरनालीढम् ॥ १७८ ॥

The Based's Counc

(Whatever be the reason why) Please don't shut off that tiny trickle Of
fondness that you've shown to me, Which helps me keep myself alive, As a
result of prejudice About the ways of whores. (171-172)

"For fondness, anger, cruelty, And kindess, rectitude and shame -These
tendencies, adduced among The properties of living beings, Are found in
prostitutes as well. (173)

"The lives of courtesans whose hearts Are overwhelmed by puissant love
That's risen up unfeigned, and who Can't bear their lovers being apart, Are
reckoned slight as straw. (174)

"In illustration, you should hear A tale that will astonish you. I'll tell
you what transpired somewhere In consequence of which a banyan Even today
is specially named. (175)

655

There is a great metropolis, The cynosure of all the world, Sarasvati's
ancestral home, Surpassing Amaravati: They call it Patalipútra. (176)

It's like a model made to scale, To demonstrate his craftsmanship, By
Viivakarma, asked by Brahmä If he had skill to engineer The city that's the
universe. (177)

Devoid of crime, not overcome By degradations, not assailed By
tribulations, quite untouched By Kaliyuga's ills. (178)

89

The doleful sale of Haralata

पातालतलं भोगिभिरम्भोधिर्वारिरवनिचयैश्व । सुरसदनं विबुधगणैर्द्रविरणोपचयैः
पुरं कुवेरस्य ॥ १७६॥

रमणीभिरसुरविवरं कटकं हेमाचलस्य गान्धर्वैः । हरिनगरं ऋतुयूपैः
शमविभवैर्मुनिजनस्थानम् ॥ १८० ॥

तिष्ठन्तु सकलशास्त्रव्यालोचनविमलबुद्धयो विप्राः । सदसद्भरणनितिौ ललना अपि
निकषभूमयो यस्मिन् ॥ १८१ ॥

कलिकालोदितभीत्या क्रतुहुतवहधूमकम्बलावरणः । तिष्ठन्निभूतो ऽपि
कृतश्वरितैरनुमीयते यस्मिन् ॥ १८२॥

अपहरति पिधातुमिव स्वकलङ्कं शशधरः प्रसार्य करान् । रात्रौ यत्र वधूनां
लावण्यं बदनकोषेभ्यः ॥ १८३॥

वल्लभसम्भोगविहतये यस्मिन् ॥ १८४॥

The Band Counsel

A subterranean paradise, Because it has its make...! No: raken A very ocean
for its wealth Of water and of precious gems: A heaven where the yoár akár
No: learned mes, Kubera's city, Because it's heaped with riches. (179)

A demon's cave because it's full Of lovely girls; a mountain reach On
Meru's slopes because there throng Gandharver...! Rather, volim. A true
Vaikuntha (Visnu's city). Because of sacrificial posts: An dirama because
it has Ascetic No: the fruits of peace. (180)

There even women, let alone The brahmin males, whose minds are bright From
study of all sciences, Are touchstones for distinguishing Between what's
good and bad. (11)

There Kitayug, that golden age. Though hid behind a pall of smoke That
wafts from sacrificial fires, As though from fear of Kaliyug, Can still be
known from inference, Based upon prople's deeds, to reign. (182)

There in the night the moon spreads out His rays to steal the loveliness
-As though to hide his blemishes-From sleeping women's bad-like faces."
(181)

The lovely spreading tadiance Of their own bodies there dispels The inky
cloak of darkness wom By crowds of girls trysting by night, Imperilling
their bliss with lovers. (184)

यत्र नितम्बवतीनां विचलन्नयनान्तशितशरैर्ब्रणितः । शिथिलयति पथिकलोकः
स्वकलत्रसमागमोत्कण्ठाम् ॥ १८५॥

यत्र च कुलमहिलानामल्पत्वं वचसि पाणिपादे च । स्वच्छत्वमाशयेषु
व्यालोलविशालनेवे च ॥ १८६॥

पीनपयोधरभारे घनता जीवेशसहजरागे च । कुलदेवतार्चनविधौ वलिशोभा मध्यभागे च ॥
१८७॥

गम्भीरता स्वभावे चेतोभवबाणतूणनाभौ च । विस्तीर्णता नितम्बे
गुरुजनपूजानुरक्तचित्ते च ॥ १८८ ॥

हरिरणायतेक्षणानां विच्छित्तिः कोषहरणमब्जेषु । कुटिलत्वमलकपङ्गौ बालानां
कामचेष्टितं यत्र ॥ १८६ ॥

The Band's Counsel

There travellers, when they are wounded By sharpened darts of sidelong
looks From eyes of callipygran girls. Let go their eagerness to sleep With
their own wives again. (181)

There, what the well-born ladies say Is very little, not unlike Their hands
and feet, limpidity Is in their hearts, as it is seen In their wide
trembling eyes. (186)

The hurdens of their rounded breasts. Are firm, at is the unfeigned love
They have for their lives' lords. And when they worship household gods,
Abundance marks their offerings. And beauty in their folds of flesh
Distinguishes their waists. (187)

Profound are both their characters, And navels too the quivers where The
mind-born god keeps arrows hid Expansive are their derrières, And feelings
of respect to elders. (188)

There there's destruction... rather make-up On girls with wide and doe-like
eyes: There's rubbing treasurin...? or rather Calling takes place of
blossom-buds Of lotuses; there's crookedness ... In locks of hair, and
wilfulness... In little children's games. (189)

There there's constraint... upon the senses; Assassination's practised
there... Or rather: sunlight there's eclipied By Rahu, stubborn fixity Is
found...among the Sala trees, Pearl necklaces fall in the hands Of
rogues... have central pendants. (190)

भुजगाः पररन्ध्रदृशः खराड्यन्ते प्रियतमाघरा यव
सूचीव्यथानुभूतिर्नृत्याभ्यासप्रवृत्तानाम् ॥ १६१ ॥

नतवपुरप्यतिसरला मन्थरगमनापि नर्मदा यस्मिन् । गुरुजनशास्त्ररतापि
स्वभावमुग्धाङ्गनाजनता ॥ १६२॥

तस्मिन्मखशतपूतः पुरुहूत इव द्विजन्मनां प्रवरः । गुरुरिव विद्यावसति-र्वसति
स्म पुरन्दरो नाम्ना ॥ १६३॥

धर्मात्मजस्य सत्यं विपुररिपोर्विजितकुसुमचापत्वम् । हरिनाभिपङ्कजभुवो
विजितेन्द्रियतां जहास यः सततम् ॥ १६४ ॥

न्यक्कृतवृष इति शर्वे याचक इति कौस्तुभाभरणे । पीडितवसुधासुत इति कपिले न
बभूव यस्य बहुमानः ॥ १६५॥

The Band' Counsel

There those who seek out other's chinks Are inakes (who search for ideal
boles) Or rabes (eyes peeled for cleavage): Misused are... loved ones'
lower lips, The prick of conscience pangs... or rather A sense of prickling
pain is felt By those who practise dance. (191)

There hunch-hack women stand quite straight (Noi) ruen girls with lavish
curves Are upright to the last degree Even the Reva there flour slow...?
(No:) strolling women give delighe The women there are simple-natured,
Though find of scholars' treatises... Obedient to elders' ordm. (192)

There in that city lived a man, Purandara by name, the best Of brahmins,
who was purified, Like Indra, by a hundred rites Of sacrifice, he was the
seat, Just like Brháspati himself Of every branch of learning. (191)

He always seemed to laugh to scorn The truthfulness of Dharma's son," The
conquest Siva made of Love, The self-control of Brahma. (194)

He didn't show respect to Siva For treating Dharma with contempt...?
Because He subjugates bur bull Nor Vişnu, for bü begging alma... Because be
asked (a boon of Bali) Nor Kápila, for mangling Marr... Or crobing all
auspicioaren Because he persecuted Earth And (Sagar's sixty thousand) sons.
(293)

ANSKA

Parándara, the bere's father

मार्गानुसृतौ लुब्धो यः प्राणिवपुर्विनाशविमुखो ऽपि । परिहृतपरदारो ऽपि
स्वाकाङ्गितगुरुजनप्रमदः ॥ १६६ ॥

यस्यान्वये महीयसि सरसीव समस्तसत्त्वनिजवसतौ । सच्चरितजन्मभूमौ
विनिवारितकलिमलप्रसरे ॥ १६७॥

पितृतर्पणप्रसङ्गे खङ्गग्रहणं न शौर्यदर्पेरण ।

वुटनं मेखलिकानां वटुकजने नो रताजिसंमर्दे ॥ १६८ ॥

श्रुतिभेदेषु विवादो नो रिक्थविभागमन्युना जनितः । तेजस्विता हविर्भुजि न
शमैकरतेषु भूमिदेवेषु ॥ १६६ ॥

जरतामेव स्खलनं जपतामेवाधरस्फुरणम् ।

यजतामेव समिधुचि-

रेणाजिन एव कृष्णसम्पर्कः ॥ २०० ॥

The Band's Caunl

Although he'd turned from killing, yet He was a bunting mania....! (But no,
that means:) be andently Pursued the Path (of Rightesuinen); Though he
avoided others" brides, He coveted this guras (But no, that rather means:)
be years To carry out his elders' wobes. (196)

In his great line, the home of all Good qualities, just as a lake Is home
to every kind of catare, The birthplace of the virtuous, From which the
spreading stains of Kali Had been entirely washed away. The scimitar is
taken up But net frem pride in maslines.... (Rathet:) rhinocere's fler, To
satisfy the antenors, And there, when little girdles split, It's only
schoolboys who're involved, Not tusslers on love's battlefield. (197-198)

In it disputes arise shout The various kinds of Vedic texts, And not from
tage that's been inspired By carving up inheritance, And filetiness is
found... in fires Of sacrifice and not among Pacific brahmin folk (199)

These stumbling slips... afflict the aged, There trembling lower lips are
seen.... On those reciting scriptural texts: There cagerness for aging....
(Or should that be) for kindling fuel Is patent when they sacrifice: A
family which blackness touches Only because they sit (in rites) Upon the
black-buck's hide. (200)

तस्याभूत्सकलकलोद्भासितपच्चद्वयस्य सुत्त एकः । नाम्ना सुन्दरसेनः कच इव

The Basod's Counsel

99

His lineage radiantly shone Both on the loom and distaff side, For skill in
every art, just as Brhaspati (or Jupiter) Is radiant in all its digits,
Illuminating both month-halves; He had an only son whose name Was
Súndaraséna, great as Kaca (The offspring of Brhaspati), (201)

Sundara described

Whom the creator made to be Another god of Love, in flesh, To pleasure
Pleasure (Kama's wife) Hearing of Love reduced to ash By fire from
Pasupati's eye. (203)

No need to speak of other dames Of noble birth, for even she, Arundhati,
the sage's wife, Could scarce have kept her conduct pure When once she'd
glimpsed his heauty. (203)

His spacious breast shone with the glow Of slabs of gold, on seeing which,
Lakumi herself supposed she might For all that while have been ill-placed.
Upon the chest of Visnu. (204)

"But how could he appear like that. Unless he's made of lunar digits! And
yet, if that is how he's made, Then why does he torment (not cool)?" So
thinking, as they gazed at him, A crowd of women mad with love Could never
quite make up their minds. (205)

He took his brightness from the moon, His steadfastness from mountain
peaks: From clouds he took his loftiness: And from the ocean, depth. (206)

The Bawd's Counsel

Of modesty he was the home, Of cleverness the dwelling place, Of stamina
the place of rest, A storehouse of most pleasing words, A mansion of good
conduct. (207)

For women he was Käma, while On virtuous men-like lily-ponds-He acted as
the moon; For qualities a testing stone; For travellers along the way A
tree beside the road. (208)

He loved to hobnob with the wise, And was a touchstone for what's good In
poetry and story-books. To suppliants he was a tree That satisfies one's
every wish; A sporting ground for Laksmi's play. (309)

He had a friend called Günapälir, Who had stood every test of faith And
shared an ocean to his moon-His waxings and his wanings. (210)

Standing one day apart with him He heard, as chance befell, this verse,
Being declaimed by somebody To whom it happened to occur. (an)

"People who don't acquaint themselves With dress and habits, ways of speech
In other lands, and won't approach Preceptors there, truly such "men" Are
oxen without horns. (211)

Then Sundara, on hearing this, Addressed that best of friends and said: "O
Gúnapalita, how fine The verse this man has sung!" (213)

The Band's Counsel

"It's one who travels all the earth That's bounded by the ocean's shores,
Who learns how virtuous people act, How rogues behave, the various whims
Prople can have, the sport of wits; The two-edged speech of courtesans, The
hidden meanings gurus teach, Roues' ways, and shenanigans With which a
rogue can double cross. (214-215)

"So let us quit this tiny scrap Of pleasure had by staying here At home:
please steel yourself, my friend, To leave with me on a grand tour The
fruits of which are multifold." (216)

After he'd spoken thus and was Agog to hear his friend's reply, His boon
companion, who, it seemed, Was somewhat ill at ease, replied To Sundarasena
thus: (212)

"It is embarrassing for friends Like me to hear repeated pleas (I should at
once now acquiesce But listen, let me spell things out About the
tribulations which A traveller may come upon. (118)

"His body wrapped in ragged clothes, His strength used up with weariness
From passing over lengthy roads, All dirty grey with layers of dust, Upon
the close of day, he'll yeam. To find some place to rest. (219)

O Mother, sister, have compassion! Don't be so heartless in this way! Your
sons and brothers too go out On business from their homes. (330)

The Band Counsel

"'D'you think we'd trash the house and then Set off tomorrow with the dawnf
Only a good man's house like this Is one where travellers can rest As if in
their own homes. (223)

* Now we will have to spend the night Somehow, oh mother, in your house The
sun has set, so tell us please Where cise should we go now!" (222)

"The wretched fellow stands before The door of every house and begs With
many an abject speech like these, To hear cach housewife spurn him thus:
(223)

"My husband isn't here just now. Why do you make this useless noise! Go to
the temple! Look at him! Even when told he doesn't budge. How very stubborn
is this man!" (234)

"Suppose some other householder On being asked repeatedly Should show him
to a nook inside A crumbling shed, and say to him Contemptuously, "Here,
sleep there!", His wife will stand there, on an on, Letting a starlit watch
elapse, And quarrel with her husband thus 'This man we do not know at all;
Why did you let him stay?" (225-226)

"And women from the next-door house Will come and make as if to ask For

happened that a person sang. In course of talk, this Giti verse. (231)

"For men who have entirely set Their hearts upon a single goal, The temple
is their choicest home The ground's a bed of luxury, And tasteless food is
nectar." (aya)

On heating this, Purandar's son Replied, delighted, to his friend, "The
feelings harboured in my heart, Have been revealed by this good man. So
come now, let's set off." (233)

308

दामनीमतम्

अथ सहचरद्वितीयः क्लेशसमुद्रावतरणकृतचित्तः । निरगात्सुन्दरसेनः
कुसुमपुरादविदितः पित्वा ॥ २३४ ॥

पश्यन् विदग्धगोष्ठीरभ्यस्यन्नायुधानि सकलानि ।
शास्त्रार्थानवगच्छन्विलोकयन्कौतुकानि विविधानि ॥ २३५ ॥

जानन् पवछेदनमालेख्यं सिक्थपुस्तकर्माणि । नृत्यं गीतोपचितं
तन्वीमुरजादिवाद्यभेदांश्व ॥ २३६ ॥

बुध्यन्वश्वकभङ्गीविटकुलटानर्मवककथितानि । बभ्राम सुहृत्सहितः सुन्दरसेनो
महीमखिलाम् ॥ २३७॥

अथ विदितसकलशास्त्रो विज्ञाताशेषजनसमाचरणः । निजगृहगमनाकाली स शिलोच्चयमर्बुदं
प्राप ॥ २३८ ॥

The Bawd's Counrel

Then, with his friend in tow, resolved To plunge himself in seas of
troubles, Sundara quit the "Flowery Town" (A soubriquet of Patna then)
Without his father knowing. (214)

Observing pandits" seminars, And practising dexterity With weaponry of
various sorts, Grasping the sense of learned works, And viewing curious
spectacles Of miscellaneous kinds; (215)

Aquiring skill in cutting leaves, In representing likenesses, And modelling
in wax and clay, In dancing and in singing too, And divers sorts of
instruments-Percussion, strings and more, (236)

Learning the crooked ways of cheats, The badinage and japery Of debauchees
and courtesans, Súndaraséna, with his friend, Wandered the whole wide
world. (257)

With every branch of knowledge learned, Familiar with the ways and wonts Of
every sort of company, Intent upon returning home, He reached one day Mount
Abu. (238)

Sensing a special eagerness In Sundara to see the top Then Gúnapălit spoke
to him: "Just contemplate this regal mountain! (239)

"It's just as if Himalaya, To show compassion on mankind, Had set his
mountain son down here, With cool and clear streams flowing down His
slopes, here in the desert. (240)

The years rall by while they're abroad

The rus friend reach Mount Arbuda

The Band's Coume

"He carries Sambhu's heavenliness (Or so my clever puns reveal): Moonstones
lie scattered on his peak-The mean is his bright diadem, On slopes of his
dwell anchorites Whose only sustenance is air Around his wrists the
serpents twine, In him are caves (but if you read The same word otherwise,
it means:) Skanda (his son) is next to bim Celestial beings live on him
He's praised by sorcerers who know His spells and incantation. (341)

"Here, I suppose, some artless girls Must wish to pluck the stars at night.
Feeling quite sure that they must be Just blossom on the tops of trees.
(242) "How marvellous that even these, The Seven Sages, stay so close!" But
after all, who isn't lured By eminence found in the great! (243) "No doubt
the World's Creator saw The solar horses' heavenly path To be without a
solid base, And set the mountain here to rest them. (244)

"Those herbs which have recourse to him Achieve a closeness to the moon
(Their tutelary genius): In general, propinquity To mighty individuals
Demands an intermediary To intercede for us. (245)

"He sprays forth drops in quantities From waterfalls as though to splash
The elephants that guard the quarters, Tired from holding up the earth: For
those who share a single task Are one another's friends. (246)

The Band's Counsel

"Journeys to many places new It seems to me are just a bore For all those
folk who never view The plateau of Mount Abu, which Excels the heavenly
plain." (255)

Now hearing this, our hero spoke: "How true the words this great man sings!
Let's climb, my friend, and see ourselves This pleasing mountain top." (216)

Then he climbed up that best of mountains And, wandering, gazed with wonder
at Its various temples, tanks and gardens, And at its lakes and streams.
(app)

And as he roamed that mountain-top Where, in the woodland pergolas, The
ground was strewn about with flowers, He saw a girl in girlish play,

Together with her confidante, -A lightning streak without its cloud, Like
moonlight severed from the moon, (Blue water lilies' bosom friend), Like
Rati tom apart from Kama, Like Sti slipped down from Vişnu's breast, The
summit of the Maker's craft, Of all created things the best. Epitome of
loveliness,

The mind-born god's triumphant bow, A wealth of flowers in full bloom, A
peerless swan upon love's river," A creeper full of grace-like buds, An
arrow that can cleave apart The armour that ascetics wear: Their mystic
concentration. (258-261)

The Band's Counsel

He, as he looked upon the girl, Was pierced at once by Käma's shafts, And,
overwhelmed with wonderment, For a long while he pondered thus: (262)

"Whete did the Maker of the World Acquire such astounding skill That even
contradicting things Are brought by him together here In this one locus!
For: (afit)

"Her form is lovely yet. "lacks armı?" (No rather) she's without a fault:
(Unless it means 'the knows no night', And yet she's gorgeous for her
bright And twinkling star?... (no rather) pupili. Her voice transcends the
veena's thram And yet her lotus-mouth can find Να πατάς... (No) cannot be
described. (264)

""She shows berself in state of war And yet ber beauty sues for peace?" Her
body's shape is clear to see And all her joints and ligatures Are fashioned
with especial grace. She's white as autumn moonlight rays And yet she's
thronged with beavy clouds?"... (Not) well-endowed with rising breasts.
(265)

""She advocates the Buddhist way And yet she bas a penchant ton For texts
of all the Vedic schools?" Delightful is the way the walks; Her way of
standing, too, delights; She has extremely ample hips: Yet wasted is her
shapelinen? Bodiless Kama lends ber grace." (166)

दामोदरनीमतम्

आविर्भवदनुरागे तस्मिन्नथ वलितलोचना सहसा । सापि बभूव मृगाक्षी हस्तगता
कुसुमचापस्य ॥ २६७ ॥

तरुमूलमाश्रिताया विस्मृतसकलान्यकर्मणः सपदि । तस्या गात्वलतायामङ्कुरितं
सात्त्विकैर्भावैः ॥ २६८ ॥

सैवोपवनसमृद्धिस्तस्मिन्नेव चक्षाणे स्मरं स्मृत्वा । तां व्यथयितुमारेभे
प्रभोहिं कृत्यं करोति खलु सर्वः ॥ २६६ ॥

गावसरसेन्धनेभ्यः प्रस्वेदजलं विनिर्ययौ तस्याः । अन्तज्वलितमनोभवहव्यभुजा
दह्यमानेभ्यः ॥ २७० ॥

कुसुमशरजालपतिता मुहुर्मुहुर्विदधती विवृत्तानि । अनिमेषं पश्यन्ती
मत्स्यवधूमनुचकार सा तन्वी ॥ २७१ ॥

स्तब्धतनुं सोत्कम्पां पुलकवतीं स्वेदिनीं सनिःश्वासाम् । विदधे तामसमशरः
क्रीडति हि शठो विशिष्टमासाद्य ॥ २७२ ॥

उच्ठासैरुल्लसनं कुचयुगले सौष्ठवं विलासानाम् । अभिलषितेन प्रेम्णा
स्निग्धत्वं चक्षुषोर्मनोहारि ॥ २७३ ॥

The Baud's Counsel

Now as his love grew manifest, Quite suddenly that doe-eyed girl With
trembling pupils found herself A toy in Kämadeva's hands. (267)

She leant herself against a bole, Ar once all other tasks forgotten, And on
her slender body grew The signs of genuine emotion (She stiffened,
sweltered, stuttered, thrilled, Trembled and paled and wept and swooned).
(268)

Remembering Kämadeva then The sylvan beauty of that place Began at once to
plague her too! For everybody, as we know, Steps up in service of the
strong. (169)

Just as from sappy kindling, sweat Steamed forth from all her limbs.
Consumed with flames from fire of love That raged inside her frame. (270)

Round and about she turned and turned And with unblinking eyes she stared:
Caught in love's net, that slender girl Behaved just like a female fish.
(271)

Love made her body freeze, then tremble, Now caused her hairs to stand on
end, Now made her sweat, now made her sigh: A rogue who's found a fitting
prey Will play with it a while. (272)

Causing her breasts to heave with sighs, Making her movements truly
charming By filling them with eagerness Lending her eyes a lovely glint, Of
moistness from affection, (273)

दामोदर तम्

अनुरक्तत्या वदनरुचिं वचसि च गमने च साध्वसस् तस्या मदनः कुर्वन्नुपनिन्ये
चारुतामधिकाम् ॥ २७४॥

पार्श्वगते ऽपि प्रेयसि कामशरासारताड्यमानापि । न शशाक साभिधातुं चित्तगतं
प्रणयभङ्गतो भीता ॥ २७५ ॥

अथ विदितचित्तवृत्तिः सक्तदृशं प्रियतमे समाकृष्य । मदनेन दह्यमानां
विहसितविशदं जगाद तामाली ॥ २७६ ॥

अयि हारलते संहर हरहुङ्कृतिदग्धदेहसंक्षोभम् । सद्भावजानुरक्तिर्न हि पण्यं
पण्यनारीणाम् ॥ २७७ ॥

अवधीरय धनविकलं कुरु गौरवमकृशसम्पदः पुंसः । अस्मादृशां हि मुग्धे धनसिद्धौ
रूपनिर्माणम् ॥ २७८ ॥

अभिरामे ऽभिनिवेशं विदधाना विभवलाभनिरपेक्षा । उपहस्यसे सुमध्ये
विदग्धवाराङ्गनावारैः ॥ २७६ ॥

येषां श्लाध्यं यौवनमभिमुखतामुपगतो विधिर्येषाम् । फलितं येषां सुकृतं
जीवितसुखिताथिता येषाम् ॥ २८० ॥

The Baud's Couniel

Prompting her face to flush with passion, And causing her to trip and
stumble. Both when she spoke and tried to walk, From trepidation-thus did
Love Bring loveliness to its perfection. (274)

Though she was right beside her love And yet was being showered with darts
Dispatched by Love, she couldn't speak To put her thoughts in words, afraid
That then their love might shatter. (275)

Now at this point her confidante, Knowing what passed within her mind Drew
her aside and spoke to her With radiant smile, while she just stared,
Burning with love, at him. (276)

Saiiprabba, her confidante, Attemptr to guide Haralata

"Häralara, ah please restrain This outburst caused by him whose form Was
burnt to ash by Siva's rage. True love, with feelings truly felt, Is no
commodity at all For girls who sell themselves. (277)

"Despise the paupers! Only those With pockets full, deserve respect! My
simple girl, for those like us, Beauty's for making money. (278)

"Let yourself fall for handsome looks, Without an eye for all the wealth
You stand to gain, and soon you'll find, Yourself to be a laughing stock
Among the clever courtesans, O slender-waisted friend! (279)

"Those men whose youth is at its peak, Upon whom fortune starts to smile,
Whose past good deeds now bear their fruit, Who wish for happiness in
life-(180)

ते ऽवश्यं स्वयमेव त्वामनुबन्नन्ति मदनशरभिन्नाः । न हि मधुलेहाः सुन्दरि

इति गदितवतीमालीं कामशरासारभिन्नसर्वाङ्गी । अव्यक्तस्खलिताच्तरमूचे कृच्छ्रेण
हारलता ॥ २८२॥

सखि कुरु तावद्यत्वं बहुमतमतिवेदनाप्रतीकारे । कोडीकृता विपत्त्या न

The Bawd's Counsel

*-Such men, being pierced by Kama's shafts, Will surely stick to you
themselves. For bees, my beauty, aren't pursued By flowering mango sprays."
(281)

Thus spoke her friend. Hätalată, Her body pierced in every part By floods
of Kämadeva's shafts, Brought out (but only just) these soft And stumbling
syllables. (282)

"My friend, do you now please attempt, To counteract these dreadful pains
Of Love! For when some hapless prey Is leapt on by Calamity She cannot heed
advice. (283)

Haralata her belp.

"Loving a man who's not one's own; A gentle breeze, a garden full Of
fragrant flowers-all these things Combine, it's clear, only for girls Whose
life-force is now spent." (184)

On seeing that the toxic shock Of snake-like Love had now convulsed The
body of her friend with bane, Sášiprabha aproached and bowed Before
Purandar's son and spoke: (281)

"Even if shame for what I am, A prostitute, arrests my tongue, I have to
speak: for when a friend Is found to be in dire straits, One does not think
of etiquette. (286)

"Through all this world, few are the men Of noble birth who spring to mind
As refuges for those confused In desperate adversity. (287)

दग्धमदनस्य ॥ २८८ ॥

रोमोद्गमसंनहनं भित्त्वान्तविग्रहं परापतिताः । तस्या

The Baod's Counel

"Right from the very moment when You came in sight of my friend here, She's
fallen utterly in theall To cursed Kamadeva. (288)

"Her hairs stood up to form stockades, But darts released from Kama's how

without restraint, Cause torment to my friend. (291)

And jubilantly pass the time In taking turns about the place. (292)

Can't leave the flowers that grace her locks: For even in adversity

It's hard to cast desires aside. (293)

"So Gunapalit, let's approach Haralata, whose wandering eyes Rival those of
a black-buck fawn, Unsettled by the Love-God's shafts, And let's revive
her! What's the need Of copious excogitation!" (330)

दामोदरगुप्तविरचित

केसरया क्षणदत्तं कृत्वांशुकमुपरि कामिजालस्य । स्तब्धग्रीवं भ्रमतश्चन्द्रोदय
पश्य माहात्म्यम् ॥ ३४६॥

कौमारकं विधातुं वाञ्छसि किल रमण मदनसेनायाः । इच्छामि कि तु तस्या मात्रातीव
प्रसारितं वदनम् ॥ ३५० ॥

विभ्रम कियतस्तपसः फलमेतद्यदुपभुज्यते मदिरा । स्वकरेण पीतशेषा
मदघूर्णितदेवदत्तया दत्ता ॥ ३५१॥

कुवलयमालानिलयो लीलोदय किमिति सम्प्रति त्यक्तः । कि विदधामस्तस्मिन्
भ्रातर्दास्या विना मूल्यम् ॥ ३५२ ॥

मुषिताशेषविभूतेरिन्दीवरकस्य यामिनी याति । संवाहयतः सम्प्रति मञ्जीरक
तिलकमञ्जरीचरणी ॥ ३५३ ॥

अद्यापि बालभावं निखिलं न जहाति मदनिका तदपि । प्रौढिन्ना मकरन्दक सकला ललना
अधः कुरुते ॥ ३५४ ॥

कुब्जे गत्वा वक्ष्यसि तं निर्दयचित्तनर्तनाचार्यम् । हारा सुकुमारतनुः किमिति
श्रममद्य कारिता भवता ॥ ३५५॥

दामोदरगुशविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

आताम्रतां समुपगतमुच्छूनं करतलं तव सुललिते । मा पुनरतिचिरमेवं प्रविधास्यसि
कन्दुकक्रीडाम् ॥ ३६२ ॥

अभिराम डोम्ब भाटी प्रथममियं गृह्यते समुत्पन्ने । नेहे तु कुसुमदेव्यास्त्वं
प्रभवसि जीवितस्यापि ॥ ३६३॥

किं चिद्यथाभिमतम् ॥ ३६४॥

न परमदाता मातः सूनुरसौ नगररोटभट्टस्य । निर्लज्जः शठवृत्तिः पुनः
पुनर्वार्यमाणो ऽपि ॥ ३६५॥

क्षपयति वसनानि सदा हठेन सकलानि सुरतसेनायाः । न ददात्येकामूर्णामुरणः परमत्ति
कर्पाशम् ॥ ३६६॥

भगिनि न मुञ्चति वेश्म चक्षणमपि मे कपटराजपुत्रो ऽसौ । भग्नो ऽन्यनरावसरी
नग्नेनाधिष्ठितं यथा तीर्थम् ॥ ३६७॥

The Baud's Counsel

These are the kinds of words he heard, Coming from libertines and bawds,
While he observed the brothel street, And went inside his lover's house.
(168)

Haralată respectfully Received him when he'd come imide, Dragged there, it
seemed, by powerful longing, Bathed in her gentle gaze's rays. (369)

Once he was seated suitably, Her friend, in greeting, bowed her head, And
with a deferential mien Addressed these words to Sundara At this propitious
juncture: (320)

457

"Happy to look upon are you! What need of many words to tell The misery of
pangs of love! Before you stands Haralatá Her life reposes in your hands.
(171)

Delightful for your bond of love That's come unplanned, completely frec
From problema posed by sundry duties. (372)

"When you make love, may every bout Be marked by lust that never stints,
And merciless, and shorn of shame, And multifold in execution, With passion
ever mounting higher." (375)

Delivering her blessings thus, The friend withdrew inside the house, Then
Love grew mighty in their limbs, And firm trust made its relish pure. (374)

The Bawd's Counsel

Their amorous exchange began (180d) As suited their immoderate love, As
fitted their untried affection, Delicious in their salad days, The climax
of a human life: (171)

Immodesty was there a virtue, And lewd behaviour quite polite, A lack of
inhibition fine, Experimenting showed respect; (376)

Tugging the hair was only grace, A blow a service; bites gave joy; The
scratch of nails was happiness: A vice-like body-squeeze was bliss; (377)

Where kissing thirsts for swallowing: And each one rubs at though to crush
The other's limbs, and tightly hugs As though to blend inside the other;
(178)

As though produced by multitudes Of Kamas, brightened by a host Of
fervencies, and reinforced By many fondnesses, and brought To blossom by a
plethora Of auras of romance: (379)

In which timidity would pain, Rigidity was not allowed, Discrimination
criminal: And shyness was a sin. (180)

159

Haralata

and Sundara Relish the joys of mutual love.

The Bawd's Counsel

Right at the first their love caught fire Ar once, and once it had matured,
Who could describe its unique states! (381)

Because of their spontaneous love, The punditry that catalogues The sundry
lists of sexual poses Described in treatises of love Seemed lifeless for
the youthful pair And so anathema for them. (382)

With amatory battle joined And raging 'twixt the two of them, Nothing was
left that "can't be done", Or "can't be said", or "needs some thought", Or
"must be hid", or "can't be borne". (383)

The strings of varied flattering tropes The slender girl had learned by
rote For use in amorous deploy Het strong, impulsive love distorted.
Reducing them to gibberish. (384)

Who could enumerate their moves, Ignited each with heartfelt passion And
taught by Kama as their guide When they left text-book sex behind (38)

This tender, creeper-slender girl Could not be said to not be frightened
When straddled by that sturdy man, Yet found herself in ecstasy. Mighty
indeed is Käma's power! (386)

अविदग्धः श्रमकठिनो दुर्लभयोषिद्यु‌वा जडो विप्रः । अपमृत्युरपक्रान्तः

नेच्छाविरतिः क्षरणमपि न च शक्तिर्वस्तुशून्यरतियवैः । केवलमलमद्याहं कदथिता
वृद्धपुरुषेण ॥ ३६४ ॥

मद्यवशादभियोक्तरि मृतकल्पे तल्पभागमन्नायाः । अनिरोधितनिद्रायाः सुखेन मे
यामिनी याता ॥ ३६५॥

सुकुमारसम्प्रयोगः पेशलवचनः सवक्रपरिहासः । शकुनवशेनोपनतो मम सखि रमरणो
मनोहराकारः ॥ ३६६ ॥

पर्यङ्कान्तनिलीनः पराङ्मुखो मुक्तमन्दनिःश्वासः । मच्चोदनया सुतरां निष्पन्दः
स्वेदसलिलसंसिक्तः ॥ ३६७॥

पर्यस्तमितानङ्गो व्यपगतनिद्रः क्षपाक्षयाकाङ्गी । ग्रामोषितः प्रहीरणो

"A toothmark's on your lower lip. A full maxilla's marked your throat, As

555

After he'd heard such words as these, From prostitutes at break of day
Who'd finished with the night's exertions. He too set out to think about
His customary obligations. (404)

A love well-formed that grew and grew Possessed his mind and while he lived
The joys of youth along with her, A year, and then a half, went past. (405)

Then came a day when Súndara, While strolling on the garden testace With
Gunapalit, hand in hand, Exchanging confidential words Discerned a
hastening messenger Aproaching them from far away, Attired in motley bits
of cloth Woven from rough and chunky threads, Furnished with blanker, gourd
and paan, All fastened round the end of a stick, His body filthy, feet
split-beeled, Ingrained with grime, in broken shoes. (406-408)

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं कुट्टमीमतम्

शुश्रूषरणमेव गुरोः परिशीलितमचलचेतसा सततम् । कुटिलमतयो भुजिष्याः कथं
त्वयाराधिता निपुरणम् ॥ ४२१ ॥

आम्नायपाठ एव स्फुटतरपदसौष्ठवं तव ख्यातम् । प्रकुपितवेश्यानुनये क्व शिक्षितं
वचनचातुर्यम् ॥ ४२२॥

अथवा कि क्रियते ऽस्मिन्नवदातकुले ऽपि लब्धजन्मानः । सदसंस्तुता भवन्ति
प्रागुपचितकर्मदोषेण ॥ ४२३॥

त्वयि विनिवेश्य कुटुम्बं परलोकहितार्जनैकनिहितात्मा । स्थास्यामीति
समीहितमनु‌दिवसं तद्विसंवदितम् ॥ ४२४ ॥

इत्यवधृतलेखार्थे सुन्दरसेने विधेयसम्मूढे । आर्यामगायदन्यः स्वावसरे
गीतिपरिकरिताम् ॥ ४२५ ॥

विषयतिमिरावृताच्क्ष्णामवटे पततामदृष्टमार्गाणाम् । पुंसां
गुरुजनबचनद्रव्यशलाकाञ्जनं शरणम् ॥ ४२६ ॥

The Bawd's Counsel

"It was obedience you learnt To cultivate towards your guru With an
unwav'ring mind, how, then, Do you now skilfully pay court To
twisted-minded filler de joie! (421)

"Your excellence in elocution When chanting Veda was renowned, Where did
you learn your eloquence For soothing slighted courtesans (422)

"In any case, what can be done! There are some people who, though born In
this pure family, become Objects of censure to the good Through fault of
karma earned before. (433)

"Each day I wish that I might first Entrust the family to you And then
wholly devote myself To storing, for the next world, merit, And this desire
of mine you thwart." (424)

The letter's purport Såndara Had grasped, but then he found himself Bemused
about what course to take, When someone sang an Arya verse, Coupled with
one in Giti form, That suited his own circumstance.(425)

"The only hope for those whose eyes Are veiled behind the cataracts That
are the pleasures of the senses And who, not seeing the way ahead, Trip and
fall headlong into pits Is wisdom that the elders teach Applied like
eye-salve from a stylus. (426)

176

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं कुट्टनीमान्

उद्वेजयति तदात्वे सुखसम्पत्तिं करोति परिणामे । कटुकौषधप्रयोगो
गुरुनिगदितकार्यनिष्वरं च वचः ॥ ४२७ ॥

लब्ध्वाथ वचोवसरं मित्रमवादीत्पुरन्दरापत्यम् । पुनरपि न हि खिद्यन्ते
प्रियजनहितभाषणे सन्तः ॥ ४२८ ॥

अगणितसहचरवचसो दुर्व्यसनमहाब्धिमग्नवपुषस्ते । मन्युव्यथितस्य पितुर्यदि
परमवलम्बनं वचनम् ॥ ४२६ ॥

निजवंशदीपभूतः कृतचरितालंकृतो महासत्त्वः । सुन्दर सम्प्रति तातः स्पृष्टो
दृष्युवदोषेण ॥ ४३० ॥

पुत्राभावः श्रेयान् दुःसुतता पुत्रिणः कुलीनस्य । अन्तस्तापयति भृशं
सच्चरितकथाप्रसङ्गेषु ॥ ४३१॥

सांव्यवहारिक एव प्रायो लोके गुणोन्नता नियताः । येन तु सुतेन जननी
बन्ध्यात्वं श्लाघते स पापीयान् ॥ ४३२ ॥

विफलं शास्त्रज्ञानं गुरुगृहसेवापि नोपकाराय । विषयवशीकृतमनसो न्याय्यं
पन्थानमुत्सृजतः ॥ ४३३ ॥

The Bated's Counsel

"To swallow bitter medicine Or swallow counsel from one's elders That's
difficult to act upon These two will at the time cause pain, But ultimately
happiness." (427)

Grasping the moment then to speak His friend addressed Purandar's son Yet
one more time, for worthy men Will spare no pains in speaking out On what
will benefit their friends. (428)

"If one more rope's thrown out for you, Who've failed to heed your friends'
advice, Whose limbs now feel the ocean's pull, Plunged in a sea of
turpitude, Then here it is your father's words While he is hopping mad with
rage. (429)

"Lamp of his lineage, your sire, Famed for his feats of Kita vintage, A
noble soul, is tainted now By having such a wastrel son. (430)

"Better to have no son at all! Having a rotten son torments A well-born
father's heart deep down, When people talk of upright men. (431)

"Among the people that we meet From day to day, the virtuous Are generally
rather few. But if a son's enough to cause A mother's wish for barrenness
He must indeed be very bad. (434)

"For one who leaves the righteous path, His mind in thrall to sensual
treats, No learning is of any use, And serving in his guru's house Will
also have no point at all. (433)

178

दामोदरगुमविरचितं

जीवन्नेव मृतो ऽसौ यस्य जनो वीच्क्ष्य वदनमन्योन्यम् । कृतमुखभङ्गो
दूरात्करोति निर्देशमङ्गल्या ॥ ४३४॥

नो परिहर्तुं विषयाः शक्याः सत्यं तथापि निपुणधियः । अभिधेयतां न
गच्छन्त्यपवादविशेषिताभिधानस्य ॥ ४३५॥

गुरुपरिचर्या जाया कुलोद्गता स्रिग्धबन्धुसम्पर्कः । ब्राह्मे कर्मणि
सक्तिर्लोकद्वयसाधनं सुधियाम् ॥ ४३६॥

सुलभा तस्य विभूतिस्तस्य गुणा यान्ति जगति विस्तारम् । बहु मनुते तं
सुजनस्तस्मै स्पृहयन्ति बान्धवाः सततम् ॥ ४३७ ॥

नासादयति स एकः सत्सेवितमार्गतः परिस्खलनम् । मण्डयति सो ऽन्ववायं स निवासः
शर्मणामशेषाणाम् ॥ ४३८ ॥

स भवति विनयाधारो युक्तायुक्ते विवेकिता तस्य । वृद्धोपदेशवाचः श्रवणोदरतर्पणं
सदा यस्य ॥ ४३१॥

प्राक्तनकर्मविपाकः चुद्रासु शरीरिणां यदासक्तिः । आयतनं तु सुखानां
संसारभुवां कुलोद्गता रामा ॥ ४४० ॥
