The Batod's Counsel

"Although alive, he is as dead Whom people point their fingers at While
grimacing conspiringly On seeing his face from far away. (434)

"The senses' objects can't, it's true, Be wholly shunned, but all the same
People of sense don't make themselves The butt of denigrating slurs. (435)

"Attending to one's guru's needs, A marriage to a well-born wife, The
company of caring friends, Observing Vedic rituals -These are the means for
prudent men For triumph in the hete and now, And in the world hereafter.
(436)

"Success comes easily for him, His virtues spread thoughout the world, Good
people hold him in esteem And friends ate ever keen to see him; Only a man
like that won't falter, Missing the path the good have trod He ornaments
his lineage. Of all that's joyful, he's a store, Of modesty, a reservoir,
And he alone discriminates With acumen what's right or wrong -A man whose
elders' guiding words Are ever nectar to his ears. (437-439)

"It's by the force of previous deeds That men become attached to whores.
But happiness in worldly life Is found beside a well-bom wife. (440)

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं सुट्ट

निविस्ये निर्विस्या मुदिते मुदिता समाकुलाकुलिते । प्रतिबिम्बसमा कान्ता
संक्रुद्धे केवलं भीता ॥ ४४१॥

यावद्वाञ्छितसुरतव्यायामसहाविरुद्धसंभाषा । चित्तानुवृत्तिकुशला पुण्यवतामेव
जायते जाया ॥ ४४२॥

सद्भावप्रेमरसं वलयावलिशब्दशङ्किता निभृतम् ।
विदधानाङ्गसमर्पणमुन्मीलितकुसुमसायकाकूता ॥ ४४३॥

हा हा किमुद्धतत्वं श्रोष्यति कश्विद्भतवप स्वैरम् । निकटे परिवारजनो विस्मृत
एव स्मरातुरस्य तव ॥ ४४४ ॥

इति हुंकृतिसंवलितैरायासनिवेदितार्थपदवाक्यैः । द्विगुणीकरोति कुलजा
नायककर्माणि मोहनप्रसरे ॥ ४४५॥

इत्थमुदीरितवाचं सुहृदमवोचत्पुरन्दरस्य सुतः । समुपस्थितजीवसमा-वियोगभयकम्पितो
वचनम् ॥ ४४६ ॥

The Bawd's Counnl

"Sad when she feels that he is sad, Delighted when he shows delight,
Distressed when he is in distress, His cherished wife's his looking-glass,
Except she's scared when he is angry. (443)

"She will endure his making love For just as long as he desires; She won't
oppose him when they talk, She'll quickly grasp the way he thinks-A bride
like that falls to the lot Of men who've stored up heaps of merit. (442)

"When, in a way that shows her love Is genuine, she manifests An eagerness
for Kama's sports And gently offers up her limbs, Made anxious when her
bracelets clink-(443)

"Oh dear! What is this rashness? Slow.... Someone will hear, you shameless
man! Have you forgot the servants here Because you're feverish with love!"
(444)

-Mixing such words and sentences, Articulated with some strain, With moans
and sighs, the well-bom wife Redoubles all her husband does As making love
follows its course." (445)

After his friend had spoken thus, Purandar's son, who quaked in fear,
Caused by the threat that now approached Of separation from the one Who was
as dear as life to him, Said to his friend these words: (446)

182

दामोदरगुमविरचितं सुनीमवम्

तातादेशे ऽलङ्घये हारलताविरहपावके तीव्रे । विधिवशवर्तिनि मरणे नो विद्यः
कार्यपरिणामम् ॥ ४४७ ॥

अनपेक्षितधनलाभां नेहैकनिबद्धमानसां दयिताम् । दैवाकृष्टो मुञ्चति घटितो वा
लोहवज्रकणिकाभिः ॥ ४४८ ॥

अथ कृतगमनविनिश्चितिरभिमतरामां चकार विदितार्थाम् । सापि तमनुवब्राज
प्रस्तुतयात्त्रं शुचाकुलिता ॥ ४४६ ॥

आसाद्य वटस्य तलं बाष्पपयः कणचिताक्षिपक्ष्माग्राम् । विनितचरणविहारो
हारलतामभिदधाति स्म ॥ ४५० ॥

आ चीरवतो वृक्षा-दा सलिलाद्वा प्रिये प्रियं यान्तम् । अनुयायादिति वचनं तेन
त्वमितो निवर्तस्व ॥ ४५१ ॥

कि कुर्मी दैवहताः प्रभवति यस्मिन् कृशोदरि प्रसभम् । प्रेमग्रन्थिच्छेत्ता
गुरुशासनसायको निरावरणः ॥ ४५२ ॥

412c-4545 are inserted on the margin in N.

The Baud's Counsel

"A father's orders can't be crossed, Yet if I quit Häralată, I shall be
scorched in seating flames; Death, it lies in the lap of fate -All this
being so, I cannot tell How what I have to do will end. (447)

"Only a person dragged by fate, Or somehow wrought of adamant And iron dust
could leave behind A girl whose heart's attached to him For love and
nothing else besides, Expecting no financial gain." (448)

When he'd made up his mind to go He told his darling of his plans And she,
distracted out of grief, Walked on behind him as he left Upon the journey
he'd announced. (449)

181

Reaching a banyan's roots, he checked His feet from walking further on And
then addressed Haralată, As teardrops thronged her lashes' tips. (450)

"They say, my love, one should conduct Departing dear ones up to where
There's water, or a ficus tree; And so you should turn back from here. (451)

"What can we do, my slender girl! We're victims of the blows of fate:
Subduing us with violence, The arrow of a father's dictate, Resistless,
cuts our knot of love." (452)

They part beneath a banyan tree.

184

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं

न द्रविणचयप्राप्तिर्नेकाश्रयपरिचयो न चाटुगुणः । न स्वामिसमादेशो
नाकारविलोभनं न च ख्यातिः ॥ ४५३ ॥

हेतुस्तव प्रवृत्तेरस्मासु तथापि दैववशात् । ईदृक्को ऽप्यनुबन्धो यस्य विपाको
ऽप्रतीकारः ॥ ४५४॥

परुषं यदभिहितासि प्रणयरुषा शङ्कितं च नर्मणि वा। सुदति न तत्स्मरणीयं
दुर्भाषणकीर्तनोद्धाते ॥ ४५५॥

तव हृदये हृदयमिदं विन्यस्तं न्यासपालनं कष्टम् । यत्वात्तथा विधेयं
स्थानभ्रंशो यथा न स्यात् ॥ ४५६ ॥

अथ विरतवचोदयितं बाष्पभरक्लिष्टवर्णपदयोगात् । इति कथमपि हारलता
संमूर्छितवस्तुभारतीमूचे ॥ ४५७॥

अविशुद्धकुलोत्पन्ना देहार्पणजीविका शठाचरणा । काहं रूपाजीवा क्क भवन्तः
श्लाघनीयजन्मगुरणाः ॥ ४५८ ॥

455-474c (fol. 24) are missing from N2.

The Baed's Counsel

185

"It wasn't winning pelf in heaps, Nor hanging on to one safe bet, Nor skill
of mine in flattery, Nor admonition by some boss, Nor beauty's lure, nor
that of fame

-None of these was the motive force That made you act. But all the same A
karmic obstacle so great That we can't halt its ripening Has cropped up now
by fate. (453-454)

"If in a jealous lover's rage, Or in a game, or in a burst Of calling out
injurious taunts, I said harsh things to you, Sweet-teeth, Then please do
not remember it.** (455)

"This heart I place inside your heart; Keeping a thing in trust is hard:
You'll have to hold it carefully Lest it should slip away." (456)

Then when her lover's speech was done, Häralată replied somehow, Her speech
intensified in sense Because its syllables and words Were hampered by the
press of tears. (457)

"How far apart are you and I A girl of impure family Whose livelihood is
hawking flesh, A whore, whose stock-in-trade's deceit, And you, whose birth
and character Command the praise of everyone! (458)

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं कुट्टनीमवम्

यत्तु विषयावलोकनकुतूहलाभ्यागतेन विश्रान्तम् । इयतो दिवसानस्मिन् तन्मे
परजन्मकृतशुभस्य फलम् ॥ ४५६ ॥

गुरुसेवां बन्धुजनं स्वदेशबसति कलवमनुकूलम् । अनुषङ्गदृष्टपरिचित आस्थां
प्रविधाय कः परित्यजति ॥ ४६० ॥

यौवनचापलमेतद्यन्मादृशि भवति कौतुकं भवताम् । यत्तु सुखमनवगीतं तस्य स्थानं
निजा दाराः ॥ ४६१॥

ते मधुराः परिहासास्ता वक्रगिरः स वामतासमयः । नो हृदये कर्तव्या रहसि
क्षेमार्थिना भवता ॥ ४६२ ॥

लाघवतो यन्मनसः प्रणयाद्वा यत्तवाचरितम् । प्रतिकूलं तत्त्र मया नाथाञ्जलिरेष
विरचितो मूर्ध्नि ॥ ४६३ ॥

दुःसंचारा मार्गा दूरे वसतिर्विसंष्ठुठलं हृदयम् । गुणपालित तव सुहृदा
भवितव्यमतो ऽप्रमत्तेन ॥ ४६४ ॥

हृदयद्वय एकत्वं याते यूनोवियोगजं क्लेशम् । अनुभवतोरपरेण प्रसङ्गतः पठ्यते
पथ्या ॥ ४६५॥

The Baud's Counsel

"But that you've stayed so many days When you'd alighted at this place In
eagerness to see the world, That must be due to virtuous deeds Performed by
me in former births. (459)

"Who would put faith in someone known From merely being glimpsed by chance
If that required abandoning One's guru's service, relatives, Inhabiting
one's native place, And marriage to a fitting wife! (460)

"Your interest in a girl like me Is just capriciousness of youth; The place
where you'll find happiness Beyond reproach is your own wife. (461)

"And all those captivating games Those quips, that time of waywardness,
Don't hoard them, secret, in your heart, For you must aim for safety. (462)

"Whatever hurt I may have caused, Out of my spirit's levity Or out of love,
my lord, for you, For that 1 (humbly) join my hands And lift them up to
touch my head. (463)

"The roads are difficult to pass, And home is far, his heart unsteady. Your
friend, o Gúnapalita, Must therefore keep his wits about him." (464)

And as the two young persons ached, With pangs of separation's pain
Afflicting hearts now fused in one, Some other person, by some chance,
Declaimed a standard Aryä verse: (465)

दामोदरगुतविरचितं कुट्टनीनतम्

अन्योन्यसुदृढचेष्टितसद्भावनेहपाशबद्धानाम् । विच्छेदकरो मृत्युर्धीराणां वा
परिच्छेदः ॥ ४६६ ॥

अथ तच्छ्रवणानन्तरमास्स्व मुखं दयितिके व्रजामीति । अभिधाय याति मन्दं
सुन्दरसेने विवर्तितग्रीवम् ॥ ४६७ ॥

वटशाखालम्बिभुजां श्वसितोष्णसमीरशुष्यदधरमुखीम् । पर्वस्तां बिभ्राणां
तन्मार्गविलोकनानिमेशदृशम् ॥ ४६८ ॥

दोलायमानवेणीं तिर्यग्गतकण्ठभूषरणविशेषाम् । गलदधुवारिपूर्णां पतितांशुकभागनिः
सहाङ्गलताम् ॥ ४६६ ॥

रुन्धानामिव हृदयं स्फुटदितरकरेण कुचयुगाश्रयिरणा । परिशेषितां
विलासैरुत्सृष्टां जीवलोककर्तव्यैः ॥ ४७० ॥

अङ्गीकृतां विपत्त्या वशीकृतां मर्मघट्टनैविषमैः ।
हारलतामपरिस्फुटमन्तःपरिकृष्यमाणभारत्या ॥ ४७१॥

मा मा तावद्यात क्षणमेकं यावदेष निष्करुणः । वनगुल्मैर्न तिरोहित इत्यभिदधतीं
जहुः प्रारणाः ॥ ४७२ ॥

The Basod's Counsel

"Two things alone can sepatate A couple bound with bonds of love Whose true
reciprocal regard Is firm and is expressed in acts: Death or a
stout-hearted resolve." (466)

Haralata

Immediately on hearing this Súndaraséna then declared "Fare well, my
darling! I must go," And started walking slowly on But with his head turned
back to her, Whose arm lay on a banyan branch, Lips by her sighs' sirocco
parched Cast down, she fixed unblinking eyes To stare along the way he'd
gone. (467-468)

cannot

Out at an angle swung her braid, Choice necklaces hung down askew; Bathed
in a flood of falling tears, Her creeper-slender frame so weak Her veil had
partly fallen off. (469)

Her other hand, upon her breasts, Appeared to check her splitting heart,
Forsaken by her coquetries, Abandoned by all duties' claims That those
who're still alive must heed, Enveloped by catastrophe, And overpowered by
sufferings That touched her to the quick. And as she said, inaudibly,

Dragging her voice from deep inside, "No, no! Just stay a minute more,
Until that cruel man's concealed Behind the thickets of that wood!", Her
vital breaths relinquished her. (470-474)

दामोदरशुभविरचितं कुहनीमजम्

अथ पश्वात्समुपेतं पप्रच्छ पुरन्दरात्मजः पथिकम् । दृष्टा शोकव्यथिता
निवर्तमानाङ्गना भवता ॥ ४७३ ॥

स उवाच वटतरोरध उर्त्यां पतिता विनिश्वलावयवा । तिष्ठति वनिता नान्या नयनावसरं
गतास्माकम् ॥ ४७४ ॥

इति तद्वचनाश्महतो विह्वलमूर्तिः पपात भूपृष्ठे । उत्थापितश्व सुहृदा सो
ऽभिदधे तेन शोकदीनेन ॥ ४७५॥

भवतु कृतार्थस्तातस्त्वमपि सुमिवास्स्व साम्प्रतं प्रीतः । समकालमेव मुक्ता
पापेन मयासुभिश्व हारलता ॥ ४७६ ॥

हा हा हाव हतो ऽसि ध्वस्ता लीला विलास किं कुरुषे । उच्च्छिन्ना
विच्छित्तिभ्रम विभ्रम दश दिशो निराधारः ॥ ४७७ ॥

किलकिंचित गच्छ वनं मोट्टायितमशरणत्वमुपयातम् । कुट्टमित प्रव्रज्यां गृहाण
बिब्बोक विश भुवो विवरम् ॥ ४७८ ॥

ललितमनाथीभूतं विहृतस्य न विद्यते गतिः कापि । शशधरबिम्बद्युतिमुषि
यातायामन्तकान्तिकं तस्याम् ॥ ४७६ ॥

Baod's Counsel

And Sundara

Now later on, Purandar's son Questioned a wayfarer he met, Who came upon
him from behind, "Did you see any lady there Returning homewards, racked
with grief?" (473)

to grieve

The man replied "There is a girl Who's fallen down upon the ground, Beneath
a banyan tree, her limbs Bereft of movement; otherwise No woman came before
my eyes." (474)

Struck by this thunderbolt of words, His body shook and fell to earth, And
when his friend had roused him up, He spoke to him, weak with despair: (473)

"My father may be satisfied! And you, my friend, be now content! At one
stroke now Haralată Is freed from my vile self and life. (476)

"Alas, o Dalliance, you are damned! Sport, you are spent! And Playfulness,
Pray how can you pursue your fun! And Decoration, you're undone!
Flirtation, flee, you're friendless now: Seek refuge in the ten directions!
(477)

"Flurry, fly off into the forest! Tender Endearments, you've no haven!
Teasing Repulses, go and beg! Affected Impassivity. Depart! Entomb
yourself! (478)

"Ah Loveliness, you've been abandoned! Now that the girl who stole away The
Lustre of the full moon's orb. Has met her death, there is no place For
you, you Bashful Silences! (479)

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं सुहगीसतम्

विनिवृत्त्य यामि दग्धुं मद्विरहे मुक्तवल्लभप्राणाम् । भवतु वराक्यास्तस्याः
सप्तार्चिर्दानमात्रमुपकारः ॥ ४८० ॥

गत्वाथ तमुद्देशं यस्मिन् सा पञ्चभावमापन्ना । विललाप मुक्तनादं विलुठन् भुवि
सहचरेण धृतमूर्तिः ॥ ४८१ ॥

एते वयं निवृत्ता मुन्द्ध रुषं देहि कोपने वाचम् । उत्तिष्ठ किमिति तिष्ठसि
भूमितले रेगुरूषितशरीरा ॥ ४८२ ॥

विनिमील्य दृशौ कस्मादप्रतिपत्त्या स्थितासि शुभवदने ।
त्वदवारितगमनविधेरपराधितया न मे योगः ॥ ४८३॥

नाकाधिपतिपुरन्ध्रीरभिभवितुं त्वयि दिवं प्रयातायाम् । सत्स्वपि शरेषु पञ्चसु
निरायुधः साम्प्रतं मदनः ॥ ४८४ ॥

वञ्चकवृत्ता वेश्या इत्यपवादो जनेषु यो रूढः । अपनीतो ऽसौ निपुणं त्वया प्रिये
जीवमोच्चक्षेण ॥ ४८५ ॥

वन्द्यः सव्रत एकत्रिपुरान्तकनन्दनो महासेनः । हृदयं यस्य स्पृष्टं न मनागपि
वामलोचनाप्रेम्णा ॥ ४८६ ॥

485 Durghasavetti ad Apädbyay 4.1.43 quotes this verse as follows:

The Baud's Counsel

"I shall turn back to burn her body, That girl who lost her dear
life-breath Because of being cut off from me. All I can do to make amends
Is to consign the wretched girl Into the seven flames of fire." (480)

He then went back to where she'd left To mingle with the elements, And
there he wailed with open throat And rolled about upon the ground, While
his friend held him in his arms. (481)

"It's me, I'm back! Do not be cross! You're angry, but please speak to me!
Get up! Why hug the earth like that, Your body soiled with dust! (482)

"Open your eyes! Why stay there dumb? How beautiful your face, my dear!
Will you disown me, hurt because You couldn't stop me leaving? (483)

"With you set off for paradise To triumph over Indra's wives, Käma,
although he keeps five darts, Is weaponless today. (484)

"Whores live by fraud: this biased charge Has spread among men everywhere,
But you have wiped it out, my love, By giving up your life. (485)

"Only Kumara, Šiva's son Deserves respect for chastity His heart not even
slightly touched By love for girls with sparkling eyes. (486)

दामोदरगुमविरचितं ‌तम्

मन्ये ऽभीष्टवियोगं निमेषमपि दुःसहं समवधार्य । हरिरणा बच्ासि लक्ष्मीविधृता
गौरी हरेण देहार्थे ॥ ४८७ ॥

अयि लोकपाल सा भुवि ललामभूता तया विना शून्यम् । विश्वमिति किं न
चिन्तितमात्मस्थानं प्रियां नयता ॥ ४८८ ॥

भगवन् हुतवह मा मा लावण्यसमुद्रसारमुद्धृत्य । कथमपि विहितां धावा
धक्ष्यस्येनां जगद्रूषाम् ॥ ४८६ ॥

इति विलपन्तं बहुविधमवधीर्य सुहृत् पुरन्दरस्य सुतम् । काष्ठविरचय्य चितां
तामकरोदग्निसागणिकाम् ॥ ४८०॥

तस्मिन्निद्धहुताशनविनिपतने कृतमतौ शुचाकुलिते । मनसि स्फुरितामार्या पपाठ
कश्चित्प्रसङ्गेन ॥ ४६१॥

अनुमरणे व्यवसायं स्त्रीधर्मे कः करोति सविवेकः । संसारमुक्तत्युपायं
दण्डग्रहणं व्रतं मुक्ता ॥ ४६२॥

श्रुत्वा सुन्दरसेनः सचिवमवोचह्मपेतवैक्लव्यः । प्रतिबोधितं मनो मे धीरेरणानेन
युक्तमुपदिशता ॥ ४६३ ॥

The Bond's Coumel

"They must consider being split For just the twinkling of an eye From their
beloved ones to be Too painful to endure, I guess, And that is why Hari
retains Laksmi against his chest, while Hata Enfolds, as half his body,
Gauri. (487)

"Ah Death, who guard the Southem Quarter, She was the omament of Earth!
Without her all the world is empty! Did you forget this as you led My love
to where you dwell! (488)

"No, no, don't bum her God of Fire! She was a jewel of the world, Created
by the demiurge Somehow using the distillate Extracted from a sea of
beauty." (489)

Paying no heed to Sundara Lamenting in these various ways, His friend built
up a pyre with logs And set the courtesan alight. (490)

When Súndara, distraught with grief, Had made resolve to leap inside, Into
the blazing fire, just then It happened that some man declaimed A verse
that came to mind: (491)

"What man of sense would make a pledge To follow someone else in death! A
woman's zêle! He'd thus renounce The means to liberate himself The rite of
taking up the staff, From endlessly repeated birth." (492)

Sundaraséna, bearing this, No longer fraught, said to his friend, "My
mind's awakened by this sage. Who indicates a fitting course. (493)

LIBRA

he everbears deflects Ho arge ra opt for micide

दामोदरशुभविरचितम्

चारणदृष्टनष्टवल्लभजन्मजराव्याधिमरणपरिभूते । आवर्तिनि संसारे कः
कुर्यादाग्रहं सुमतिः ॥ ४६४॥

यातु भवान् कुसुमपुरं वयमप्यन्त्याश्रमे समाश्रयणम् । अङ्गीकुर्मो
ऽविद्याप्रहारणसंसिद्धये विहितम् ॥ ४६५ ॥

सो ऽवददभिजातजनो बाल्यात्प्रभृति त्वया च न वियुक्तः । संन्यसनबुद्धिमधुना
कथमुज्झति विषयनिः स्पृहं सुहृदम् ॥ ४६६ ॥

एवमिति सो ऽभिधाय स्थिरयतिनियमैस्तपोधनैर्जुष्टम् । गुरणपालितेन सहितः
सुन्दरसेनो जगाम वनम् ॥ ४६७॥

एवं भवन्तु वेश्याः स्वार्थेकदृशो व्यपेतसद्भावाः । अभिलषितविषयसिद्धेः का
हानिस्तदपि युष्माकम् ॥ ४६८ ॥

रमणहृदयानुवर्तनचतुरचतुःषष्टिकर्मकुशलानाम् । न स्पृशति तत्त्वचर्चा
पण्यवधूनां विदग्धचेतांसि ॥ ४६६ ॥

The Baud's Counsel

"What man of judgement would eling on To incarnation's spinning wheel,
Which pullulates with births and deaths, Senescence, illness; where one's
loves Are barely glimpsed before they perish! (494)

"You, you may head for Küsumpür, Myself. I'm going to adopt The final
aframa of life, Created so that men may leave The state of nescience
behind." (495)

He chanses to adapt

He said, "How could a gentleman, From whom you've never been apart Since
childhood, now abandon you. A friend who's settled on sannyas, Shunning
desire for carnal pleasures?" (496)

And Gúnapälite

"So be it then", said Sündara, And with his friend entered the woods. The
favoured haunt of anchorites Who heed ascetics' stringent rules. (497)

555

"So therefore just let prostitutes Be focussed only on self-interest,
Devoid of genuine affection. If you should gain what you desire What harm
is there in any case? (498)

"When prostitutes are masterly At gratifying lovers' hearts And skilled in
sixty-four fine arts, Wise men decline to probe and see If they are true in
nature. (499)

197

How Malati should clarify The lesson of this moral tale.

दामोदरगुतविरचितं

वलित्प्पतचिवगतिस्थितिवेगैश्वोदनानुवृत्त्या च । रागस्पर्शेन विना विशति मनः
सादिनां तुरगः ॥ ५०० ॥

गन्धो ऽपि कुतः प्रेम्णः परभृतहारीतगृहकपोतानाम् । उज्ज्वलयन्त्यसमेषु
विरुतविशेषैस्तथापि ते यूनाम् ॥ ५०१ ॥

आहितयुक्ताहार्यः सम्यक्सकलप्रयोगनिष्पत्त्या । भावविहीनो ऽपि नटः
सामाजिकचित्तरञ्जनं कुरुते ॥ ५०२ ॥

ये ऽपि धनक्षयदोषं पश्यन्ति जडा विलासिनीश्लेषे । प्रष्टव्यास्ते भवता
किमकृतकशिपुव्यया दाराः ॥ ५०३॥

न च लाभ एक एव प्रवर्तने कारणं मनुष्येषु । रागादयो ऽपि सन्ति
वैशिकशास्त्रप्रणेतृभिः कथिताः ॥ ५०४ ॥

जीवितं क्षणार्धेन ॥ ५०५ ॥

The Baud's Counsel

"By rapidly effecting tums And jumps and other sorts of moves, Or standing
still or running fast, According to the orders given, A horse contrives to
get inside The rider's heart and yet without The slightest trace of
fondness. (300)

"Even a whiff of love's not there Among the cuckoos and green doves, The
pigeons kept as pets at home; And yet with various warbling sounds They
kindle juvenescents' passion. (301)

"An actor dons the proper dress, Presents the whole performance well, And,
though the feelings are not his, Impassions his spectators' hearts. (302)

"As for the fools who see some fault In spending money shtupping tarts,
Just ask them this: 'Isn't your wife A profligate with food and clothes?"
503

"And gain is not the only thing That motivates our acts with men Passion
and such exist as well-They're mentioned by the experts too In learned
treatises on harlots. (504)

"And anyway, what wealth was gained By that poor girl from Súndarat Struck
down by parting's thunderbolt She quit her life in half a trice. (505)

दामोदरगुमचिरचित

उत्तमतरुणप्रकृतिः पुलकादिकसूचितान्यतनुसक्तिः । स्फुटसंनिहितविभावो निवार्यते
केन शृङ्गारः ॥ ५०६॥

अन्तःकररणविकारं गुरुपरिजनसंकटे ऽपि कुलटानाम् । बुध्यन्ति तदभियुक्ता

रागोपरत्तहृदया यान्ति दिगन्तं मनुष्य आसज्य ॥ ५०८ ॥

अपमानः पतिविहितो गुरुपरिकरतीव्रता गृहे ऽपि दौर्गत्यम् ।

रागविमुक्तास्तिष्ठन्त्यौचित्यमावेण ॥ ५१०॥

The Bawd's Counsel

"Who can stop love, arising in Two peerless hearts in youthful prime When
everything that triggers it Is plainly present and the pull That draws
their hodies close is clear From suchlike tell-tale indices As shivers of
horripilation! (106)

"Even when elders crowd her in, Or members of domestic staff, A man of some
experience Can still detect the changing moods Even a wanton can't conceal
From a sweet sideways darting glance Or from a lifted brow. (507)

"Some women, not considering The stigma faced by family. When others bruit
abroad their shame Will hook up with some man and bolt, Leaving their
husbands' homes behind, With hearts aflame, to the world's ends. (508)

"For certain wives, a husband's scorn, The bitterness of elders, staff,
Unhappiness at home conspire To wear away their rectitude And these as well
become attached Romantically to paramours. (509)

"As for the ladies who remain, Their conduct never wavering. Devoted to
their husbands' care, They too are only so-disposed Just for the sake of
seemliness Because no love has come their way. (sto)

दामोदरगुतविरचितं

तस्मादस्त्वभिगमनं विविधनिमित्तं निवार्यते केन । निजपरपण्यस्त्रीणां रागाधीनं
तु हृदयनिर्वहणम् ॥ ५११॥

एवंविधदृष्टान्तैरुपपत्तियुतैस्तथेदृशैर्वाक्यैः । अन्यैरपि
चाटुपदैरावर्जितमानसं गम्यम् ॥ ५१२ ॥

विहितस्वापविबोधं किंचित्प्रकटीकृतश्रमलान्या । उत्पादितजृम्भिकया परिरभ्य घनं
निशापगमे ॥ ५१३॥

खले किं प्रयातासि ॥ ५१४ ॥

विकसितसुरभिमनोहरसंस्थानं सरसकुसुममप्राप्तम् । न करोति तथा
पीडामास्वादितविच्युतं यथा भृङ्गयाः ॥ ५१७॥

विज्ञापयाम्यतस्त्वां रचिताञ्जलिमौलिना विधाय नतिम् । परिचारकजनमध्ये गणनीयाहं
प्रसादेन ॥ ५१८ ॥

अथ दीपितरागाङ्गैरपहस्तितलाभदिक्क्रमोपचितैः । मृदुद्भिश्चित्तानुगतैरुपचारैः

जालमार्गेण ॥ ५२० ॥

मालत्या सह केलि विदधासि सखी ममेति न विरोधः । यत्तु चिरं स्निग्धदृशा पश्यसि
तां तव मे शङ्का ॥ ५२१॥

ग्राहिता कमलदेवी ॥ ५२२ ॥

इत्थं मिथ्यावचः कलहम् ॥ ५२८ ॥

अक्लेशोपनतधनः प्रेमप्रह्लो निरर्गलत्यागः । भट्टानन्दस्य सुतो निधिभूतो
ऽभव्यया त्वया त्यक्तः ॥ ५२६॥

व्यसनोपहतविवेको दैवैकगतिः स्वदारविद्वेषी । मामविगणय्य मूडे निर्भत्सित एव
केशवस्वामी ॥ ५३० ॥

अगणितराजापायो ऽविच्छिन्नायः स्वभावतस्त्यागी । किमुपेचितो ऽनुरक्तो वामधिया

चित्रादिकलाकुशलः स्मरशास्त्रविचक्षरणो वृषप्रकृतिः । उपकुर्वन्नपि सर्वो
विद्वेषिगणे त्वया क्षिप्तः ॥ ५३५॥

चन्द्रवतीमाभरणं दत्तं मधुसूदनस्य पुत्रेण । पश्यन्ती विभ्राणामयि रागिणि किं
न जिल्लेषि ॥ ५३६ ॥

पर्याप्तम् ॥ ५३८ ॥

सकलस्य ॥ ५३६॥

तमुपभुङ्गे ॥ ५४० ॥

212

दामोदरगुप्मविरचितमतम्

पुंस्त्वख्यापनकामो न स्त्री न पुमान् किल प्रभुस्वामी ।
अनुबन्नन्नुपहसितस्त्वया जडे स्वार्थयोगमनपेक्ष्य ॥ ५४१ ॥

वाजीकरणैकमतिर्नरनाथानुग्रहेण विख्यातः । प्रत्याख्यातः स तथा रविदेवः
किंकरत्वमाकाङ्गन् ॥ ५४२ ॥

कि कन्दर्पकुटुम्बे जातो ऽसावुत वशीकररणयोगम् । जानाति कमपि सिद्धं
येनाकृष्टासि सर्वभावेन ॥ ५४३ ॥

बाल्ये तावदयोग्या पश्चादपि वृद्धभावपरिभूता । तारुण्ये रागहृता यदि गणिका
भ्रमतु तद्भिक्षाम् ॥ ५४४ ॥

उपनय भाण्डकमेतद्यदर्जितं मामकेन देहेन । विदधामि तीर्थयात्रामास्स्व सुखं
प्रेयसा सार्धम् ॥ ५४५ ॥

आर्यजननिन्दितानां पापैकरसप्रकाशनारीणाम् । एतावानेव गुरणो यदभीष्टसमागमो
निरावरणः ॥ ५४६ ॥

The Basod's Counsel

"That Prabhusvami, epicene To all appearances, desired To advertise his
manliness And so attached himself to you; But you, you fool, took no
account Of what you stood to gain, and scoffed. (543)

"You've also turned down Rávidév, Known to be favoured by the king. Who is
obsessed with quaffing philtres And who aspired to be your slave. (542)

"Was this man born of Kama's blood! Or does he know some sure-fire means Of
subjugation which explains How he has fully vanquished you! (543)

"First, when a child, she isn't fit, And later on, old age will triumph:
If, in the flower of youth, a whore Allows herself to fall in love, She
might as well set out to beg. (544)

"Bring me my little chest, the fruit Of hawking round these limbs of mine,
And I'll set off on pilgrimage While you stay here, taking your ease,
Together with your paramour." (345)

555

"For whores," whom honest folk revile, Who're famed for having taste for
vice And vice alone, togetherness, Quite unconstrained, with men they love
Is all they really value. (546)

215

Then Malari

defend her side, Rating above

all lucre love.

दामोदरगुमविरचितं तुट्टनीमतम्

नो धनलाभो लाभो लाभः खलु बल्लभेन संसर्गः । अक्षिगतादर्थाप्तिर्न भवति मनसः
प्रमोदाय ॥ ५४७॥

गाडानुरागभिन्नं तारुण्यरसामृतेन संसिक्तम् । न भजति सहृदयहृदयं
विभवार्जनसम्भवा चिन्ता ॥ ५४८ ॥

लाभः स एव परमः पर्याप्तं तेन तेन तृप्तास्मि । विनिवेश्य यदुत्सङ्गे
निक्षिपति मुखे मुखेन ताम्बूलम् ॥ ५४६ ॥

सुरतश्रमवारिकरणान् परिमाष्टि निजांशुकेन गावेषु । यदुरसि निधाय विहसंस्तस्य न
मूल्यं वसुन्धरा सकला ॥ ५५० ॥

शिथिलितनिजदाररतिर्मयि सक्तमना अनन्यकर्तव्यः । यदसौ जितनलरूपस्तिरस्कृतं तेन
गाणिक्यम् ॥ ५५१ ॥

बहुकुसुमरसास्वादं कुर्वाणा मधुकरी विधिनियोगात् । ईदृक्प्रसवविशेषं लभते खलु
येन भवति कृतकृत्या ॥ ५५२ ॥

अयि सरले तावदिमा उपदेशगिरो विशन्ति कन्तिः । यावन्नान्तर्भूतं तच्चेतसि मामकं
चेतः ॥ ५५३ ॥

The Baud's Counsel

"The gain of lucre isn't gain, It's obvious that gain in truth Is union
with the man you love. Earning one's keep from those one loathes Just
cannot gratify the heart. (147)

"The hearts of those of finer sense, Awakened by a potent love, Drenched
with the nectar-juice of youth, Don't fret about the quest for cash. (548)

"That is the ultimate reward, That is enough, that satisfies! That moment
when he lifts me up Into his lap and puts his mouth To mine, to share his
betel quid. (349)

"When, with a smile, he lays me down Upon his chest and takes his robe To
stroke my limbs and wipe away The drops of sweat from love's exertions, All
of the treasure-yielding carth Would not be price enough to pay. (150)

"Because this man, whose beauty vies With that of Nala, turns to me,
Leaving all other work aside, And lets his marriage bed grow cold-. This
raises me above all whores." (151)

"The bee, of course, by fate's decree, While sipping juice from many
flowers, Can light upon a bloom to choice That all its aims seem then
attained. (552)

"Oh how naive! Such guiding words Can only penetrate my ears Until the
moment when my heart Has merged itself in his. (391)

दामोदरनुमकिन कुट्टनीमतम्

श्रीरस्तु दुर्गतिर्वा वेश्मनि वासो महत्यरण्ये वा । स्वलकि नरके वा कि बहुना
तेन मे सार्धम् ॥ ५५४ ॥

इदमास्ते (लंकरणं दुर्जननि गृहाण कि ममैतेन । तेनैव भूषिताहं गुणनिधिना
भट्टपुत्रेण ॥ ५५५ ॥

समुत्सृज्य ॥ ५५६ ॥

सीमन्तिन्यस्तृणाय मन्यन्ते ॥ ५५८ ॥

The Band's Counsel

"Let there be luck or indigence, A home to dwell or wilderness, Heaven or
hell-it's all the same If he is by my side. (554)

"Stop decking me with jewellery, You horrid mother, take it off! What do I
need with that, when he, This treasure-house, this son of Bhatt Is all the
ornament I need!" (355)

While making speeches such as these Start hastily unfastening The ornaments
from here and there, Where they're attached, set them all down Before the
bawd and rush away. (556)

Blinded with love at hearing this He may perhaps reflect as follows: And
then there's nothing they won't do. (557) "Affection conquers women's hearts

mother, hearth and relatives, For property, or life itself. (538)

"Just like a thunder-bolt, a stone Cast by a catapult laid low A certain
Vajra in the van Of battle, whereupon his love, Although no spell enchanted
her. Breathed out, they say, a final time. (559)

She storms away to and the row

Ther Bhatta will ruminate About true love

The Band's Counsel

"A Southerner called Mánikantha Had died because his time had come; A
prostitute, who worshipped him, Returned with him to ash.

119

"There once was a man from the South

Who passed on in due time forsooth. His lover adoring. Though given to
whoring. Was burnt with that man who went South. (160)

"When Bhaskar Värman passed away His mistress couldn't bear that grief And,
though impeded by the king, Consigned her mortal self to flames. (561)

"A gura called Lion, who went naked,

"The naked guru, Narasimha, Fell in a fiercely flaming fire, His whore,
oppressed with misery, Gave to those very flames her limbs As sacrificial
offering.

Once fell in the fire and was haked. His whore was so fazed That she leapt
in the blaze And her body was promptly oblated. (162)

"Her heart weighed down with heavy love, Jihalla quit her livelihood, A
heavenly job bequeathed to her, And took the hand of Visau Bhatt Until such
time as death should part them.

"A girl called Jihallä inherits A heavenly job which she quits For love has
oppressed her So she's joined her molester

And plighted her troth till their obits. (163)

"When Vämadeva fell in war, That pair of women from abroad On whom he'd
deigned to cast a glance, Just out of kindness, couldn't bear Abandoning
his feet. (364)

The moment when Kadamba's son Had left for Yama's residence, The greatest
of the courtesans, His Ranadevi, took her life. (561)

breasts. (571)

"A man who hasn't stored away A hoard of merit will not hear Her cries of
"AAA" while making love Mixed up with agitated moans At mercilessly bitten
lips. (374)

"When trembling in the bump and grind Of carnal combat in full swing. This
mistress can eclipse this world Of never-ending birth and death Behind a
spreading vine of leaves That are the ourward marks of love. (573)

"When very tightly clutched, my love Will faint away with happiness, But
when my hold grows somewhat loose She breathes again and, as she does,
Displays her various emotions. (574)

"True, there are other women too Who're specialists of cow-girl sex, But
she is certainly the one To found a learned institute For 'girls on top in
bed'. (175)

"Passion uncurbed, she manifests A mastery in giving voice To koel's hoots,
to sighs, to trills, And in so doing ties the tongues Of veenas and of
other strings. (576)

The Bawd's Counsel

"If Love could see her lovely gestures, Her stretchings, turnings, smiles,
and tremblings, He wouldn't hold in high regard The moves in sex that Rati
makes. (577)

"Her sense of humour isn't coane, The way she casts her eyes about Is never
graceless; when she halts Her amorous activities Then Käma's left with
thirsts unquenched. (178)

"Her tone of speech does not suggest Venality; she lacks no skill Intuiting
what others wish; Nor does she want for eloquence, When that's required, to
compliment The sterling traits of others. (579)

"This girl, this fountainhead of wit, Whose ample buttocks slow her pace,
She never praises other men, Nor does she ever disregard, The proper time
and place and dress. (580)

"According to the circumstance, This slender-waisted girl performs The
sheldrake's clasp, the swan's embrace, The dove's caress, the
mongoose-clinch. (181)

"A man whose heart she has beguiled With conversation, quips and jests Will
find his wife appear to him -Though biddable and not unlovely-Only a burden
to be borne. (582)

आकरार्य मामवादीद्धन्यास्ता युवतयः सखि कठोराः । या विषहन्ते दीर्घ
प्रियतमविरहानलासङ्गम् ॥ ५८६॥

मम तु दिनान्तरिते ऽपि प्रेयसि लब्ध्वा सहायसामग्रीम् । विदधाति मकरकेतन
उत्कलिकाविधुरितं हृदयम् ॥ ५६० ॥

उत्कण्ठयति भृशं मां समीरणो बकुलकुसुमगन्धाक्यः । प्रच्यावयन्ति
धैर्यान्मधुरध्वनितैः कलापभृतः ॥ ५६१॥

वीक्षितुमविरलमालिङ्गितो यया कान्तः ॥ ५६२॥

स्वेच्छागमनलघुत्वं बहुलापायं निशासु पन्थानम् । न विचारयन्ति महिला
अभीष्टतमसङ्गतावुत्काः ॥ ५६३ ॥

क्रियतां भूषणशोभा त्वरयति मे मानसं मनोजन्मा । रञ्जयति मनो नितरां

दामोदरसुमविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

संनिहितकलवाणामनुचितमिति बाह्यलोकसंवदनात् । अन्यस्मिन्नुदवसिते
विसर्जितामिष्टमालतीकेन ॥ ६०१॥

लोकेन हास्यमानां बिभ्राणां वाससी जलक्लिन्ने । रूपमदमुत्सृजन्तीं
वैलक्ष्यब्रीडितेन नतवदनाम् ॥ ६०२ ॥

पश्चात्तापगृहीतां कण्टकदर्भाग्रभिन्नपादतलाम् । अस्मद्वचः स्मरन्तीं

एषा प्रपञ्चरचना यदि भवति वृथा पुनः पुरस्तस्य । वणिगिदमुपेत्य वक्ष्यति
सहायपरिचोदितो भवतीम् ॥ ६०५ ॥

Venturing our for night-time trysts, Ditching the pride you feel in beauty
Rigged out in saturated clothes, Ashamed to find yourself transformed, And
therefore bowing down your head, With scars of thorns and darbha-grass Upon
your feet, seized by remorse, You will recall these words of mine. (602-603)

"Her mother gave this harsh advice Which she ignored, and on the way To
meet with you, some wretched thieves, Driving away her body-guard. Have
gone and robbed my friend." (604)

But if this convoluted scheme Should prove in vain, in front of him, Upon a
sign some friend will give, A merchant should come up to you And speak to
you like this: (605)

"Against those pearls you earlier pawned Your maid took thirty silvers
more," She's asked some more for more expenses." As for the camphor,
saffron, sandal, The incense and the other things That you bought
separately, I have them Logged in the ledger. Listen here. I'll tot the
total up for you. (606-607)

555

If you should notice him to be A little too affectionate, Desiring now to
eat and sleep And live with you, then use these means -Some laxative and
some emetic To spew him out or dump him. (613)

Once you have ascertained by skill That he has spent what wealth he had By
seeing that the usurer Does not respect him, shapely girl, Or seeing that
the food and clothes He gives to pamper you decline, Or hearing what his
friends remark, (616)

Bearing in mind that later on Having him hear discordant wonds Could be a
bar to your desires, These are the means that you could use, With care, to
jettison your client. (617)

Assigning him a distant seats A languidness in getting up To welcome him
when he returns And other points of etiquette, A tendency to carp or saunt
In conversation; jokes that wound; (618)

The Bawd's Counsel

Lauding his rivals, harping on In praise of their superior traits, Their
ardour too, and when he speaks With honeyed words persistently, Reminding
him he talks too much, (619)

And interrupting conversation Whenever he's relating something With
fusillades of other chatter; Recoiling at his settled habits; Leaving his
side upon a pretext; (630)

At bedtime, making some excuse And so delaying; when in bed Rolling away
from him, declaring That you are just too sleepy now, And if he tries to
make you turn To face him, getting all upset; (621)

Preventing him from touching "there", Indifference in bouts of sex: With
kisses, screwing up your face, Drawing your limbs together tight Whenever
he embraces you, (622)

Unwillingness to tolerate His slaps and bites and pinching nails;
Repugnance when the act of love Goes on and on; and telling him "Just
sleep" if he begins again; (623)

Insisting on it when he's spent; Deriding when he shows off skills; And, in
the hope the night will end. Quizzing the night-watch over and over; (644)

On vaulting briskly out of bed, Quitting the bed-chamber at dawn And
vigorously shouting out "The night has gone! daybreak has come!" (625)

उभयेच्छया प्रवृत्तं निरुपाधि प्रेम भवति रमणीयम् । अन्योन्यसमासक्ती
संस्थानमिवाभिजातमणिहेस्रोः ॥

६२६॥

यस्त्वेकाश्रयरागः परिभवदौर्बल्यदैन्यनाशानाम् । स निदानमसंदिग्धं सीतां प्रति
दशमुखस्येव ॥ ६२७ ॥

यानि हरन्ति मनांसि स्मितवीक्षितजल्पितानि रक्तानाम् । तान्येव विरक्तानां
प्रतिभान्ति विवर्तितानीव ॥ ६२८ ॥

विदधातु किमपि कथमपि निगृह्यमारणा मुहूर्तमासिष्ये । इति यत्र मनः स्त्रीणां
तवापि रते रमन्ति पशुतुल्याः ॥ ६२६ ॥

यत्र न मदनविकाराः सद्भावसमर्पणं न गात्राणाम् । तस्मिन्नुज्झितभावे पशुकर्मणि
पशव एव रज्यन्ते ॥ ६३० ॥

त्यजति ॥ ६३१ ॥

तस्मै मही रन्ध्रम् ॥ ६३२ ॥

"Love, which is free of artifice, Which starts by mutual wish, delights,
Just like the meld of gold and gems Of noblest worth when set together.
(626)

"Whereas an unrequited love Will prove without a doubt a source Of failure,
weakness, grief and death, Like that of Rävaņa for Sitä. (637)

"Those smiles, those glances, and that blarney Which charm the hearts of
those in love. Appear, for those who're not entranced, Transformed into
their opposites. (638)

"The likes of beasts may take delight In acts of love in which the girl
Must feel: 'Oh let him go ahead And do just what he wants: somehow I'll lie
pinned down a while. (639)

"When signs of passion don't appear, And limbs are proffered
lovelessly-Such bestial, unfeeling acts Are wallowed in by beasts alone.
(610)

"A man with sense of self-respect Who's daily treated with contempt And
finds affection for himself Diminishing, will leave a girl, Even his lawful
wedded wife. (631)

"If, to a friend, a woman winks And laughing slaps her hand ('High five!"),
At his expense, then let the earth Open itself and swallow him. (632)

The Band's Counnel

"A man who, even when he hears The merits of his rivals praised And
criticism of himself Comparing him with someone else. Retains his poise,
will keep it still When snared within the noose of Death. (633)

"A man whom even servants treat As nothing, once they've understood The way
their mistress thinks of him Is barely worth five cowry-shells. (634)

"A man who cannot tell apart When someone acts from truth or lies Is surely
Siva and deeres A crescent moon?... a prince of beasts Who ought to be
half-nelsconed. (635)

"The worthless wustrel bobs about On seas of girls, and cannot reach
Discrimination's further shore, And casting piecemeal overboard His
gravitas, he soon achieves, Through emptiness, the weight of chaff. (696)

"It's fools who take the outward signs Produced by artifice and effort To
stir erotic sentiment By those whose livelihood depends On Amatory Arts-as
truth. (617)

"Some simpletons, alas, crave bonda Of friendship that will never die Even
with girls who can be bought, Who have no thought of modesty, Who're
focussed on the job they do. (638)

The Baud's Counsel

"Monographers on joys of sex Prescribe that even wealthy men Are only fit
for intercourse When ready money's visible! Why talk of those whose wealth
is spent! (639)

"And Vyasa too, the sage, has sang: The two worst types of men are these
The man who lusts but cannot pay. And one who rages but is weak. The pair
of them distress the heart. (640)

"Liven a wife will not respect A person stripped of property. So how much
less a prostitute, Who lives by selling fleshly wares, Whose one delight is
revenue? (641)

"Even the lowliest of beasts.

Unable to discriminate What should be sought, what cast aside, Will leave a
flower whose nectar's drained. So will not then the courtesan, Who knows
her business, toss away A man whose wealth she's syphoned off! (642)

"A lover who is liberal Inspires affection liberally. Conversely,
indisputably, liberal men do not. (641)

"What's past is past: and future gain Does not command such great respect:
Money in cash in band right now Thrills girls who're shared by many men.
(644)

दामोदरनुमितम्

पीडितमधु मधुजालं तुच्छीभूतं च मन्मथाग्रस्तम् । मुञ्चन्ति मदनशेषं चुद्राश्व
प्रकटरामाश्च ॥ ६४५॥

एकः कीरणात्यद्य प्रातो भविता तथापरः क्रेता । अन्यवशे चक्षणमेकं न विक्रयः
शाश्वतो ऽस्ति वेश्यानाम् ॥ ६४६ ॥

संदर्शितपरमार्थ भूक्षेपकटाचादृष्टहसितादि । शृण्वन्तु ये सकणस्तित्कृतमन्यव
संक्रान्तम् ॥ ६४७ ॥

यदि नाम निराकरणे न समर्था छिन्नकार्यबन्धे ऽपि। का चिन्महानुभावा बोद्धव्यं
तदपि चेतनावद्भिः ॥ ६४८ ॥

तेनार्थेनोपकृतं तयापि तस्य स्वदेहदानेन । तच्चातीतं सम्प्रति निरर्थकः
शुष्कशृङ्गारः ॥ ६४६॥

अवधीरणा रसायनमपमानो भवति यस्य परितुष्ट्यै । योग्यो ऽसौ पुरुषखरः
खरतरनिर्भर्त्सनोक्तिलकुटानाम् ॥ ६५० ॥

The Baud's Counsel

"As bees abandon honeycombs Squeezed dry of every drop of honey, So easy
women lovelorn swains, Reduced to husks of what they were: The first leave
only aww behind, The second, only wanting. (643)

"One man will purchase her today Tomorrow someone else will buy: At any
given time another's.... There is no "freehold' sale of whores. (646)

"An eyebrow's twitch, a sidelong glance, A way of laughing and so
forth-Their real meaning has been shown: May those with ears to hear catch
on. They're done with now (at least for you): They've been transferred to
other men." (647)

"Now if some girl of generous heart Should find she cannot ditch her man,
Although their business ties are cut, You'd think a man of sense would
twig. (648)

"Money the man bestowed on her, While she bestowed on him her flesh. But
that is over now: today Their dried up love is vain. (649)

"A man for whom disparagement Is elixir, and disrepect Provides delight
that human ass. Is fit for pummeling with words Of bitterest excoriation.
(650)

The Batod's Counsel

"A woman and a lantern's flame Are really not dissimiları For both will
tend towards sirudna, That is: 'to bliss' or 'to extinction'; And yet there
is this difference: The second does so when it lacks A certain stickiness
(wil: oil), The first requires the glue of love. (61)

"Dharma, for her, is realised By sex with some lovelom young man, Deserving
but necessitous; The aim of Wealth can be attained By congress with a
wealthy man; Käma, the third of human goals, She satisfies by savouring A
man whose lusts suit hers. (652)

"But if a man's no use for these-For winning Duty, Wealth, Desire-And those
who live a worthwhile life Should ask what purpose he might serve. How will
a man like that give answer? (653)

"Caught in a turmoil of desires, With tender feelings ridiculed By
libertines and dogged by need, The youthful years of fools portend Nothing
but rack and rain. (654)

"Just as the bud unfurls itself A little honey bee will plunge Inside a
carmine lotus-bloom, Attracted only by one thing: That's where it finds a
drink. A harlot, on the other hand, Her mind upon her selfish goals, Drawn
only by a draught of cash Won't cast herself on carb-mapped main (655)

यामः मम्

यासां कायपिच्क्षा सकटाक्षनिरीक्षणे ऽपि वेश्यान दर्शनमावचु‌भितैर्वच्यन्ते
ताः कथं पुरुषैः ॥ ६५६८

क्लेशाय दुर्गतानां मानस्तुतिगावभङ्गविन्यासम् । गणिकाभिनयचतुष्टयमाकृष्टौ
स्वापतेयपुष्टानाम् ॥ ६५७॥

किं धच्यति भूयो ऽपि ज्वलनस्तं तादृशं कुलाङ्गारम् । यो दह्यते ऽविरामं
विरक्तदासीतिरस्कारैः ॥ ६५८ ॥

गृहमेतदीश्वरारणां कान्तारं दुष्प्रवेशमन्येषाम् । पूत्कृतमिदमुद्भूजया न
मालती कामसत्तूदानपरा ॥ ६५६ ॥

इति चोदितगृहचेटीनिगदितकटुकाक्षरान्यकृतलक्ष्याः । आकर्णयतो वाचो दैवोपहतस्य
तस्य मर्मभिदः ॥ ६६० ॥

एवमभिधीयमानो नो बुध्यति यदि पशुर्नराकारः । तदिदं सुन्दरि वाच्यः प्रथितवचसा
त्वया कामी ॥ ६६१ ॥

The Bared' Cowael

"Before so much as sidling looks, Whores will observe good business sense:
How could they then be gulled by men Whom just a glaner can agitase! (616)

"Her injured pride, her blandishments, Het postures and her ornaments, -A

But touching him at tender points. (660)

Now if that beast in human form Although he's spoken of this way Somehow
declines to understand Then you, my beauty, courteously, Address your lover
thus: (661)

दामोदर

कुद्धनीमतम्

प्रीयत एव तवोपरि हृदयं मे कि तु गुरुजनाधीना । मातृवचोतिक्रमणं न समर्था
संविधातुमहम् ॥ ६६२॥

अर्हसि तावदतस्त्वं गन्तुमितः कतिपयान्यपि दिनानि । पुनरपि भवतैव समं
भोक्तव्यं जीवलोकसुखम् ॥ ६६३ ॥

निर्वासिते तु तस्मिन् यः कामी पूर्वमुज्झितो भुक्ता । तस्य
प्राप्तविभूतेर्युक्तिरियं भिन्नसंधाने ॥ ६६४ ॥

उपवनलीलाविहरणहावोज्ज्वलमञ्जुलस्य सह तेन । वर्णनमितिवृत्तस्य स्मरजविकाराश्व
वीक्षिते तस्मिन् ॥ ६६५ ॥

इदमुपवनमतिधन्यं निर्भरमालिङ्गितं सुरभिलक्ष्म्या । मत्स्कन्धापितपाणिर्वभ्राम
स यव जीविताधीशः ॥ ६६६ ॥

सख्य इतो भ्रमरकुलवासितया प्रियतमो मया सहसा । वक्रीभवत्पयोधरमुपगूढो
धीरसीत्कारम् ॥ ६६७ ॥

सुश्लिष्टो हावविधिर्मदनालसगावजृम्भितं ललितम् ।

नीविविमोचनबन्धौ मुहुर्मुहुः केशपाशविश्लेषः । स्वाधरदशनग्रहणं बालकपरिचुम्बनं
रतोत्सुकता ॥ ६६३ ॥

साकाङ्गितं चिपन्त्यास्तरलायतलोचने मुहुः काम्ये । उद्दिश्य तद्वयस्यकमिति
शोकग्रस्तवर्णगिरः ॥ ६६४ ॥

एकीभावं गतयोर्जलपयसोर्मित्रचेतसोध तथा । व्यतिरेककृती शक्तिहँसानां
दुर्जनानां च ॥ ६६५ ॥

अतिपेलवमतिपरिमितवर्णं लघुतरमुदाहरति शण्ठः । परमार्थतः स हृदयं वहति पुनः
कालकूटघटितमिव ॥ ६६६ ॥

येन तदा मामूचे परिजनमुत्सार्य विवृतनटमन्युः । दर्शितहितस्वरूपः
परपीडाकरणपण्डितः प्रखलः ॥ ६६७॥

दामोदरगुतविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

अविदितगुरणान्तराणां को दोषः प्रान्तदेशवासानाम् । स्वाधीनकुङ्कुमा अपि

क महीतलरम्भा त्वं न्यक्कृतचन्द्रप्रभा स्वदेहरुचा । चिवलता व बराकी
नीचैरुपसेविता वोटा ॥ ६६६ ॥

यस्यार्थे न विगणिताः प्रह्वात्मानो महाधनाः कुलजाः । सोऽद्य हृदयेन तस्यां
त्वयि तिष्ठति बाह्यवृत्तेन ॥ ७०० ॥

पारिचित्येन ॥ ७०२ ॥

The Baud's Counsel

"What fault can we ascribe to those Who live in border provinces And
therefore cannot recognise The special qualities (of goods), When even
those who have to hand Their saffron value indigo" (698)

""How far apart the two of you! You are nymph Rambha come to earth! Your
body's glow occludes the moonbeam! You are a rooted plantain stem

Wee shame the (dotony) Purple Fleabane! ... And poor, hermaphroditic Citrä,
Surrounded by her train of dwarves, A Scrambling Madder grown by oiks! (699)

The man for whom you've snubhed the rich, The gently bom, the grovelling,
Belongs to her now, in his heart, And only outwardly to you. (200)

"In such a case the shrewd can tell Between the very self-same acts When
done with love or done without, By whether there is care or staleness. (201)

'Now love in you is deep entrenched: The workings of your mind won't rise
To bring themselves to analyse The way he acts, and that is why I have
informed you how things stand, For, after all, we're friends." (702)

words And pangs of jealousy took root That choked my ingrained love. (203)

"For, since her heart is far from stout, When struck by such a thunderbolt

the saddened crook. Won't count as one he's really lived. (207)

मालत्या गुणवत्तां सम्यग्नो वेत्ति मधुकरस्तावत् । अनुभवमेति न

कोमलमानकदर्थों भजमानो भजति दीप्ततामधिकाम् । संचाल्यमानदारुः पावक इव सुप्रभः

The Bated's Counsel

"A "honey-bee' won't fully know How well-endowed his 'Jasmine' is, Until he
has experienced The taste of kissing other 'flowers". (715)

"Just as a radiant fire flates up More brightly when its firewood's stirred
So too does glowing love, provoked By gently needled slighted pride. (716)

"If on the other hand it's stretched Too far in anger's fiery heat It will,
we know, transform and break In pieces, like a bead of glass. (717)

"It's easy, if in need of fees, To service many kinds of mens But one in
whom one's heart may rest Is hard to find in thousands. (718)

"Manu and other honoured seers, To whom past times, the here and now And
future ages are revealed, Can't tell what hoards of merit stored Suffice to
have your lover rush To clutch you in a tight embrace. (719)

"If even when her love appears Again beside her, still she broods On
slights remembered, such a girl Must be, I feel, quite lost to Love, Who's
left with all five arrows blunted. (220)

दामोदरमुमविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

जीव्यत एव कथं चिद्धिग्वृत्तिमिमां महद्भिरवगीताम् । विजहाति यन्न गणिका
तद्वाञ्छितरमणलाभलोभेन ॥ ७२१ ॥

कराटकिनः कटुकरसान् करीरखदिरादिविटपतरुगुल्मान् । उपभुञ्जाना करभी
दैवादाप्नोति मधुरमधुजालम् ॥ ७२२॥

का श्रीरप्रणयिवशा का विलसितयो मनोभवविहीनाः । को धर्मो निरुपशमः किं सौख्यं
वल्लभेन रहितानाम् ॥ ७२३ ॥

स्वाच्छन्द्यफलं बाल्यं तारुण्यं रुचितसुरतभोगफलम् । स्थविरत्वमुपशमफलं
परहितसम्पादनं च जन्मफलम् ॥ ७२४ ॥

अभिदधतीमिदमालीमवकर्य गृहीतयेव भूतेन । यौवनसुखेन सार्धं मयैव यूयं

The Basod's Counsel

"One can contrive to live somehow, But damn this wretched livelihood Which
worthy folk abominate! The reason why a prostitute Won't quit is that she
yearns to find That special swain she craves. (221)

"A camel feeds on thorny plants, On Desert Kair and Catechu And other
bushes, trees and shrubs Of bitter sap; she too, with luck, May stumble on
sweet honeycomb. (723)

"What is our beauty if unclaimed By one who has desire for it! What are our
coquetries, unless They bring upon us someone's love? What is the need of
heeding rules If that won't lead to peace of mind! What happiness is
possible For women dispossessed of lovers! (723)

'Childhood's reward is being free; Youth as reward has savoured sex; Old
age has peace as its reward: And life's reward is helping others." (784)

"After I heard my friend say this, 1 was the one who cut you off, Along
with all the joys of youth, As though some fiend had me in thrall. (225)

"Now I'm ablaze in every limb Enveloped in remorse's flames. My birth seems
void of any goal, I live only in that I breathe. (126)

273

Her friend' remark on life recalled;

She then mall and rue their rift.

दामोदरगुतविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

स्थानेषु येषु युष्मत्संगतया क्रीडितं चिरं धृत्या । तानि खलु वीच्क्षमारणा
भवामि कण्ठस्थितप्राणा ॥ ७२७॥

अन्यवशेन विसंज्ञा कृतभूषा यन्त्रसूत्रसंचारा । दारुमयीव प्रतिमा विदधामि
विडम्बना बह्वीः ॥ ७२८॥

यदि नामोदरभरणप्राप्यै कुरुते ऽन्यपुष्पसंश्लेषम् । तदपि न पुष्टिर्भुङ्गा
अपिबन्त्या आरविन्दमकरन्दम् ॥ ७२६ ॥

आस्तामपरो लोकः क्रीडापेक्षी परापदि प्रीतः । व्यसनार्णवे पतन्ती न वारिता
परिजनेनास्मि ॥ ७३०॥

किं वा बहुभिः कथितैः सम्प्रति नियमे तु नियमिता बुद्धिः । स्थास्यामि
संनियुक्ता भवद्भहे प्रेष्यकार्येषु ॥ ७३१॥

इति नेवादिविकारैर्वशमुपनीतं प्रलीनधैर्याङ्गम् । मारग्रहाभिभूतं
परिमृष्टप्राङनिराकृतिस्मरणम् ॥ ७३२ ॥

प्रादुर्भूतरिरंसं चक्षणे चक्षणे जतुदेशगतदृष्टिम् । पक्काम्रमिव विमोचयसि
पूर्ववदाचूष्य सुभ्रु निःशेषम् ॥ ७३३ ॥

The Band's Counsel

"Now when I see those haunts, of course, In which I dallied happily A while
with you, my life-breath mounts And catches in my throat. (727)

"Controlled by others, vaid of sense, I dress myself in ornaments, And
mimicking my various tasks, I'm like a wooden marionette Mechanically
steered by strings. (228)

"To find some way to fill her belly The bee unites with other flowers But
nonetheless she cannot thrive Unless she quaffs the lotus nectar. (729)

"Forget about the outside world Which looks for sport, and takes delight In
others' pain, even my friends Did not prevent me tumbling down Into a sea
of tribulations. (730)

"But what's the need for many words? I've now decided on a vow: In your
household I shall remain, Engaged in doing servants' chores." (734)

With side-long looks and other plays Above described, entangle him, His
body drained of all defence, Prey to the demon god of love, All memory
wiped of past rebuffs, Exhibiting his prurience. His gaze returning
frequently To somewhere near your collar-bone,

-Treating him like a ripened mango Suck everything you can from him Then,
as before, you'll let him go. My girl with shapely brows! (232-733)

And end by making up her mind To poin hir bourrheld as a maid.

The Bawd's Couniel

Cast out your hook of leering looks, Of veiled hints, of lolling smiles,
Once baited with your body's meat Draw him in senseless, nice and fat, And
not disposed to theesh and flail. (714)

With kindnesses spice up your fish, Once you have caught him in your hands,
Then eat up every bit of flesh And cast him off, just skin and bones. (735)

Hear this, you lavish-buttocked girl, About a certain Manjari, A garland
garnishing the feet Of Siva Kálašéśvará The child of guru Právará, And how
she gorged upon a prince, And spat him out again. (296)

The tale of Manjari begins

555

There lived a king called Simhabhara, The very best among the mighty, His
son (whose name was Samara) Commanded Dévarăştta's camp. (737)

Prince Samará it introduced.

ष्ठरा

One day he came along to see The God whose banner is the ball A few close
friends attended him, Whose dress and manners bore the stamp Of
supercilious youth. (738)

The Band Counsel

A large kerchief tied back his hair And covered one third of his head. He
wore some see-through body oil And sideburns thickly smeated with saffron.
(239)

The decoration on his brow Was stippled with white mustard seeds And he was

necklace, wrought To look like little lapwings." (740)

shooting glittering shafts. (741)

Swathed in the very softest silks, He held a bamboo swagger-stick, And at
his hips, attired in wool, He wore a dagger and a sword. His footwear could
be recognised By noisy creaking sounds. (742)

दामोदरमुतविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

दर्शयति दिशः फलिता अमृतगभस्ति करे ऽवतारयति । सुरदेवि चन्द्रवर्मा
निर्वस्तुकवाक्प्रपञ्चेन ॥ ७४४ ॥

त्वामनुयान्तं सम्प्रति पश्यामि कुरङ्गि वसुषेणम् । सुनिरूपिता भविष्यसि विषमा
गुडजिह्विका तस्य ॥ ७४५॥

चर्वयति जलं यो ऽसौ हरिणि हरो धूर्तताभिमानेन । लिखति शतं दशवृद्ध्या स
निमग्नस्तरलिकावर्ते ॥ ७४६ ॥

गृह्णासि यत्पटान्ते मम पश्यत एव नन्द मदिराख्याम् । अत आवयोरवश्यं मा

The Bared's Counsel

By keeping up a vow of silence, Inspiriting religious faith In each and
every Vaisnava, This votary of Vişnu's writ, Pretending here to visit...
Siva, Ogles the women artfully While dodging catching others' eyes! That
plaster saint, Kumudini, Must satisfy my inclinations. (748-750)

"He's looking when he's not observed And when he's seen he looks away.
Esurient, he mutters something. But when he's spoken to himself, His vocal
powers are paralysed. He cannot leave her side at all, Nor bear to stand
where they might touch! This man appears, it seems to me, Concupiscent and
clueless." (731-754)

"Those days, we know, are over now, In which I billed and cooed with you;
Now you're Madame la professeur, Because of your relationship With
Bhabuddha the guru." (753)

"You can meander as you please, Lólikadäsa, clutching girls As though you
might be picking flowers, Till Naradevi can contrive To have you enter in
her noose." (754)

Tor Band's Counsel

The temple seethed with rakes and molls Engaged in banter of this kind;
Some skilled attendants, sent ahead, Cleared them aside to make a path.
(255)

Once he'd accomplished Siva's worship, The crowd was ruthlessly restrained
With lathis: hurried lackeys placed A chair, and Sámara sat down. (756)

A group of dancers, flautists, singers And courtesans sat down before him,
And he was offered paan and flowers, And scented unguents by merchants
Headed by foremen of the guilds. (737)

With trusted bodyguards behind, Who held their cutlasses unsheathed, And
someone with a rapier And variously embellished quoits, He wasn't
unaccompanied. (28)

His paan-box bearer, using tongs, Proffered his betel preparation: On
taking it in his left hand, Shaped as to hold an arrow's flights." He
gently kneaded it a little. (259)

दामोदरसुभविरचित बुट्टनीमतम्

एष विशेषः स्पष्टो वलेश्व त्वत्प्रतापवलेश्व ।

ददतो वाञ्छितमर्थ सदानुरक्तस्य तव गृहे त्यक्त्वा ।

स्त्रीचापलेन कीति-र्नग्नासक्ता गत्ता ककुभः ॥ ७६७॥

भवतः भवतो धैर्य तेन हि भिन्नों ऽन्धकः प्रणतः ।

मुक्तास्त्वया तु बहवो रिपवो ऽपि प्रेक्षकाः समरे ॥ ७६८ ॥

दामोदरमुभविरचितं कुहनीमतम्

अटता जगतीमखिलामिदमाश्वर्यं मया दृष्टम् । धनदो ऽपि नयननन्दन परिहरसि

इदमपरमद्भूततमं युवतिसहखैर्विलुप्यमानस्य । वृद्धिर्भवति न हानिर्यत्तव
सौभाग्यकोशस्य ॥ ७७० ॥

अपरं विस्मयजननं धवलत्वं नापयाति यद्भवतः । ललनालोचनकुवलयदलत्विषा शबलितस्यापि
॥ ७७१ ॥

तेन विश्वरूपो ऽसि ॥ ७७२ ॥

कि वहसि वृथा गर्वं प्रियो ऽहमिति योषितां नराधीश । काङ्खन्ति स्म मुरारिं

दानैकनिषस्पहृदयेन ॥ ७७४ ॥

The Batod's Counsel

"In all my travels round the world, This is the strangest thing I've serns
That you, a pleasure to one's eyes, A veritable God of Wealth, Should shun
the company of Siva.... (Not) steer away from criminals." (769)

"Another most unusual point Is that the trove of your allure Appears to wax
and not to wane Although a thousand girls deplete it. (770)

"A further thing astonishes: Your fait complexion doesn't dim Though
brushed with darkling radiance Of women's lily-petal eyes. (771)

"Because, although you're only one. You live in many lovers' hearts, O Spur
of Love! O Kama's sire! O Best of Men! O Supreme Soul! You are an
all-pervading Visna." (772)

"Why do you vainly pride yourself On being popular with women! Full sixteen
thousand cowherd girls, O lord of men, once yearned for Kisna. (273)

"Since Visnu took on brahmin form When Bali had his sacrifice And went to
plead for alms from him. He an't on a par with you,

Whuse beart is pained to have to give...!? hase heart is pained to have to
take...! Wase heart is fixed an saking only...? (Not clear what this should
mean perhaps-) Whose heart is given to giving only. (774)

The Batod's Counsel

"You are exactly like a cloud: In altitude, it rests above The mountains:
you're above all kings, From every creature it removes The pain of thirst:
you take away The tribulations of desire, It's capable of bringing water:
When are you not just what is needed? (Or should we understand instead:)
When are you like a cloud? You klute! (775)

"The Ganges water flowing by In billows down its many paths, Hugging the
contours of the land, Mightily cutting through the hills, Can vie with you
in purity And nothing else (for you are not) A man of many ways and wiler,
Much balked, bent on dishonesty. Adept at stirring family strife! (Or
should that mean that you are not) A man of many stratagems, Exempt from
failure, ill-depand To fraudulence, as capabic As Indra on the battlefield
(776)

"The origin of villainy, The fountainhead of foolishness, A reservoir of
judgement's dearth-This age of Kali yew produced, The one successful
connoisseur Of vice! (Unless we split the words Again and read:) You are
alone The one trut pandit, whe've transformed This Kali-age to golden Kita,
In which all litigation grounds Have been made narie, and foolishness Has
been expelled, and judgement reigns. (777)

The Band's Counsel

"You for whom Siva is supreme, O quiddity of all that's worldly?! You

been talk Of best of manly qualities! There too are meta-rules, subile you

The Bated's Counsel

"My lord, you dignify the earth Wish natural sterling qualities, Even soben

The Bawd's Counsel

"It's dreadful that I have to praise you, Whom even Raksasas outclan..?
It's dreadfully inadequate, My praise of you, unparallelled Even by Ravana,
for he Never would show a woman force, While you do rape your rivali'
wives! While you by force appropriate Your adversaries' wealthy splendour.
(786)

"This accolade of flatteries And winsome words, for which I'm paid, Touches
upon your real nature. I'm off. Respect! And all the best!" (787)

said "But wait a while! Why be on edge? You shall depart when sent away By
me when I've sewarded you. (788)

"Recite that couplet once again Which you recited earlier When you were
hidden in some nook And I was in a lady's chamber." (789)

"In uttering these words of praise You have unlocked my power to speak
Before this learned gathering!" (The bard.) on saying this, declaimed With
flawless orotundity In all three registers of voice: (790)

The Baud's Counsel

"One lady, jilted, is enraged; Another's lost her appetite From having her
requests turned down; Another is downhearted. Why! She hasn't got a seat
beside him. (791)

"One is estranged, after a tiff;

Another's crushed by bashfulness, Unused to her new wedded state. What can
he do, crazed in his lust, The spouse of all too many wives Surrounded by
his host of beauties!" (794)

With eyebrows raised, wobbling his head, Sámara showed that he approved. He
then addressed the dancing-master: "So, are performances here good!" (793)

To which the latter then replied: "Where theatre-patrons live from trade,
And actresses are prostitutes -Embodiments of perfidy-How can the plays be
any good? (794)

"Some beefcake bullies one of them, One cannot leave her handsome beau;
Another lets the day go by With boyfriends in some drinking dive. (295)

The dancing

master belluaches About his problems staging plays.

दामोदरगुप्भविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

नोत्सृजति सततमेका पुरुषागमनाशया गृहद्वारम् । शूलापालः कथयति लब्धोत्कोचो
रजस्वलामपराम् ॥ ७६६ ॥

रङ्गगतापि चुद्रा शृणोति यदि परिचितं गृहायातम् । उद्दिश्य वारिकार्य व्रजति
ततः प्रकृतमुत्सृज्य ॥ ७६७ ॥

आ तारुण्योद्भेदात्कान्ते दृष्टिर्ययाभ्यस्ता । सामाजिकमध्यस्था सा कथमन्यासु
याति परभागम् ॥ ७६८ ॥

चेतोवशिता सत्त्वं सत्त्वे सति चारुता प्रयोगस्य । न भवति सा वेश्यानां
मद्यामिषपुरुषनिहितहृदयानाम् ॥ ७६६ ॥

वयमपि देवनिकेतनमनङ्गहर्षे गते विदशलोकम् । आश्रितवन्तो ऽगत्या
तीर्थस्थानोपरोधेन ॥ ८०० ॥

305

"A fourth one cannot leave her door Because she constantly expects Some man
to come, and of a fifth, Her pimp, who has received a bribe, Explains she's
at 'that monthly time'. (796)

"One floozy, if she should find our Some man she knows has reached her

water, Then drop her rôle and walk away. (797)

amongst the male spectators! (198)

this is something prostitutes Do not possess: their hearts are fixed On
alcohol and meat and men. (299)

"As for myself, now Harsa's gone (The author of that wondrous verse
Rarnávali 1.22), To join the gods in their abode, Because I have no other
haven, And other holy sites were full," I've taken refuge in this temple.
(Boo)

दामोदरसुमविरचितं कुट्टनीमत्तम्

इह तु कदा चित्ति चिद्वृत्तिनिरोधाभिशङ्कया निरुत्साहाः । रत्नावल्यामेता
विदधति करपादविक्षेपम् ॥ ८०१ ॥

वत्सेशभूमिकास्या इयमनुकुरुते नरेश्वरवयस्यम् । वासवदत्ताचरितप्रयोगमेषा
विडम्बयति ॥ ८०२॥

उद्यमसाहित्यवशाच्छोभातिशयेन मदनुबन्धेन । अनया प्रसिद्धिराप्ता
सिंहलराजात्मजानुकृतौ ॥ ८०३ ॥

विविधस्थानकरचनां परिक्रमं गाववलनलालित्यम् काकुविभक्तार्थगिरी रसपुष्टिं
वासनास्थैर्यम् ॥ ८०४ ॥

सात्त्विकभावोन्मीलनमभिनयमनुरूपवर्तनाभरणम् । मिश्रामिथे वाद्ये लयाच्युति
वर्णयन्ति मञ्जर्याः ॥ ८०५ ॥

The Band's Coumel

"And here these girls, without much seal, Because they fear their trade
might flag. Occasionally throw about Their hands and feet a little bit To
act the play Racsával, (301)

"The rôle this lady takes is (King Udayana,) the Vatsas Lord; This lady
plays (Vasantaká, Jester,) companion of the king: This one impersonates
(his queen And senior wife) Väsavadátta. (803)

"Because she has such energy. Because her beauty's exquisite. Because she
follows what I teach, Celebrity has been attained By this one here, for
acting out The King of Lanka's daughter's part (Ratnavali, the heroine).
(803)

"When people speak of Manjari, They comment on the way she shapes Her
varied postures, how she walks, The grace in movements of her limbs, The
intonation of her speech That nuances the sense that's meant, The way she
amplifies the mood, The firmness of the mental world That she projects, her
calling op The body's outward signs of feeling. Mimetic gestures, omaments
And maquillage that fit the bill, Trueness of pitch when singing songs As
solos or with instruments." (804-803)

The Bawd's Counsel

"The moment when she sees the king Of Vatsa through an aperture Among the
Sinduväta trees, With eyes in which infatuation. Has burgeoned suddenly to
blossom, And when his name (Udayaná) Is uttered, quelling her belief That
this was Love made flesh again, She steals away from viewers' minds -Whose
mouths are filled with cries of 'Bravo!"-The power to differentiate Her art
and what it imitates. (806-807)

"Sketching the Lord of Vatsa's form, The hand she moves is tremulous, As
gradually she manifests Each stage of love, with shiverings, With
perspiration, hairs on end. (808)

"When she is teeteting on the brink Of death by hanging, then she shows The
tragic mood, void of all hope, Distinguished from the mood of pain That
sundered lovers feel, although The groups of symptomatic signs In both
those moods are similar." (809)

While Manjari was thus described, The prince surveyed her lustfully, Then,
with his bamboo swagger-stick, He tapped her, asking "Is this her!" (810)

दामोदरगुमविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

बुद्धाथ तस्य भावं प्रसारयन्युवतिसंकथाकेलिम् । न्यक्ङ्कुर्वन्वारवधूः सचिवः
प्रशशंस बन्धकीगमनम् ॥ ८११॥

दाररतिः संततये व्याधिप्रशमाय चेटिकाश्लेषः । तत्खलु सुरतं सुरतं
कृच्छ्रप्राप्यं यदन्यनारीषु ॥ ८१२ ॥

स्वव्यापारैकमतेः परचिन्ता नास्ति मे कदा चिदपि । पश्यन्त्यास्त्वामीदृशमद्य
तु मे मानसं व्यथितम् ॥ ८१३ ॥

लगुडैः संचूर्णयिष्यामि ॥ ८१४॥

कृतं वन्ध्यजन्मफलम् ॥ ८१५॥

The Rawd's Counsel

His comrade (standing at his side), Who'd ascertained his state of mind,
Expanded then, in playful vein, Upon the subject of young women And,

dissipated wives. (81)

555

"Sex with one's wife's for procreation; Rolls in the hay with servant-girls
Can serve to lay to rest the itch; But, as we know, the real sex, The sex
that really earns its name, And which is difficult to find, Is had with
wives of other men. (812)

"'Because I tend to concentrate Uniquely on my own affairs, I never care a
whit for others, But seeing you in such a state My heart today is ill at
case. (813)

'If I knew where that Brahma lived, And if my strength were more than his,
Then I would go and pummel him-That pesky god of fate!-with sticks. (814)

O lissome thing, that fool Creator! If he could make your limbs thus
matchless, Then why predestine them to life Without a fitting male admirer
And thus devoid of purpose? (815)

"'It might be fine, were you a child, Decrepit, ill, soon doomed to die;
But when you've shapeliness and youth, They shouldn't wither trapped inside
A feckless husband's disregard. (816)

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

केलिः प्रदहति मज्जां शृङ्गारो (स्थीनि चाटवः कटुकाः । न करोति मनस्तुष्टिं
दानमभव्यस्य गृहभर्तुः ॥ ८१७ ॥

कुत आगतासि कस्मिन्वेलामियतीं स्थिता किमर्थमिति । पृच्छन्नस्वस्थमना जनयति
गेही शिरः शूलम् ॥ ८१८॥

यदि भवति दैवयोगाच्चक्षुविषये समुज्ज्वलस्तरुरणः । तवात्मानं क्षपयति जायां च
रटन् गृहस्वामी ॥ ८१६ ॥

सविवादे परलोके जनापवादे ऽपि जगति बहुचिते । देवाधीने प्रलये न विदग्धा
हारयन्ति तारुण्यम् ॥ ८२० ॥

दुर्भर्तृकरास्फालनमलिनीक्रियमाणशोभमनुदिवसम् । तुङ्गमपि पतितकल्पं स्तनशालिनि
तव पयोधरद्वन्द्वम् ॥ ८२१॥

पर्यङ्कः स्वास्तरणः पतिरनुकूलो मनोहरं सदनम् । तुल्यन्ति न लक्षांशं
त्वरिततरणचौर्यसुरतस्य ॥ ८२२ ॥

817 The first half of this verse agrees (with some variation) with
Pancatantra, techwim plizier, 1. 175ab. 820 ab Pañcatantra, the simplicior,
1. 180ab (with variants) Baa abPasicatantra, textas semplicer, 1. 174ab
(with variants). The whole verse is quoted as Damodaragupta's in the
Subhasitavali (2144). In the Sarigadhanapaddhati (3766) the second half of
the verse agrees with the version found in the Fuscatantra.

""Canoodling with a worthless spouse Scorches one's marrow, being romanced
Bums up the bones; and badinage Turns bitter: gifts don't thrill the heart.
(817)

Where have you come from! Where've you beenf Why did you stay for such a
time! By catechising you this way, A nervous spouse induces migraines. (818)

""And if by chance some toothsome youth Should stray across your field of
view, Your husband will torment himself, And also you, his wife, with
yelling. (819)

"The world hereafter lan't sure, And gossips in this world of ours Don't
cast their blame alike, the end Of days depends on fate-and so Wise girls
don't throw their youth away. (820)

""Your pair of breasts, my buxom peach -Whose loveliness your rotten
husband Daily pollutes with chafing hands-Though lofty still, now seem to
slump. (821)

A well-made bed, a pliant spouse, A pleasing home these things aren't worth
A millionth part of one brief bout Of urgent, furtive fornication. (Baa)

दामोदरसुभविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

सहसा संकटवर्त्मन्यवितर्कितसम्मुखागतेन विशा ।
अभिलषितेनोद्धष्टकमनल्पशुभकर्मरणा लभ्यम् ॥ ८२३॥

प्रीतिः किल निरतिशया स्वर्गः परलोकचिन्तकैर्गदितः । तस्यास्तु जन्मलाभो
हृदयेप्सितपुरुषसंभोगात् ॥ ८२४ ॥

अतटस्थस्वादुफलग्रहणव्यवसायनिश्चयो येषाम् । ते शोकक्लेशरुजां केवलमुपयान्ति
पावतां मन्दाः ॥ ८२५॥

कि प्रतिकूला ग्रहगतिरुत परिणतमन्यजन्मश्चरितम् । स्वानुष्ठानाभ्यसनं कि वा
तस्यात्मयोनिहतकस्य ॥ ८२६ ॥

येन तपस्वी स युवा स्तौति समीरं त्वदङ्गसंस्पृष्टम् । त्वत्पादाक्रान्तभुवे
स्पृहयति ककुभं त्वदाश्रितां नमति ॥ ८२७ ॥

एकाग्रीकृतचेतास्त्वत्संगतसौख्यसिद्धिमभिकाङ्गन् ॥ ८२८ ॥

The Baud's Counsel

""When, on a narrow path, you feel A man whom you desire brush past, Who's
suddenly come face to face Without a warning- oh what heaps Of saintly
karma mast accrue To let you take that prize! (823)

*"Heaven, it seems, is happiness Beyond compare, so say those men Who
ponder worlds that follow ours; In fact such bliss is realised By love with
one the heart desires. (824)

"Such foolish folk who've made resolve To stay away from sweet ripe fruit
That lies to hand... (Or should that means Those foolish folk wbe've made
resolve To strive to harvest sweet ripe fruit Beyond their reach!) deserve
just pain And suffering and sorrow. (821)

'Is it some baleful planet's path, Or crimes from other births now ripe, Or
is it just that wretched Brahmä, Going about his usual works, That makes
this stricken youth caress The breeze that's brushed across your limbs,
Desire the ground your foot has trod, And bow his head in your direction,
And meditate upon your form, Intone the string of syllables That form your
name; control his thoughts To focus on a single point. Yearning to win that
yogic goal Of happiness beside you? (826-828)

316

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं बुद्धनीमतम्

उत्सृष्टसकलकार्यस्तिर्यग्ग्रीवं विलोकयन्भवतीम् । कुरुते गृहाग्ररथ्यां
यातायातैः शतावर्ताम् ॥ ८२६ ॥

दृष्टो ऽसि तया सुचिरं गेहाभ्याशे परिभ्रमन्स्पृहया । संदेश एष दत्तः
प्राभृतमेतत्तव प्रहितम् ॥ ८३० ॥

शुष्यति सालभमाना भवत्कृते वेश्मनिर्गमावसरम् । इति चतुरशठस्त्रीभिर्विलुप्यते
त्वदपदेशेन ॥ ८३१॥

कि वा कथितैरधिकैरस्थानाविष्टचेतसस्तस्य । अनुतिष्ठ यथायुक्तं त्वत्तो नाशश्व
जीवरक्षा च ॥ ८३२ ॥

कुलपतनं जनगर्दा नरकगति प्राणितव्यसंदेहम् । अङ्गीकरोति तत्क्षणमबला
परपुरुषमभियान्ती ॥ ८३३॥

स तु लिखति दासपवं त्यजति कुटुम्बं ददाति सर्वस्वम् । यावन्न भवति पुरतः
परयुवतिः प्रोज्झितावरणा ॥ ८३४ ॥

317

*"Neglecting all his usual tasks, Craning his neck to gape at you, And
pacing ever back and forth Before your house he makes the street A
whirlpool of a hundred gyres. (829)

""Some scheming, ill-willed women say, To ruin him, on your account: 1
She's seen you wandering about Outside the house for quite a while,
Desiringly, and gives you this, A gift, and sends this message. Finding no
chance to leave the house To get to you, she wilts away. (810-831)

He's fixed unsuitably on you. Whether he dies or stays alive Depends on
what you do. (832)

"A member of the weaker sex

Who turns towards another man At once incurs her family's fall, The world's
reproach, rebirth in hell, And doubt about her very life. (833)

his family. And giving up his worldly wealth, Until he sees that married
woman Unclothed, in front of him. (834)

"Speaking on your be half, to steal him", in which case the female
messenger's scheen would be to tell the young man that the lady pining for
him is unavailable, in order then to lure him away for hemelf.

दामोदरगुभविरचितं कुड़नीमतम्

दृष्टं यदृष्टव्यं व्यपयातं कौतुकं विदितमन्तः । इति याति मनसि कृत्वा
विहितविधेयस्ततस्तूर्णम् ॥ ८३५ ॥

सापि च्छिन्ना च्छोटनगृहीतमुक्ता विलोकयन्त्याशाः । विशति गृहं संत्रस्ता
सर्वत आशङ्किता सवैलच्या ॥ ८३६ ॥

नवचारिवभ्रंशात्सुरचितकुलटोत्तरेषु नो निपुरणा । पृष्टा व गतासि त्वं न क्व
चिदिति सम्भ्रमाद् बूते ॥ ८३७ ॥

एते दोषा बहवः पुरुषा अपि चपलकौतुकाः प्रायः । त्वं च ग्रहेण लग्ना
कार्यविमूढाव तिष्ठामि ॥ ८३८ ॥

इति दोलागतहृदया स्थिरीकृताभ्यस्तकर्मणा दूत्या । दृष्टेति शङ्कमाना पदे पदे
चलति पर्यो ऽपि ॥ ८३६ ॥

शतगुणितमनोरथाकृष्टा ॥ ८४०॥

The Baud's Counsel

'Once what he had to do is done, He thinks The thing I had to see Is seen.
My curiosity Is spent: I know, and quickly leaves. (835)

"She, on the other hand, debased, Used and discarded in a snap. Enters her
house in trepidation, Looking around in each direction, Aware that all
suspect, ashamed. (836)

"New to her fallen ways, unskilled In trotting out the well-wrought lines
Adulteresses know to use, Upon being asked Where have you been? She
answers, flustered, Nowhere. (817)

"These are the many stumbling blocks, And men, to speak in general terms,
Are fickle in their interests; Yet you have got this idée fixe! I stand
here at a loss to act. (838)

555

"Thus in her heart she vacillates; The go-between, who knows her business,
Emboldens her with every step, If just a single leaf is rustled, She has
the feeling: I've been seen'. (819)

"Casting her fearful, trembling eyes Repeatedly on every side, Drawn by
desires now magnified A hundred times, she makes her way To reach the
chosen rendez-vous, (840)

भयशृङ्गारव्रीडामिश्रीभूतानुभावसन्दोहम् । जनयन्ती
लोलांशुकदृष्टादृष्टांसकुचनाभिः ॥ ८४१ ॥

नीवीष्वथनारम्भं निरुन्धती न न न यामि यामीति । निभृतास्फुटाभिधानैः

322

दामोदरशुभविरचितं कुट्ट‌नीमतम्

न कृता चरिवरक्षा न च भुक्तं त्वच्छरीरमपयन्वम् । दृष्टादृष्टभ्रष्टा क यामि
कि वा करोमि दुर्जाता ॥ ८४७॥

अवगुण्ठनविनयरति स्वैरालपनं सुमन्दसंचारम् । सम्प्रति मम पापायाः करपिहितमुखा
हसन्ति भूमिज्ञाः ॥ ८४८ ॥

यासामासीत्सख्यं मया समं समवयः कुलस्त्रीरणाम् । ता वारयन्ति मत्तः कुसङ्ग इति
तन्नियन्तारः ॥ ८४॥

धिग्वादान्परिजनतः सहमाना मन्युरोधनतवदना । तिष्ठामि निरभिमाना
स्वविनिर्मितदोषदौर्बल्यात् ॥ ८५० ॥

सद्धिविधीयमानं प्रसङ्गपतितं पतिव्रतास्तवनम् । हृदयेन दूयमाना मूडा सीदामि
शृण्वन्ती ॥ ८५१॥

ग्रासन्न उपविशन्तीं मन्दाक्षान्मां निषेद्मसमर्थाः । अन्योन्यमीच्क्षमाणा
ज्ञातिजनाः संकुचन्ति भुञ्जानाः ॥ ८५२ ॥

The Band's Cownsel

"My reputation isn't saved; Your body I could not enjoy. Without restraint.
I've heen deprived Of this world and the next one too. Ah wretched me!
Where should I go! And anyway, what should I dof (847)

"They're laughing now, the worldly-wise, Who know I'm acting out a rile.
Hiding their mouths behind their hands, At my desire for modesty Shown by
the veiling of my head, Or when my diction is subdued, Or when I walk
sedately. (848)

Those well-born ladies of my age Who used to be my friends before Are
warned away from me today: Bad company!, their minders say. (849)

With face turned down to curb my tage. I stand enduring obloquy From those
around me, robbed of pride, Because I've made myself so weak By my mistaken
actions. (850)

"When upright folk by chance describe The virtues of a faithful wife, With
agitated heart, downcast, Confused, I only listen. (851)

"If while they eat I sit nearby My relatives, discomfited, Do not know how
to fend me off: Exchanging looks, they shrink away. (852)

प्रकटीकृता त्वयैव चारणमात्रममुञ्चता गृहोपान्तम् । अस्मासु दृशं मग्नां
प्रेमस्निग्धामनुद्धरता ॥ ८५३ ॥

परगृहविनाशपिशुनाः सुभगंमन्याभिरूप्यकृतदर्पाः । कृकलासतुल्यरागा भवन्ति
युष्मद्विधा एव ॥ ८५४ ॥

अनभीष्टव्यवहारप्रभवशुचा पीडिताच्क्षरा इत्थम् । सोपालम्भा विजने धन्याः

पुरारणतृणतुल्याः ॥ ८५६ ॥

उपनमति रतिमहोत्सव आराधितदेवताविशेषारणाम् । वचनमपि प्रेमाई स्वैरिण्याः
श्रवणमेति पुण्यवताम् ॥ ८५७ ॥

"You were the one who showed me up: You never left the area, Even an

valueless as withered grass. (856)

too a wanton woman's words, Warm with affection, reach the ears Of those
who've stored up merit. (857)

yogic trance, she touches them Upon the marrow! How much more Must she
affect a wretch in thrall To objects of the senses! (858)

330

दामोदरगुमविरचितं चुट्टनीमतम्

विनिवार्य तं प्रवर्तितवाक्यविकासं नतोत्तमाङ्गेन । श्रीसिंहभटस्य सुतं समुवाच
वचो ऽथ नर्तकाचार्यः ॥ ८७५ ॥

नायकभूमौ भरतः कुशीलवाः कोहलादयो मुनयः । अप्सरसः स्त्रीनाट्ये गान्धर्वः
कमलजन्मनस्तनयः ॥ ८७६ ॥

सुषिरस्वरप्रयोगप्रतिपादनपण्डितः मतङ्गमुनिः । यदि रञ्जयन्ति हृदयं भवतो
भूमिस्पृशां कुतः शक्तिः ॥ ८७७ ॥

अभ्यधिकं धृष्टत्वं प्रायेण हि शिल्पजीविनां भवति । आधितनर्तकवृत्तेविशेषतो
विजितरङ्गस्य ॥ ८७८ ॥

विज्ञापयाम्यतस्त्वां निर्मितनाट्ये प्रजासृजा सदृशम् । अवलोकयाङ्कमेकं मा
भवतु मम श्रमो वन्ध्यः ॥ ८७६ ॥

The Band's Counsel

The dancing master shook his head To stop this flow of speech she'd
launched, And then to Simhabháta's son Addressed these words. (875)

"If Bharata in hero's guise, And Kohala and other seers Taking the other
actors' parts, Celestial nymphs in female rôles And Närada as vocalist,
Matanga, skilled with oaten stop -If only these will please your heart,
What can our earthbound troupe achieve! (876-877)

"Excessive brazenness, of course, Is not untypical of those Who live by
art-especially so In those who choose the actor's life And gain success
upon the stage. (878)

"That is the reason I request That you, who're similar to Brahmä -Creating
here dramatic art-Should deigo to watch a single act; Don't let our efforts
be in vain 1" (879)

Just as he spoke these words, an eyebrow Raised by the prince commanded
him; Once all the orchestra was set, He cued the leading actor, (880)

334

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं हनीमउम्

वांशिकदत्तस्थानक उद्घाहितभिन्नपञ्चमे सम्यक् । प्रावेशिक्या ध्रुवया
द्विपदीग्रहरणान्तरे ऽविशत्सूवी ॥ ८८१ ॥

उत्साहभावयुक्तः सामाजिकहृदयरञ्जनं कुर्वन् ।
कविनैपुणवत्सेश्वरचरितस्वविधेयदाक्ष्यसामय्या ॥ ८८२ ॥

अष्टकलापरिमाणां ध्रुवां परिक्रम्य ताललययुक्ताम् । आहूय नहीं कृत्वा तया समं
स्वगृहकार्यसंलापम् ॥ ८८३ ॥

सूचितपात्रागमनः किं चिद्दत्त्वा पदानि ललितानि । निश्वक्राम गृहिण्या सार्धं
निःसरणगीतेन ॥ ८८४ ॥

आथित्य कथोद्धातं प्रविवेश ततः सविस्मयो ऽमात्यः । दुर्घटसंघटनेन
क्षितिनाथस्योदयेन मुदितश्व ॥ ८८५॥

प्रासादमारुहन्तं कुसुमायुधपर्वचर्चरीं द्रष्टुम् । निर्दिश्य वत्सनाथं
समनन्तरकार्यसिद्धये निरगात् ॥ ८८६ ॥

The Bawd's Counel

The flautist sounds the solfa 'do', It's taken up, combined with 'sol", In
tune, and with the entrance song A duple time picks up, whereat, There
enters then the leading actor. (88%)

Full of enthusiasm he worked A charm upon the warchers' hearts By blending
in a single cause The poet's skill, the history Of Vatsa's king, and
artistry In practising his own profession. (882)

He paces through the introit Of rhythmic beats in eight-beat time Then
calls the leading lady in And talks about their home affairs." (883)

He made it known who'd next appear Among the characters, and then Danced
with his wife some graceful steps, Then left the stage while someone sang A
song of parting (from the curtain). (884)

Launching the play in such a way That plot and prologue interlink, Enter
the minister, amased At some anlikely incident That turned out in a happy
way, And joyful at the king's success. (885)

He indicates the King of Vatsa Ascending to a roof-top tertace To watch the
dance of Kama's feast-day, Then leaves upon some urgent business. (886)

Act the first of Rárnavali

दामोदरसुतविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

अथ विशति स्म नरेन्द्रः प्रासादगतः समं वयस्येन । अवलोकयन्प्रमोदं
प्रमुदितचेताः स्वसौख्यसम्पत्त्या ॥ ८८७ ॥

विस्मयभावाकृष्टः प्रोत्फुल्लविलोचने ततो विसृजन् । नृत्यति पौरजनौघे प्रोवाच
वयस्य पश्य पश्येति ॥ ८८८ ॥

तुल्यशिशुतरुणवृद्धं समगुप्तागुप्तयुवतिपरिवेष्टम् । अगणितवाच्यावाच्यं
क्रीडति जनता प्रवृद्धहर्षरसा ॥ ८८६ ॥

गावायाससमुत्थितबहुनिःश्वासप्रकीर्णपदगीतम् ॥ ८६० ॥

तूर्यरवव्यामिथितकरतालैरुद्भूजं प्रनृत्यन्तम् । मुहरपि जातस्खलनं
संदर्शितदार्थसौष्ठवं स्थविरम् ॥ ८६१ ॥

मदवशात्तरुणी ॥ ८६२ ॥

The Baud's Counsel

Then there appears upon the stage The king, together with his friend: They
watch the revels from the roof; His thoughts are ones of exaltation At
happinesses he's attained. (887)

Moved by a sense of wonderment, Casting his widened eyes across A sea of
dancing citizens, He says: "Look, look, my friend! (888)

"Children, youths and the old alike, And girls, behaving all the same,
Whether they're cloistered or unveiled, Unmindful of what should be said
And what should not-with rampant joy The people whirl about in play. (889)

"They're powdered red with coloured dusts, And have in teadiness arrays Of
heady flowery perfumes, Singing their songs with muddled words While
panting from their limbs" exertions." (890)

"Let spring be always just like this: A time when loved ones freely kiss!"
So sings that tipsy girl, and gives A forceful hug to that old gent Who's
showing off his stamina Dancing about but staggering Repeatedly, with hands
raised up. And clapping to the sound of pipes. (891-892)
