दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं बुद्धनीमतम्

भग्ने लज्जासेतौ पर्वावसरेरण कुलवधूवदनात् । अश्लीलोक्तिजलौघे नियति केन
वार्यते प्रसरः ॥ ८६४ ॥

तुल्यव्यापारगिरां ललनानां देवनप्रसक्तानाम् । आर्यानार्यावगमं वदनावृतिजालिका

The Bated' Counsel

"A naughty youth, being struck by jets Of water shot from her syringe At
random in that matron's fun, Reckons himself in luck. (893)

"When once the dam of shame's been barst And floods of ribald speech flow
forth -Occasioned by the carnival-From cultivated ladies' mouths, Can
anybody stem the surge? (894)

"Of all these women bent on fun, Their gestures and their speech the same,
Only the veil that hides the face Can tell the ladies from the rest." (89)

The king's companion then points out Two servant girls who enter, sent At
Väsavadatra's behest, Dancing and miming messily. With tippsily tottering
feet. (896)

When what they mean to show are darts (Of Kama), all they sign in mime Is
"lotus-twirling", then, with eyes That shine with valour, they display The
sign for "any sort of weapon". (897)

Turning his gaze on them, the king, Beguiled by curiosity Exclaimed "How
vehement the dance Of these delightful ladies!" (898)

दामोदरगुतविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

करपीडनोपमर्दव्यतिकरसमये कदर्थ्यमानो ऽपि । स्तनमण्डले स्थितो ऽहं त्वं
पुनराकृष्य कुत्र चित्क्षिप्तः ॥ ८६६ ॥

अधुनान्तरयसि मामिति कोपादिव वारवाणमभिरामम् । बहुचिवपदन्यासैर्वल्गन्त्या
हन्ति हार उच्छलितः ॥ ६००॥

चूतलता धम्मिल्लस्थानच्युतशेखरं दधौ श्लाघ्यम् । अधृत पतन्निर्यूहां न त्वेषा
मदनिका वेणीम् ॥ ६०१॥

स्तनभारावनतस्य प्रतनोर्मध्यस्य नास्ति ते ऽपेक्षा । इत्थमिव पादलग्नौ
क्रीडन्त्या नूपुरौ रसतः ॥ १०२॥

वहति स्म यं नितम्बं कथमपि कृच्छ्रेण मन्दसंचारा । कलयति तं तूललघु जयति
मनोजन्मनो महिमा ॥ १०३ ॥

उदयनसमनुज्ञातो नर्नर्ति वसन्तको ऽपि मुदितात्मा । हास्यत्त्रपाभिरामं
चर्चरितालेन तन्मध्ये ॥ ६०४ ॥

The Band's Counsel

340

"(Quoth necklace:) "Though I'm treated rough By hands that knead while
making love, I still remain upon her breasts, While you are snatched away
and cast Indifferently aside. (899)

*'But now it's you who're in the way!" Thus speaks the necklace as it
strikes The shapely bodice furiously, Or so it seems, as various steps
Stamped by the vaulting dancer shake The necklace up and down. (goo)

"Cütalatá has deftly kept Her chaplet on, though slipping down From her
coiffure; Madáníka, In contrast, couldn't hold in place Her braid from
which the crest keeps slipping. (901)

"The anklets jingle on her legs At every leap, as though to say: Your heavy
breasts are bowing down Your slender waist: do you not caref (902)

"Her buttocks, which she used to shift With difficulty, moving slow. She
treats as if as light as fluff. How marvellous the power of Käma!" (903)

The King permits Vasantaki To dance away delightedly, Between the pair, to
carcari, Combining in a charming way-Both jollity and shyness. (904)

342

दामोदरगुमविरचितं कुड्नीसतम्

धीरोद्धतललितपदैः क्रीडित्वा ते चिराय नरनाथम् । प्रद्योतस्य सुतायाः
संदेशकमूचतुः समुपगम्य ॥ ६०५॥

आदिशति देव देवीत्यर्थोक्ते ते सलज्जमन्योन्यम् अवलोक्य मुखं न हि न हि
विज्ञापयति प्रणम्य विनयेन ॥ ६०६ ॥

मकरध्वजस्य पूजां त्वत्पादसरोजसंनिधौ कर्तुम् । पृथिवीमण्डलमण्डन समीहते मे
मनोवृत्तिः ॥ ६०७॥

प्रियरतिभोगो मदनो दयितवसन्तो जनस्य मनसि वसन् । भावेन भवान् पूज्यो
लोकस्थित्या तु कुसुमशरपाणिः ॥ ६०८ ॥

इति दत्त्वा संदेशं प्रकृतिवयः कालसमुचितं भ्रान्त्वा । ते मदमदनाविष्टे
बभूवतुर्जवनिकान्तरिते ॥ ६०६ ॥

अपनीततिरस्करिणी ततो ऽभवन्नृपसुता समं चेट्या । अविदितरत्नावल्या
पूजोचितवस्तुहस्तयानुगता ॥ ६१०॥

9052 पदैः 2, "परैः DEx 903d देशकम् 2, "देशमधी DEEc 9066 ते सलजम्] N,G.

The Bavod's Counsel

With bold, lofty and graceful steps, They danced a while and then
approached To pass the King the following words, Sent by the Queen,
Pradyota's daughter: (905)

"Your Majesty, the Queen commands..." They stop, embarassed, half-way
through, And turn to catch each other's eye. "No, no, she bows and humbly
says: (906)

"My heart desires to venerate The god of love not far away From where you
plant your lotus-feet, O jewel of the earth's extent! (907)

'Fond of the joys of making love, Breath of my life, Vasinta's friend,
Forever there in subjects' hearts-It's you whom I should venerate With all
my love, and yet (it seems) The world requires we reverence equites The god
who holds the flower-arrows. It be not fond of Rati too? I be not handsome?
Friends with Spring? He too resides in all men hearts (908)

Thus they presented what she'd told them -A message suited to their nature,
Their youthfulness and to the season-Then blundered off behind the curtain,
Aglow with wine and spring. (909)

The curtain removed to show The Queen attended by her maid, -None other
than Ratnavali, Who hadn't yet been recognised-Clutching the wherewithal
for pajd. (910)

344

दामोदरगुमविरचितं कुट्ट‌नीमतम्

अथ दृष्ट्वा सागरिकां प्रमादितां परिजनस्य निन्दित्वा ।
काञ्चनमालामवदन्नृपमहिषी जातसंच्चोभा ॥ ६११॥

प्रेषय कन्यामेनामवरोधं त्वं गृहाण कुसुमादि । यावन्न भवति विषये
वीच्तणयोर्भूमिनाथस्य ॥ ११२ ॥

उपगम्य ततोटी तामवदत्त्वं किमर्थमायाता । मेधाविनीं विमुच्य व्रज तस्मिन्मा
विलम्बस्व ॥ ११३॥

विहिते देव्यादेशे मनसीदं सन्निधाय सा तस्थौ । विहगी सुसङ्गताया हस्ते निहिता
मनोभवसपर्याम् ॥ ३१४ ॥

अवलोकयामि तावत्तिरोहिता सिन्दुवारविटपेन । तातान्तः पुरिकाभिर्यथार्ज्यते किं
तथेह नो वेति ॥ ६१५॥

पिण्डीकृतमिव रागं हृच्छयमिव लब्धविग्रहोत्कर्षम् । समुपेत्य वत्सराजं जगाद सा
जयतु देव इति ॥ ११६ ॥

परिभुक्तमपि नवत्वं शृङ्गारं मदनपर्वणा नीतम् । भजमानो भजमानां
स्वागतवचसाभिनन्द्य तामूचे ॥ ६१७ ॥

The Bawd's Counsel

The Queen then glimpsed Ratnävali (Known as Sagarika at court), Censured
the servants carelessness, And, in some agitation, spoke To Käñcanamälä
thus: (911)

"Send this girl back behind the purdah, And take these flowers and other
things Yourself, so that she doesn't reach The sovereign's field of view."
(91)

That maid stepped forward to address Ratnavali: "Why did you come, Leaving
the mynah Clever Polly! Go back there! Do not dawdle." (gay)

Complying with the Queen's command, She had a sudden thought, and stopped:
"The bird is with Susangati. Why don't I just conceal myself Behind the
Sinduvära tee And watch to ses if hern as well They worship Käma like at
home Among my father's ladies! (914-915)

The Queen approached Udayani, The quiddity of love made flesh. As though a
perfect human frame Had been reclaimed by Kämadeva-And said "Long live the
king!" (926)

While savouring the mood of love, Though often relished, fresh again
Because of Kama's festival, The King bid welcome to the Queen, Wiko
honoured him, and spoke to her: (917)

The queen send off Rarnavali

Ramavall though staye behind To see be Kama worshipped here

शर्वविलोचनपावकदाहाभ्यधिकां मनोभवो मन्ये । प्राप्स्यति तव

अथ मत्मथमभ्यर्च्य चितिनाथं तदनु समधिकं तस्याम् । परमां मुदं वहन्त्यां
विग्रहवन्मदनमनसि कन्यायाम् ॥ ६१६ ॥

साम्प्रतं जातम् ॥ ६२३ ॥

The Bawd's Counsel

obliging him. Oh gosh! My very servitude Has proved to have its purpose
now. (923)

"While noone notices me here, I'll just slip off immediately." So saying,
she tore her eyes away. With difficulty, from the hero, Abandoning the
stage. (924)

दामोदरगुप्मविरचितं तुट्टनीमतम्

कन्दर्पमहमहोत्सवहृतहृदयैर्नावधारितो ऽस्माभिः । संध्यातिक्रमकालः पश्य त्वं
प्रियवयस्यक तथा हि ॥ ६२५॥

उदयतटान्तरितमियं प्राची सूचयति दिङनिशानाथम् । परिपाण्डुना मुखेन प्रियमिव
हृदयस्थितं रमरणी ॥ ६२६॥

देवि त्वन्मुखपद्मः पद्मान् विदधाति पश्य विच्छायान् । अलयो ऽपि लज्जिता इव
शनैः शनैस्तदुदरेषु लीयन्ते ॥ ६२७॥

एवमभिधाय चिवैश्वरणन्यासैः परिक्रमं कृत्वा । नैष्क्रामिक्या ध्रुवया
विनिर्ययौ नायको ऽपि सह सर्वैः ॥ ६२८ ॥

ठक्कुरो दिवसैः ॥ ६३१॥

The Bated's Cowwel

"My friend, I hadn't even seen, So captivated have I been By revels for the
love-god's feast That sunset had already past: Just look, for over there:

The king observer the fall of night.

"The orient now shows the moon Concealed behind the Sunrise Range -Just so
some beauty gives away The lover hidden in her heart By colour draining
from her face. (ga6)

"My queen, the lotus of your face Reduces other blooms to shadows. Even the

The act was ended, sounds of songs And instruments faded away. Ar which the
prince began to judge The merits of the show. (929)

"People like me, preoccupied With tasks concerning vehicles And rituals and
infantry And villages, etcetera, Are generally not the sost To penetrate
the subtleties Of plays produced on stage. (910)

"There is a village written here. It's well-appointed, rich in land. Take

yourself a Thakur. (931)

दामोदरसुसविरचितं बुद्धनीमतम्

कृतजीवनसंस्थो ऽपि त्वमपि किमर्थं करोषि विज्ञप्तिम् । अर्पय वा यदि नेच्छसि
कुरु स्थिति हस्तदानेन ॥ ६३२ ॥

न च पत्तत्यो न सप्तिर्न च पोष्यजनस्तथाप्यसंतुष्टः । लभमानो ऽपि स दायं
चिरन्तनत्वाभिमानेन ॥ ६३३॥

विज्ञप्तिकोन्मुखत्वं दूरत एवावधारितं भवतः । तूष्णीं क्रियतामस्माच्छ्रोष्यसि
कार्यं प्रतीहारात् ॥ ६३४ ॥

यूर्य कुटुम्बमध्ये क्व गम्यते गोवपुवसामान्यम् । आदाय संविभागं स्वगृह इव
स्थीयतां यथासौख्यम् ॥ ६३५॥

अभ्यन्तरव्ययार्थं न विलब्धो यो मया महोद्रङ्गः। तवापि ते ऽनुबन्धो नो जाने कि
करोमीति ॥ ६३६ ॥

प्रथमतरमेव कल्पितमनल्पफलजीवनं प्रदेशस्थम् । अद्यापि ते न जातं नियोगिनां
पश्य मन्थरताम् ॥ ६३७॥

एवंप्रायैरनुदिनलाभोदयमोहकारिभिर्वचनैः । फलशून्यैरनुजीवी प्रतारितः कः
कियत्कालम् ॥ ६३८ ॥

'Although you've made your livelihood, Why do you too apply to me! If you

your home." (935)

household matters; do you want To claim a part of that as well? I don't
know what I ought to do." (436)

And still today it's not transferred! Just look at how extremely slow Are
bureaucratic factors!" (932)

"With suchlike empty words that thwart His hopes of gain each day, what
man, Dependent for his livelihood, Will be deceived! How long! (938)

The Batod's Counsel

"In things like those I have some skill; But here, with what I understand
Of role-play (and about the world). Because I am a chatterbox, I'll tell
you something of my views The kind of thing that you'd expect To hear from
dull spectators. (939)

"This mise en scène has seven (notes), In seven resides this band with life
-The seven derivates of food Sisfold (in properties of song): It's
sixfold-for it is encaind In sheaths each parent has bequeathed It's given
shape (by song and dance); A baman body groen it form It's fixed by three
authorities (Tradition, introspection, life) Three means of knowing govern
is (Scripture, perception, inference) It's ringing with dramatic truth And
therefore quite superlative;" Superior when its full of sattva (The highest
of the Sankly gu Achieved with three constituents," Now used alone, now
intermised, A blend of all three strands of life, It's execution has two
modes: Sweet tenderness and forcefulness, Men's acts are kind or violent;
Adorned with melismatic notes, Stained by the pleasures of this world
Performed in four theatric modes (The Lingual, Stately, Gracious, Fierce),
Sustained by various livelihoods; Produced with feelings of three types The
prized, the loathed, the neutral. )940-تهو

The Baud's Counsel

"Deep and mellifluous was the sound The instrumentalist produced. Adorned
with many ornaments. And yet with melody kept clear: Nor did the tempo
break apart As he displayed variety. (942)

"Impeccable in every tongue, The recitation gave delight And never
faltered: words gave sense With clarity, aided by mood And intonation;
sounds emerged From where they should, without a slip (No dental sounding
retroflex). (945)

"The singers sang with even skill, With tasteful tone, and full control Of
ornaments and agréments, Changing their pace, now fast, now slow And now
again andante. (944)

The wardrobe of costumery -Used to express the states of mind That fit the
natures of each part-Has brought this mise en scène beyond That which
contains the two ideals (Of poetry) in perfect form (Namely the play
itself). (945)

The Bawd's Counsel

"The harem ladies of the King Called Naghusa were taught by sons Of
Bharata, and even they Did not, it seems to me, attain Such excellence in
drama. (946)

"This gem, the play Ratnanali, Shines bright, examined by an expert, Set in
the gold of skilled artistes, Its junctures fitting well together." (947)

The prince, enthused, was deep engaged Expounding suchlike merits, when, By
chance, some person sang a verse That came just then to mind: (948)

"Standing one's ground on battle-fields, And keeping up-to-date with plays,
Delighting in a well-turned verne, Devotion to the chase-it's these That
are the perquisites of princes." (949)

When this new thought came to his ears, The prince, in transports, turned
to speak About the hunt, thus cutting off The talk he'd been engaged in.
(950)

"By often following the chase One learns to hit a moving mark, One grasps
just how the land can lie And how to keep a steady seat Upon a horse that's
cantering. (951)

The prince' thoughts turn to the hunt.

359

The Bawd's Counsel

"The horse is galloping along, The lucky hunter holds his feet Firm in the
stirrups, strains his chest Forward, and scans the ground ahead For bumps
and hollows then he spots The creature dashing fast away For all its
breath, beside itself With terror of the hounds, and quick He moves to
shoot his arrow. (952-933)

"What bliss ineffable is his When beaters coas a prey in range Then startle
it to leap in view Before him where he lies concealed By roots of trees,
and lo! he fells it. (954)

"How much we envy those who catch A deer they've made to settle down With
ears pricked up to hear them sing. Stock-still, its mouth with tufts of
grass Suspended there immobile. (955)

"Great is the man who checks a boar That comes his way with just one blow,
Emerging from some jungle brake To flee a forest fire's heat. (956)

"Only a tracker of some note Approaches cautiously a hare Asleep amidst the
undergrowth Without his footsteps making noise And easily despatches it."
(957)

While Simhabhata's son spoke thus In praise of hunters feats of skill, Some
other person sang a verse That chanced to spring to mind. (958)

"Leaving aside the joy one feels When in the chase, the very act Of talking
of the hunt impedes Eating and such at proper times For those whose minds
are so inclined." (959)

Samarahhața understood The sense the song conveyed, informed His factor of
the benefice And, as he fondly cast a glance At Manjari, stood up to go.
(960)

He then went home to have his food And carry out his other tasks. His mind
immersed in Manjari, He mused aloud before his friend: (961)

"How come the god with flowery bow, Though one, can take his place at once
In these her fucling brow, her smile, Her speech that's soft, yet witty
tooυ, Her gaze, het gestures and her gait! (962)

"Tilottama (the apsaras) Was made by Brahma to destroy Súnda and Opasünda
(fiends): What good did he intend for gods When he created Manjari, Who'll
be the death of mortalsf (961)

After the play

at home.

now hack The prince mitts

on Manjari

The Bawd's Counsel

She bowed before him, serting down A little dish of paan and flowers, Then,
when the moment came, explained The business of her friend. (990)

"The foolish girl desires to dangle The bloom that sprouts from Visnu's
navel As if it were a pendant carring. Down from the sky she wants to
snatch The orb that is the Lord of Stars. (991)

"Insane, she craves ambrosia, Food of the heaven-dwelling gods; She wants
to lay herself upon A bed of sandal and fresh sprouts, But one that's hot
to touch. (993)

"She lives in hope of pinning up Her chignon with the flowers that grow On
heaven's Parijata trees: Misguidedly she wants to prise The jewel from
Visna's chest. (993)

"In other words, how must we seem, Base and of lowly birth, caressed By any
man who wants our souch, When juxtaposed with such as you. Intelligent and
Indra-like And graced with all good qualities! (994)

"But that's the way of wretched Käma, Mean-natured god, who fixes hearts,
Not caring if things fit or don't, On quite unsuited objects. (991)

"Before, she'd mock the lotus plant "It's juicy, and it blushes red
Spontaneously, is what she'd think She'd fault the workings of the minds Of
meditators, always fixed On just 'One Man' alone. (996)

The Bated's Counsel

375

"She wouldn't in a hundred births Think highly of a stream of ghee: "It's
sirupy!" She'd never praise Pursuit of five-dice gambling games, Linked to
the thrills that lead to rain... (Since that might also mean instead:)
Involving impecunisut lovers. (997)

"She doesn't prize the sandal plant For being wrapped around with snakes...
(Because those words might also mean:) For being ringed by paramours, 'It's
sappy', she would think. The fish That guzzled Kämadeva down (When he took
form as Krsna's child) The fish that woons away for love... She wouldn't
hear of being praised, Not even in her dreams. (998)

"Of all organs she loathes her tongue: "It seems inflamed", she'd say, "by
paan". The equanimity of those Who seek to reach beyond this world She
would admire because they treat A hate, a bull, a horse, a man The zaurine
man, the leporine, The man endowed just like a home (The trinity of sexual
types)-Without a jot of difference. (999)

"Rambha she never much admired, Who, mad with love, set out to meet (By
night) with Nálakübará, And she detested Urvasi, The empyrean courtesan
Besotted with Purüravás. (1000)

पापकुसुमास्त्रः ॥ १००५॥

The Bawd's Counsel

377

"Of other men she takes the hearts But never lets her own be seized;
Passion she stirs in others, yet She's never felt the thing herself.
Ensnaring men with various wiles She never lets herself be trapped By oh so
many favours. (1001)

"Love, it appeared, made up her being: She knew it, though, by name alone.
And when engaged in making love, Although her hairs stood up on end, The
pleasure that the act can give She only knew from hearsay. (1003)

"Her blandishments of divers kinds Were works of art, but not of feeling.
In innocence of lovesickness She stirs the pangs of longing. (1001)

"Though just a girl, void of all sense Of rectitude, she glisters bright
When any powerful man is near, Just like the crescent lunar sliver When
seuly risen shines on Siva, Not straight, but curved, her conduct seems
Like that of Ravana, who stole Kubera's majesty: she steals The dignity of
wealthy meся. (1004)

"Your Majesty, what can I say? Though torched by fire from Siva's eye, That
wretched flower-weaponed Love Will not give up his stubborn will, However
difficult his goal, To get at what he's set his sights on. (1003)

त्वां महापुरुषम् ॥ १०११॥

दामोदरपुतिभिरमितं भीमतम्

गतमेवमेवमासितमालोकितमेवमेवमालपितम् । इति विस्मृतान्यकार्या स्मरति कृशाङ्गी
त्वदीयलीलानाम् ॥ १०१२ ॥

नलकूबरी बराको रतिरमणी रमण एव किं तेन । अनिरुद्धो ऽपि न बुद्धो
विदग्धविहितासु सुरतगोष्ठीषु ॥ १०१३॥

न जयन्तो ऽनन्तगुरणो न कुमारः मारकर्मणो ऽबाह्यः । केन समतां नयामस्तमिति सखी
वहति मानसं क्लेशम् ॥ १०१४ ॥

आगतमागच्छन्तं पुरतः पार्श्वे प्रसन्नमथ कुपितम् । पश्यति भवन्तमेकं
संकल्पनिवेशितं बाला ॥ १०१५॥

रुच्यः कान्तो हृद्यः सुभगः सुखदो मनोहरो रमणः । इष्टः स्वामी दयितः प्राणेशः
केलिकरणनिपुरण इति ॥ १०१६ ॥

मुक्तान्यसमारम्भा बरतनुरनुपप्लुतेन चित्तेन । जपति समीहितसिद्ध्यै
त्वद्वादशनामकं महास्तोत्रम् ॥ १०१७ ॥

तामेव गच्छ यस्यामासज्य विलम्बितो ऽसि गतलज्ज । वेलामियतीमलमलमेतैरधुना

मनोरथावृत्तिम् ॥ १०१६॥

उत्सहते न द्रष्टुं प्रतिबिम्बितमाननं कुतः शशिनम् । का संकथा मृरणाले क्षिपति

पल्लवेष्विति विरुद्धम् ॥ १०२१ ॥

एतदायातम् ॥ १०२२ ॥

यस्याः कामः कृपरणो रागाकृष्टिस्तृणोलपप्रख्या । सापि गता भूमिमिमां जीवन्त्या

तद्विदामेव ॥ १०२४॥

व्यथयन्नपि सच्छायः परिजनचिन्ताकरो ऽपि रमणीयः । आधत्ते त्वयि

एकः स एव जातो भुवने ऽस्मिन्नसमसायकस्पर्धी । तेन शशिबिम्बफलके सुजन्मना
लेखितं निजं नाम ॥ १०२६ ॥

पादस्तेन सलीलं विन्यस्तः सुभगमानिनां मूर्ध्नि। सौभाग्ययशः कुसुमं धनपतिसूनोः
कदर्थितं तेन ॥ १०२७ ॥

सुभगेन ॥ १०२८ ॥

कृच्छ्रसाध्यरोगे ऽपि ॥ १०२६ ॥

चिराद्विभावितं क्लिष्टहुंकारम् ॥ १०३० ॥

386

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं तुट्टनीमतम्

का पुरुषार्थसमीहा द्योतयतः शर्वरी शशाङ्कस्य । तर्पयतां भुवमखिलां सलिलमुचां
को ऽभिकाङ्गितो लाभः ॥ १०३१ ॥

मराडयितुं वियदुदयति पुरुहूतधनुविनैव फलवाञ्छाम् । अनपेक्षितात्मकार्यः
परहितकरणग्रहः सतां सहजः ॥ १०३२ ॥

प्रायेण यन्निदानं तदसेवनमुपशमाय रोगाणाम् । स्मरमान्दयं तु यदुत्थं तदेव खलु
भेषजं यतस्तस्य ॥ १०३३॥

तेन स्पृहयति सुतनुस्त्वत्पादयुगाब्जरेणुसंगतये । आशीविषयोपेते सम्भोगसुखोदये
तु नाकाला ॥ १०३४॥

प्रमदमुपैति मयूरी परमं शब्देन वारिवाहस्य । अनिमिषविलोकितेन प्राप्नोति झषी
कृतार्थतामेव ॥ १०३५॥

न वृथास्तुतिमुखरतया न च युष्मल्लोभनाभियोगेन । विदधामि तद्गुणाख्यां
स्वरूपमावप्रसङ्गेन ॥ १०३६ ॥

103ad काङ्गिती। 2. काङ्किते D so3gb तदसेवन N₂NG, तत्वन N.Dick उपशमाय। 2.

The Band's Coun

"What does the moon desire to reach In terms of human goals when he
Illuminates the sky at night! What gain do clouds attempt to win When
irrigating all the earth (101)

"To ornament the firmament The rainbow soars without the sense It must get
something in renam. The urge to act for others' sakes Rises unbidden in the
good, Without regard to their own needs. (sega)

"In general, avuiding things That generate a malady Suffices to effect a
cure But in the of Love's Disease, We know the cause itself to be Its
remedy as well. (1013)

"And that is why the lovely girl Desires to touch the very dust Adhering to
your lotus-fest, But doesn't even dare to hope To gain thar bliss what
benison!-Of love-making with you. (1054)

"The peahen climbs to pleasure's heights Hearing the rumbling of the
clouds, The female fish reaps every wish By garing with unblinking eyes.
(1015)

"Her virtues I'll declaim, but not

In pointless prolix eulogy. Nor to insist on luring you, But just because
it's pertinent To state things as they are. (3036)

Eler go-between then pererates

दामोदरगुप्तविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

सद्भावबद्धमूले स्मितदृष्टिभ्रूविकारपल्लविते । सेवन्ते हृद्यरसां रागतरी
मञ्जरीं धन्याः ॥ १०३७॥

तिष्ठतु तदङ्गसङ्गो विलोकिता येन झगिति वरगावी । तस्यान्यो युवतिजनः प्रतिभाति
मनुष्यरूपेण ॥ १०३८ ॥

सकृदपि यैरनुभूतस्तत्तनुपरिरम्भसुखरसास्वादः । विद्धि नराधिप तेषां दूरीभूतं
प्रजाकार्यम् ॥ १०३६॥

आस्था का खलु तस्या विषयग्रहदुर्बलेषु पुरुषेषु । यस्या विलासजालकपतितः
शकुनायते कपिलः ॥ १०४० ॥

दग्ध्वापि पुनर्दग्धो नूनमनङ्गः हरेण तां तन्वीम् । दृष्ट्वापि येन तिष्ठसि
निराकुलः स्वस्थवृत्तेन ॥ १०४१॥

अथ विरतोक्तौ तस्यामुल्लासितमानसे च नृपसूनौ । कश्विदगायगीति
स्मृतिसंगतिमागतां प्रसङ्गेन ॥ १०४२ ॥

10326 विकार 2. विलास DE, 1037d तरी NEC, तरोर् DE (G) वरी धन्याः 2. री पन्या

The Bawd's Counsel

"Happy the men who can enjoy The sweetly juicy blossom-sprig, This Manjari,
on passion's tree Rooted in true affection's soil, Which has, to serve for
shoots, her smiles, Her glances and her eyebrows' twists. (1037)

"Let's leave aside her body's touch, For even if one just sets eyes An
instant on that shapely girl, All other women after that Will rather seem
like men. (1038)

"When someone's once experienced The relish of the thrill produced By
folding in embrace her body, That man, o prince, sets far aside His duties
to his citizens..... The våle of siring children. (1019)

"What should she care for men who're weak Because of sensual pleasures'
hold If even reddish (pigs?) might fly If even Kapila (the sage) Might
flutter like a little bird Caught in her nets of coquetty! (1040)

"Love, though Siva's burnt him once, Is surely scorched again to see That
you can stand there healthy still, Your wits about you, even though You've
glimpsed that slender girl." (1041)

555

Just as she rounded off her speech, The prince's heart being galvanised,
Some person sang out loud a verse That chanced to come to mind. (1042)

390

दामोदरगुमविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

अन्योन्यगाढरागप्रबलीकृतचित्तजन्मनोर्युनोः । कालात्ययो मनागपि
समागमानन्दविघ्नकरः ॥ १०४३ ॥

श्रुत्वा सिंहभटसुतः प्रियाप्रियां प्रीतिमान् स्मितप्रथमम् । निजगाद
चारुभाषिणि गीतिकया समयसम्मतं कथितम् ॥ १०४४ ॥

अभिनन्द्य सा तथेति प्रययौ प्रमदावती निजं भवनम् । अकरोच्च विदितकार्या युक्ते
ऽवसरे मनोरमां गणिकाम् ॥ १०४५ ॥

अथ सा कृतसंकल्पा सत्वरमादाय रुचिरविच्छित्तिम् । आसाद्य नृपनिशान्तं विवेश
संचारिकासहिता ॥ १०४६ ॥

विहितनमस्कृतिरासनमधितष्ठौ नायकेन निर्दिष्टम् । पृष्टे च देहकुशले
विनयान्वितमभ्यधाद्दती ॥ १०४७॥

श्रीमन्नद्य श्रेयःसम्पन्ना गुरुजनाशिषो ऽशेषाः । अद्य मदनः प्रसन्नो
भाग्यचयैरद्य परिणतं फलतः ॥ १०४८ ॥

अद्य जननी प्रसूता सौभाग्यगुणोदयो ऽद्य निष्णातः । त्वयि वितरति सस्नेहं
निरामयप्रश्वभारतीं तस्याः ॥ १०४६ ॥

The Batod's Counsel

"When two young persons yearn with love That mutual passion amplifies, A
lapse of time, however slight, Diminishes the bliss of union." (1041)

On hearing this, the flattered prince Addressed his loved one's confidante
Giving her first a smile: "This verse, O sweetly spoken girl, expressed A
meaning that is opportune." (1044)

This Pramadavati (her aide) Assented, then, taking her leave, She set off
home, there to acquaint, When she found time, fair Mañjarī, The odalisque,
of what transpired. (1043)

555

She then made up her mind to act Quickly she donned sweet bibelots,
Approached the prince's residence And with her confidante went in. (1046)

(Joining her hands) she made a bow And took the seat the hero showed. When
she'd been asked about her health, The go-between respectfully Began to
speak as follows: (1047)

"Your Highness, now has every wish Our elders ever blessed us with Been
smiled upon by providence! Today is Käma gratified! Today what merit we had
stored Has been transformed to fruit. (1048)

"Today her mother's given birth! Today her fortune's star has risen! Now
that you kindly formulate Questions about her health. (1049)

Same person gang by chance.

The flattered prince agrees to meet

Manjari call on Samará

The go-between, with honryed Unites the Jerm, then withdraw

392

दामोदरमुभविरचितं मुट्टनीमतम्

उत्कलिकाकुलमनसामुद्रिक्तरिरंसयाभिभूतानाम् । औदासीन्यं भजतां समागता भवति
नालिका यूनाम् ॥ १०५० ॥

धृतसुमनःशरधनुषा सहायवांस्तिष्ठ दयितया सार्धम् । यामो वयं न राजति
विजनस्थितमिथुनसंनिधावपरः ॥ १०५१॥

एषा नृत्यबान्ता मदनेनावासितातिसुकुमारा । त्वमपि रतिसमरशूरः स्वर्गभुवः सन्तु
कुशलाय ॥ १०५२॥

यावद्यावदशक्ति प्रथयति ललना हि मोहनाक्रान्ता । तावत्तावत्युंसामुत्साहः
पल्लवान् समुत्सृजति ॥ १०५३॥

इति शून्यीकृतवेश्मनि हरति शनैः सहजमंशुकं तस्मिन् । दर्शितसाध्वसलज्जा जगाद
सा कि करोषीति ॥ १०५४ ॥

अयि मुग्धे तत्क्रियते पुरुषार्थचतुष्टयस्य यत्सारम् । इति निगदितसस्मेरः
स्मरविधुरित आततान रतिकलहम् ॥ १०५५ ॥

The Band Counsel

"When youthful persons yearn so much Their wits are addled, overcome With
patent appetite for love, And yet they must remain aloof, Another girl's a
gooseberry. (1050)

"Stay there in company with Him Who holds the flowery bow and arrows, And
with your lady-love! T'II leaves A surplus person brings no charm Where, in
sequestered privacy, A loving pair unites. (1051)

"She's tired herself with dancing now, Extremely delicate, fatigued By
love, while you're a hero wied Upon the battlefields of last. Ye gods, be
favourable! (1054)

"For it's well known: with every show Of woman's weakness when assailed By
amatory wooziness, Man's boldness puts forth shoots." (1013)

When thus the house was all their own, He started gradually to lif Her veil
of natural reserve, While she, displaying fear and shame, Cried out: "What
are you doing!" (1054)

"O simple girl! What I'm about Is striving for the very core Of all four
goals of human life." Smiling at what he'd said, deranged By Kama, he then
launched himself Upon the battleground of ses. (1055)

दामोदरगुप्सविरचितं कुट्टनीमतम्

नानासुरतवियेपैराराध्य चकार मुक्तसर्वस्वम् । गणिकानौ राजसुतं त्वगस्थिशेषं
मुमोच नातिचिरात् ॥ १०५६ ॥

तद्यन्मयोपदिष्टं कामिजनार्थातिकारां तेन । महतीं समृद्धिमेष्यसि
कामुकलोकाहतेन वित्तेन ॥ १०५७ ॥

इत्युपदेशधवरणप्रबोधतुष्टा जगाम धाम स्वम् । मालत्यपगतमीहा विकरालापादवन्दनां
कृत्वा ॥ १०५८ ॥

काव्यमिदं यः शृणुते सम्यक्काव्यार्थपालनेनासौ । नो वच्यते कदा
चिह्निटवेश्याधूर्तकुट्टनीभिरिति ॥ १०५८ ॥

1056 Verses 1056-1059 are found only in N,DECE. 1059 N, explicit:
kastanimatam kävyam samaptam, varmalatadvayam jäte novatyám sadviziyake |
pärnamärjäi ca phalgunyam likh tam dharmarthakamati ||

395

The Band's Counel

That prostitute treated the prince With various modes of making love.
Consumed entire his worldly wealth, And not long after let him go, Nothing
but skin and hones. (1016)

So using these techniques I've taught Of getting wealth from pramoun You'll
thrive in great prosperity With riches taken from your lovers. (10)

Pleased with the insights she'd received From listening to this hamily,
Delusion lifted, Malati, Revering Vikarälä's feen, Set of to go hack home.
(105)

Whoever hears this poem through And heeds its moral carefully Will never
find himself deceived By zakes and trulls and rogues and bawds. (9)
